# ROM data

All offsets are for Mega Man Battle Network 6: Cybeast Gregar (USA), SHA-1
`89fe0bac4fd3d2ab1d2ca35e87ef8b1294a84cd6`, and live in `RomLayout` in
`src/core/rom.c`. The [bn6f disassembly](https://github.com/dism-exe/bn6f)
targets Cybeast Falzar; most code-region tables sit at the same addresses in
Gregar, and the rest were found by searching the Gregar ROM for the
structures the disassembly describes. `tools/romlab` runs the ROM in libmgba
to confirm findings against real frames and VRAM.

| Data | Offset | How it was found / verified |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Sprite pointer lists | `0x031CC4` | Ten pointers, the first pointing just past the list (`SpritePointersList`). Every category renders correctly. |
| Chip records (0x2C bytes) | `0x021DA8` | Same address as Falzar's `ChipDataArr`. Cannon = 40 power, codes A B C *. Codes at 0, rarity 0-4 at 5, library type at 7 (0 standard, 1 Mega, 2 Giga, 3 secret, 4 Program Advance), attack family at 0xB (27 the Navi chips, 81 the Dragons, 73 MchnSwrd's line, 21 placed objects and traps, 36 TankCan1-3: chips 12-14, power 120, 160 and 200, which fire after a wind-up), lock-on setting at 0xF (1 for chips that strike an enemy outright, Cannon, the swords, bombs; 0 for traps, counters, MchnSwrd's paralysed-only cut, VarSwrd's key inputs, GolmHit's and TimeBom's delays, and the Navi chips and Dragons; checked against those chips), library number at 0x15, library flags at 0x16 (bit 0: the other version's Navi chips and Otenko; 0x30: the arm chips and the five DarkChips the US version cut, whose 0x20 keeps a chip out of the pack's list (BN6's own DarkChips, below); 0x10 alone: the Beast chips), and two picture pointers at 0x24 and 0x28, which the chips cut from the US version share as one blank (`0x086F3AF0`, the same word in both). A run can find or buy 249 of them (`src/core/chip_pool.c`): standard 1-202, the Megas 221-274 but Falzar's Navis, the ten Gigas 301-310. HackJack, HackJck EX and HackJck SP (275-277, Megas of a Navi the Japanese version has) pass every rule but the blank picture: taken from an official vault, one used in battle made MegaMan vanish (his Navi's sprite is a white dot in the US version, sprite category 0x3F) until he was deleted (issue #15). Verify by listing the records whose words at 0x24 and 0x28 are equal: 34, all left out by the other rules but 275-277. Records 203-220 are blank, 315 on pseudo-chips. |
| Chip names | `0x6E88D0`, `0x6E92D8` | Text archives found by encoding "Cannon" with the game's character table; pointers in code reference both. The core's copy reads them rebuilt for ids to 511 (The chip registry, below). |
| Chip descriptions | `0x6E983C`, `0x6EC050` | The two text archives after the names', as the bn6f disassembly lays them (`text_archives_86ea94c`: names 0 and 1, then `TextScriptChipDescriptions0` and `TextScriptChipDesc1`); the code holds their pointers beside the names' (`0x027D50` and `0x027D54` after `0x027D2C` and `0x027D30`), and as a pair at `0x11A9BC` and `0x11B3BC`. A script opens the box (E8 06 1 1), sets the text speed (F1 0 0), and holds three short lines broken by E9 up to a key wait (E7). Verify: script 1 of the first reads "Cannon to / attack / 1 enemy", script 0 of the second "Attk ahd. / Suck in & / shoot out" as in the disassembly. |
| Enemy id table | `0x0182C4` | `GetVerActorTyAndAIIdx`: (version, actor type, AI index) triples. |
| Font | `0x6B5A2C` | 8x16 glyphs indexed by character code (the battle font); the run summary uses it. |
| Title screen | `RomLayout.title`; load table at `0x02FCD8` | The game's own load list (source, VRAM or palette-buffer destination, size) gives the LZ77 blocks: the 256-colour picture's tiles `0x7F3040` (to 0x06000000), PRESS START / NEW GAME / CONTINUE `0x7F1EBC` (OBJ tile 1 on) with palettes `0x7F216C` and `0x7F218C`, and the copyright line `0x7F21EC` (8 OBJs of 32x32, palette `0x7F2C20`). The picture's 32x20 map sits at `0x7F7CFC`, its palette at `0x7F2E40` (banks 0-13). The picture is one layer, told apart by palette bank: banks 0-4 draw the logo, 5-6 the blue behind it and 7-13 the Cybeast (the six palette scripts at `0x02F5F0` pulse banks 7-10 and 13-14, the Cybeast only). The logo's pixels split into the emblem, MEGAMAN and BATTLE NETWORK (rows up to 95), the CYBEAST GREGAR plate (rows 97-112, not connected) and the 6, which touches the N and the K; the engine cuts the 6 along its black outline (x 177 on row 44 to 162 on row 81, 175 from BATTLE NETWORK down) and its dark rim (indices 0x01-0x02, 0x16-0x1C). Check by drawing indices 1-0x4F of the picture with that cut: no 6 pixels remain and the N keeps its outline. The menu arrow is 3 frames at `0x6A280C` (palette `0x6A344C`). Positions and timing came from recorded OAM and palette RAM: PRESS START blinks 32/32 and START plays sound 0x67; NEW GAME plays 0x9D, CONTINUE 0x9C, and the music stops. |
| Title marks (`scene_title.c`, `meta.h`) | OAM lists `0x02FB7C`-`0x02FC88`; tiles in `0x7F21EC`, palettes `0x7F2C20` | BN6 draws a mark for each bit of the word `GetTitleScreenIconCount` returns (bn6f: 0x80 the ending's event 0xE00, 0x40-0x04 counts of the Library and the P.A. memo, 0x02 and 0x100 the events 0x370 and 0x340), each by a routine (`sub_802FB64` for 0x80 to `sub_802FC70` for 0x100) that hands an OAM list to the game's sprite writer. An entry is three GBA OAM attributes: the 0x80 mark's `02 40 04 80 80 92` is y 2, x 4, 32x16, tile 0x280, palette 9, and a 16x8 part follows at y 18. The tiles are the copyright line's block (OBJ tile 0x180 on), the palettes `0x7F2C20`'s 0x220 bytes from OBJ palette 5: the marks use 7-9, and 10, for a mark of 0x200, is empty in Gregar. Check with `build.py shot --scene title --marks 1FE`: each mark whole, in its colours, where BN6 puts it. |
| Boot and warps (`src/emu/boot.c`) | `0x02009CD0` eChatbox, `0x02009F38` its flags, `0x02011BB0` Warp2011bb0, `0x02011C50` CutsceneState, `0x08005C05` map_triggerEnterMapOnWarp, `0x080050EC` cbGameState | The engine's warp copies a warp record into Warp2011bb0 and calls the game's own warp routine as a game call: a hook at cbGameState (run every frame of the game mode, from the main loop's `mov lr,pc; bx r0`, bn6f `main.s`) jumps to the routine in place of that frame's update, and a hook where it returns (`EMU_FREE` + 0x100) goes back to the main loop, r4-r11 as they were and lr's Thumb bit set (issue #33; until then a stub over cbGameState's first 8 bytes, there for one frame); the overworld routine at `0x08005A8C` borrowed before that does not run on every map frame. The update a call takes is the frame's sprites too: bn6f's main loop (`main.s`) clears the sprites' buffer (sprite_resetObjVars) before the state update and copies it out after, so a frame whose update a call took shows no sprite at all (issue #187: an emote's start, every MegaMan, the HP box and the area's name gone for a frame in the Square's captures). `game_call_then_update` goes on into the update once its routine returns: cbGameState reads no argument register (its table from its own pool, the state from r10's toolkit) and returns by `pop {r4-r7,pc}`, which keeps Thumb, so the return hook jumps to it with the main loop's lr: Gregar's bytes at `0x080050EC` are `F0 B5 06 48 55 46 ED 6B 29 78 40 58 FE 46 00 47 FC F7 0E FA FC F7 24 FA F0 BD` (push {r4-r7,lr} ... mov lr,pc; bx r0; two bl; pop {r4-r7,pc}), as bn6f's cbGameState_80050EC. After NEW GAME it warps to Seaside Area 1 and ends the intro by clearing the cutscene script and closing the chat box as the chat script's end command (E6) does. Found in the bn6f disassembly and tested in `tools/romlab`. |
| MegaMan held on the map (`src/director/director_hold.c`, issue #23) | `0x0200A440` eScreenFade (`BN6_SCREEN_FADE`, +3 `BN6_FADE_ACTIVE`), `0x0200ACF5` eStruct200ace0 +0x15 (`BN6_SCRIPTED_WALK`), eGameState +0x0A BattlePaused (`BN6_MAP_PAUSED`) and +0x10 (`BN6_NPC_CHAT`), eFlags2009F38 bit `0x80` (`BN6_CHAT_ACTIVE`), the player object +0x08 (`BN6_PLAYER_MAIN`) and +0x4C (`BN6_PLAYER_PAD`, `BN6_PAD_STEP` `0x30`), flags `0x1707` (`BN6_FLAG_PET_OFF`) and `0x1717` (`BN6_FLAG_CONVEYOR`), sub-mode `0x00` (`BN6_SUB_ENTER`) | What BN6 tests before it takes the pad (bn6f sub_809D9E0: the scripted walk, a fade, EVENT_PLAYER_CAN_MOVE, the conveyor flag, a cutscene) and START (sub_8005AF4: the sub-mode, the conveyor flag, the NPC chat, a warp, the PET bit, a fade, a cutscene, the chat flag, EVENT_PET_DISABLED), read in the bn6f disassembly; the RAM layout is Falzar's. EnterMap (the CONTINUE's warp) clears the cutscene, the warp, EVENT_PET_DISABLED and respawns the player object, and waits for the fade; the scripted walk, the conveyor flag, the chat flag and BattlePaused survive it. Verified in `tools/play.py` with the dev state (`held ...`): each set on the map holds MegaMan as described (the fade's mark and the conveyor flag hold the pad and START with L, SELECT's map and the dev menu still working, as issue #23 reports; the scripted walk the pad alone, the chat flag START alone, BattlePaused every object), and `stuck NAME` saves a run so for its CONTINUE: the fade's mark kept EnterMap waiting, a black screen, the rest held MegaMan on the map, until the watch let them go. +0x4C is 0x10 or 0x20 the frame BN6 takes a walk or a run, against a wall too. |
| The game's structures read field by field (`src/emu/bn6.h`, issue #34) | `0x02009F40` OverworldPlayerObject, `0x020057B0` eOverworldNPCObjects (16 of 0xD8: `BN6_NPC_OBJECTS`, its radius `0x0C`, centre offsets `0x11`/`0x12`, position `0x26`/`0x2A`/`0x2E` `BN6_NPC_Z16`), `0x0203A9B0` the T1 BattleObjects, `0x02001B80` eGameState, `0x020047CC` eNaviStats0 | Each field is named after bn6f's `include/structs/*.inc` (OverworldPlayerObject, OverworldNPCObject, BattleObject, GameState, Chatbox, NaviStats, Warp2011bb0, CutsceneState), whose RAM layout Gregar shares with Falzar: only the ROM's code moved. `bn6.h` holds every offset into them; `src/emu/bn6_fields.h` reads MegaMan's 16.16 position in whole units. Verified by `CYBERWORLD_STATE_POS=1`, whose state prints MegaMan's position and every NPC's (`npc N ... at x y z`) where they stand on the map, and by an autopilot run whose log is the same, line for line, as before the names. |
| Encounter roll (`src/director/encounter.c`, issue #29) | `0x0ABD30`; hooks at `0x08005A98` and `0x08005AE2`, jumping to `0x08005AE5` | The roll returns the `BattleSettings` record (bn6f `battle_settings_struct`, 16 bytes: field, music, type, background, side modifier, options, entity list pointer) for a random battle, and leaves its flags for its one caller, bn6f `checkThenStartBattle` (`0x08005A8C`, at the same address in Gregar), which tests MegaMan on the map (`cmp r0,#4`), then the event flags, fades and chat that hold a battle, calls the roll at `0x08005ADE` and branches on its flags (`beq` at `0x08005AE2`) before `movs r1,#1; bl StartBattle` at `0x08005AE4`. Found by disassembling Gregar there and matching bn6f's instructions. A hook on that `beq` swaps a non-NULL result for the engine's record in the free ROM space, so the game's own step counter and chance still decide when a battle happens; one at `0x08005A98`, the first test past the map's, jumps straight to `0x08005AE5` with the record while a battle is forced. Until issue #82's last round, on a layer whose battles were another game's (docs/MULTIROM.md, Guest battles) the `beq` hook queued the guest's battle and jumped to the check's `pop {r5,pc}` at `0x08005AF2` (`BN6_ENCOUNTER_SKIP`, `0x08005AF3` for Thumb), so no BN6 battle began and the step counter the roll reset counted again; every battle is BN6's since, and nothing jumps there. Entity entries are kind (0x00 MegaMan, 0x11 enemy), panel (row << 4 \| column, from 1) and the enemy id, ending with 0xF0. Verify with an autopilot run: its random and guardian battles, and its log, as before. |
| Battle events by hook (`src/director/encounter.c`, issue #30) | `0x08005BC8` StartBattle, `0x080AC180` the reward pick, `0x0800E2D8` object_subtractHP, `0x08007740` in the enemy spawn | Hooks in place of reading the battle every frame (docs/EMULATION.md, Hooks). StartBattle (bn6f, at the same address in Gregar, `BN6_START_BATTLE`) takes the battle's `BattleSettings` in r0, stores it at the game state's `+0x1C` and sets the battle mode; checkThenStartBattle (row above) calls it for every battle the engine starts. An event hook at its first instruction names the record a battle starts from; a story or test battle's is neither of the engine's two. The reward pick, bn6f `sub_80AA910` (`BN6_REWARD_PICK`: Falzar's address + 0x1870, found by its opening, `push {r4-r7,lr}; sub sp,#0x2c`, r0-r5 stored to the stack, then `GetBattleNaviStatsByte(r5, 0x26)`), is the only reader of the reward rows, through its helpers `sub_80AAA3C` and `sub_80AAE98` (Battle rewards, below): its one caller passes r0, the enemies' u16 ids (BattleState `+0x54` for MegaMan's side), and r1, their count (`+9`). An answer hook at its entry rewrites those enemies' rows. object_subtractHP (bn6f, same address, `BN6_SUBTRACT_HP`): `ldrh r1,[r5,#0x24]; subs r1,r1,r0`, clamped at 0, run for every battle object every frame with r0 the damage, 0 but for a hit. A trial with a hook on it saw all of MegaMan's damage pass through it; `basicHandleBattleObjectDamage` (`0x0801156A`) serves only some Navis. The enemy spawn, bn6f `sub_800768C` (enemy id, panel x, panel y, alliance; same address), an entry that branches to the body bn6f puts in `sub_80076A0` (`0x080076A0`, where the stores below are; tools/bn6f_match.py's cross-check, docs/SYMBOLS.md): its alliance at `+0x16` (`0x080076DC`) and NameID at `+0x28`, then r2, the HP from the enemy's data (`bl 0x0800F23C`), stored as HP by `strh r2,[r5,#0x24]` at `0x08007740` (`BN6_SPAWN_HP`) and as MaxHP at `0x08007742`; a hook there caps the duel's first enemy, and gives a guardian the Net's clock's HP (docs/HOME.md, going back: BlastMan's 400 spawns at 520 with `--dev clock=3`, seed 7's layer 3 with `--net-biome 11 --guardian 12` and `CYBERWORLD_AUTOPILOT=1`). Verify: an autopilot run's log is the same as before. The rival's rung-1 duel shows "Hit!" as a Mettaur's wave hits MegaMan, and Chaud's verdict says so; a clean bust is "and not a scratch" (`tools/play.py start rv1 --fresh --seed 3 -- --scene emu --run-depth 2 --dev quiet,duels=1,onehit --talk duel:900`). The act-3 netbattle's ProtoMan stands with 500 HP (`--seed 14989687`, `--run-depth 8 --dev quiet,duels=2 --talk duel:1500`). |
| Map, flag and key item events by hook (`src/director/events.c`, issue #31) | `0x08005148` EnterMap (`0x08005152` hooked), `0x0802F114` SetEventFlag, `0x0803CD6C` GiveItem; `0x0802F0D8`/`0x0802F0F4` the map flags' clears | EnterMap is game state 0x00's routine, the first entry of the game state's jump table (`0x0800510C`, which cbGameState `0x080050EC` indexes by the state's first byte); bn6f's, at the same address in Gregar. While the screen still fades it returns at once (`bl 0x080062F8; bne`); past that, at `0x08005152`, it runs once a map is entered, after a warp and after every battle, and clears the map's flags: `sub_802F0F4` zeroes bytes `0x2C8`-`0x2DD` of the event flags (`0x1640`-`0x16EF`) and `sub_802F0D8` `0x2DE`-`0x2DF` (`0x16F0`-`0x16FF`, the warp triggers' off flags `BN6_FLAG_WARP_OFF`); the credits scene is their only other caller. An event hook there (`BN6_ENTER_MAP`) has the director write the exit pad's off flag again. SetEventFlag (`BN6_SET_EVENT_FLAG`) found by its bytes (`mov r3,r10; ldr r3,[r3,#0x44]; lsrs r1,r0,#3; adds r3,r3,r1; lsls r0,r0,#29; lsrs r0,r0,#29; movs r1,#0x80; lsrs r1,r0; ldrb r0,[r3]; orrs r0,r1; strb r0,[r3]; mov pc,lr`, one match); SetEventFlagFromImmediate (`0x0802F110`) falls into it, and the chat's `EA 00` command (bn6f `chatbox_80411B0`) calls it, so a Yes in a layer's chat reaches it; the game calls it about once a frame on the map. GiveItem (`BN6_GIVE_ITEM`; bn6f, Falzar `0x0803CD98`) found by its bytes after the call it opens with (`pop {r0,r1}; mov r2,r10; ldr r2,[r2,#0x50]; movs r6,#0xff; cmp r0,#0x80`): r0 the key item, r1 how many; a Mystery Data's key item (content type 4) goes through it. Verify: an autopilot run's log is the same as before; a layer's challenge and its Undernet warp start on a Yes (`tools/play.py start ch --fresh --seed 31 -- --scene emu --run-depth 4 --dev quiet --talk challenge:300`; `--seed 41`, `--run-depth 4 --net-biome 4 --talk undernet:300`); a ScrtData picked up is counted and MegaMan says what it is for (`--seed 53`, `--run-depth 5`: its Mystery Data at 348, -44, MegaMan put beside it with the dev step `place 328 -44 0`); a guardian's exit pad stays shut after each battle on its layer and after a CONTINUE there. |
| BN6's wait for a frame (`src/emu/idle.c`, issue #35) | `0x080003A0` main_awaitFrame, its loop at `0x080003A6`; DISPSTAT `0x04000004`, IE `0x04000200`, IME `0x04000208` | bn6f `main_awaitFrame`, at the same address in Gregar: `ldr r0,=0x04000004; movs r2,#1`, then the loop `ldrh r1,[r0]; tst r1,r2; beq` (`0x080003A6`-`0x080003AA`) until DISPSTAT's VBlank flag is set; then the VBlank count at `0x0200A870` against `0x02009930`, back to the start while short, and the count zeroed. Found by sampling the PC every 1000 GBA cycles: 48% of a layer's cycles and 57% of a battle's in that 16-byte span. mGBA does not take it for an idle loop: its `GBAIORead` clears `haltPending` on any register read but a constant one, and DISPSTAT is not one (`src/gba/io.c`). The hook (`BN6_AWAIT_FRAME_LOOP`) halts the CPU there while DISPSTAT's VBlank flag (bit 0) is clear and its interrupt is on (DISPSTAT bit 3, IE bit 0, IME bit 0), so the VBlank interrupt ends the halt by the time the flag would end the loop. Verify: the autopilot's debug log and run log are the same with and without it, and `CYBERWORLD_EMU_DEBUG`'s 30-frame line counts about four more hook hits a frame (each interrupt that wakes the CPU before VBlank brings it back to the loop). |
| ProtoMan's cross (`src/emu/protoman_cross.c`, docs/FIDELITY.md, issue #55) | `0x080FDCF0` ProtoMan's cross, his action 0x0E; `0x080FDE8C` the cross's strokes; `0x080FDF74` the cross's landing, hooked at `0x080FDFA0` (`BN6_PROTOMAN_CROSS_END`, its `strh r0,[r2,#0x2E]`, r2 his collision data); a battle object's collision data at `+0x54`, its Region at `+0x01` (`BN6_COLL_REGION`) | bn6f's ProtoMan (AI 11, his actions at `off_80FB9A0`; the cross bn6f `sub_80FC9C8`, its strokes `sub_80FCB64`, its landing `sub_80FCC4C`), located in Gregar by tools/bn6f_match.py by their bytes (docs/SYMBOLS.md) and read there. At half his HP or less, with MegaMan on the middle row's middle or front panel (bn6f `sub_80FBDF4`), he lights an X round MegaMan and dashes its two strokes, his body made the blade (`object_updateCollisionData(0x53, 2, 1)`). Each frame of a stroke clears his Region where his collision data took any contact (its `+0x70`, `0x080FDEE4`: `bl object_clearCollisionRegion`, `0x0801A074`) and sets it to 1 where he moved into another column (`0x080FDEF6`: `object_setCollisionRegion`, `0x0801A07C`); the last phase restores his collision data (`0x080FDF92`) but not his Region. Region 0 is an empty list in bn6f `PanelOffsetListsPointerTable` (`0x7F` alone): he stands on no panel, and every attack passes through him. Seen in romlab on the plain ROM, 6 October 2026: checkThenStartBattle patched at `0x08005A98` to start BN6's own record of ProtoMan V1 (`0x080B0850`) from Central Area 1, his HP set to 800 of 1800, MegaMan on 2,2 at 9999 HP with a folder of Silence. Silence used 45 frames into a cross: its music's pulses (a zero-damage blind on every enemy on the field, every few frames, bn6f `sub_80D43E8`) cleared his Region in the last stroke's last column (3,1; the write at `0x0801A078`, called from `0x080FDEEC`), and nothing wrote it until his next cross: 23 buster shots from column 1 in his row, 0 damage, through 1200 frames of his moves and slashes. The same without Silence: 34 shots, 25 damage. Without Silence, MegaMan stepping onto 3,1 while the X was lit, struck there by the last stroke: 22 shots, 0 damage. BN6's reset for an attack cut short (bn6f `sub_80FBCA2`, `0x080FCFCA`) sets his Region to 1. A search of bn6f's navi AIs in `asm31.s` for a Region cleared on contact, `object_clearCollisionRegion` just after a test of `FlagsFromCollision`, finds four attacks: the cross, and three of the Cybeast's, which each set it again as they end (bn6f `sub_8104A30`, `sub_8104DC8`). Verify: the act-3 netbattle, `--scene emu --run-depth 8 --seed 7 --net-biome 11 --dev god,onehit,quiet,duels=2,folder=149,hp=2000 --talk duel:900`, Yes taken, a Silence picked, used at frame 2250 as the X is lit, MegaMan to column 1 at 2506 and the buster mashed: `CYBERWORLD_EMU_DEBUG` prints "his cross left him untouchable" at 2470, and the shot at 2560, ProtoMan beside MegaMan in his row, deletes him; without the hook every frame to 2470 is the same, that shot passes through him, and the battle goes on to frame 4000. |
| Random battles (`src/core/formations.c`) | `0x020170` four tables | The default table and three that event flags `0x67F`, `0x680` and `0x681` choose while story scenes set them (bn6f `sub_80AA5F4`); compared pointer by pointer they differ only in Central and Seaside. Each table holds two pointers, real world and internet; the internet one lists per map group (from 0x80) per map a run of 16-byte BattleSettings ending with 0xFF (bn6f `sub_80AA5F4`). Byte 0 is the battlefield (the panels' layout: grass, holes, ice, poison...), bytes 12-15 the entity list. Byte 7 says when a battle may come: 0 always, 1 a rare battle (the game waits for EVENT_1D8 and 16 or 32 of the family deleted, counted at `0x02000070`; the engine leaves these out), 3-11 an SP navi each behind one of EVENT_212-219 and 222 (BlastMan, DiveMan, CircusMan, JudgeMan, ElementMan, navi 17, Colonel, Bass BX, GBeast). Every internet map has 10-50 of them: Central's Mettaur, Swordy, Quaker, Champy, Trumpy, OldStov, Gunner, Armadill; Seaside's Piranha, Puffy, StarFish; Green's BombCorn, HonyBmbr, Armadill; Sky's ScarCrow, FgtrPlne, Catack; Undernet's BigHat, SnakeArm, PulsBulb, DarkMech, Shrubby, Cragger; Graveyard and Underground most of the 29 families, with ErthDrgn and Nghtmare; each area also hides a rare pair and one SP navi. An area fights the formations of its maps (`RomLayout.net_area[].battles`), weighted by how many tables list them. The virus names archive (`TextScriptVirusChipNames`) is indexed by the enemy id of `0x0182C4`. |
| Map takeover (`src/map/mapslot.c`) | `0x0347E0` NPCList_maps80, `0x034670` map scripts, `0x03483C` object spawners, `0x0A484C` Mystery Data groups, `0x02004348` Mystery Data picks | Per group (index group − 0x80; the internet is 0x90-0x96) the engine points the layer's map at its own NPC list, empty map scripts and objects, and its Mystery Data (12-byte entries: type 5 green, 1 blue or 3 purple, flag 0x1400+n, one placement `[1, 0x20, X, Y, Z]`, one content `[kind, 0x20, 0xFF, code, value]`; kinds 1 chip, 3 zenny, 4 key item, 5 BugFrags). The picks (a placement and a content index per flag, 2 bytes at `0x02004348` + 2 × (flag − 0x1400), bn6f `sub_809FADE`) are shared with the game's own Mystery Data of every map, and a new game steps the picks of its blue and purple ones (`sub_809FAF4`): each placement and content is written eight times over, so any pick reads it. The object table is the literal of the spawner's first `ldr r1, [pc]`. Every jack-in rolls the green ones' picks again, of every map at once: BN6's jack-in (bn6f `sub_8033FDC`) calls bn6f `sub_809F9DC` (Gregar `0x080A0EBC`; its other caller the game's load at `0x08004E8C`, both found by a scan of Gregar's BL instructions for that target), which walks `0x0A484C`'s groups, each group's per-map lists until a null pointer, and each type 5 entry's placements and contents into BN6's weighted pick (`0x08000E3A`, bn6f `sub_8000E3A`). A map a layer left keeps the list it was given, in the half of the layer data a later layer writes over: the jack-in from home before the Nest crashed (the PC at 0 with the stack overwritten, the last frame inside that pick, called from the roll). The roll clears each green entry's flag too (`ldrh r0,[r7,#2]` at `0x080A0F14`, then `bl` ClearEventFlag `0x0802F130` at `0x080A0F18`; bn6f the same after `sub_809FACE`), and BN6's own maps' flags are the run's: of the 70 green entries in `0x0A484C`'s lists (flags `0x1402`-`0x158F`), Underground's (group `0x93`) map 0 holds `0x1462` and `0x1463` (`0x080A38C8`, `0x080A38D4`: the lab's request taken, a request held), its map 1 `0x146A` and `0x146B` (`0x080A39D0`, `0x080A39DC`: a request settled, the visit's order at AsterLand's counter), and group `0x92`'s map 1 `0x144A` and `0x144B` (a guardian's data shown and taken). So a jack-in cleared a settled request's flag, and the lab's asker paid his 1000 Zenny again (issue #122). Each layer's install points every map the roll reaches but its own at an empty list (`EMU_FREE` + 0x0180), BN6's untouched maps too, by the roll's own walk (a group 0 skipped, each group's maps to their null); not at none, which would end its group's walk. A net map installed with no Mystery Data (Lan's HP) points only its own at it, and the layer's stays for the jack-in. Found by listing each green entry's flag in Gregar's lists, and the roll's code read in Gregar and bn6f. Verify: `CYBERWORLD_AUTOPILOT=weak build.py shot --scene emu --seed 1 --setup short,0,0,0 --frames 32000`, home after each act, wins the Nest (the runlog's `run won at depth 10`); at home before layer 10 with the lab's 1000 Zenny due (`--scene home --seed 7 --run-depth 10 --dev job=3,jobstate=2`), the asker pays it, and after a jack-in at Lan's PC and a jack-out he pays nothing more. |
| Lan's HP (`src/map/lanhp.c`, `src/director/director_home.c`, issue #93) | map `0x88:00`; Lan's room's jack-in table `0x0804F874`; jack-in destination 1 (`0x08099A00` + 20); Lan's HP's warp list `0x0806CE90`; map objects `0xCB`-`0xCE` (`0x0A4F24` + 16 × id); flags `0x017D`-`0x0180`, `0x16F3`-`0x16F6`; the warp departures' jump table (bn6f `JumpTable8005948`) | Lan lives in Central Town (group `0x01`: the town `0x00`, his house `0x01`, his room `0x02`, the bathroom `0x03`, AsterLand `0x04`; bn6f `constants/enums/GameAreas.inc`). His room's jack-in table (read through the literal its group's map-enter routine stores to GameState `+0x64`, as `mapslot.c`'s `jack_in_table` does) sends point `0x40` to destination 1: Lan's HP (`0x88:00`, world -178, -14, on its blue pad). Central Town's two points go to destination 0, the RoboDog Comp (`0x8C:00`); ACDC Town's `0x40` to 23, ACDC HP, and its `0x41` to 41, the doghouse comp (`0x8D:0E`); the bathroom's to 7, its comp. Lan's HP's trigger cells hold warp entries 1 to 6 (their middles at -176, -16; 208, -16; -28, -72; 72, 32; 68, -72; -32, 32), and its warp list (16 bytes an entry; trigger value v runs entry v - 1) sends 1 to BN6's jack-out (departure `0x10`), 2 (the pink pad) to Central Area 1, and 3 to 6 (the link squares) to the Aquarium, Green, Sky and ACDC HPs. A departure byte indexes bn6f `JumpTable8005948` by fours: `0x00` fade to black, `0x04` to white, `0x08` jack out, fade and jack in (the net's links, every exit pad), `0x0C` the same in one map (teleports), `0x10` jack out to where Lan jacked in, `0x14` the Levbus, `0x18` underground. Each link square has a map object (`0xCB` to `0xCE`, kind 5 at the link's place 64 below the floor): sprite 0x88 of list 7, BN6's link marker, shown while its flag `0x017D` + n is set; while it is clear the object sets warp-off flag `0x16F3` + n every frame, so the link is locked (and BN6's `sub_8034F68` runs its entrance cutscene there). BN6's homepages play song `0x13` (the map music lists, every chapter). The way from the blue pad to the pink pad runs up a corridor whose mouth, at x 119, is open from y -24 to -4: MegaMan placed at x 90 on each y and walked +x passed it from -24 to -4 and stopped at 119 elsewhere, and a walk straight from the blue pad keeps to y -1, a few units off the line (session 69); the arrow leads onto it (`director_town.c`, `hp_way`). BN6's trigger for the pink pad (cells x 192 to 223, y -32 to -1, value 2) stops short of the cells' front corner: MegaMan walked -y from x 194, 196, 198 and 200 at y 4 set its warp under way at y -7, -5, -3 and -1, the line x - y = 201, the same on v0.12.0; standing on 194, -1 (inside cell 192, -8) did not, and the pad's pink middle as drawn is about 194, 0, outside it (session 74), so the director takes the pad's warp on its art (`lanhp_pad_take`). Its art, measured over a frame with MegaMan at 176, 0 (his sprite's foot 3 pixels under his place, as over a layer's pad): a ring 62 by 42 pixels round a middle 4 units in front of its cells' (about 204, -12). There, on its left rim, a playtester stood 150 frames, and at 171, -5, and nothing warped (session 75): his feet 16 pixels left of its middle and 20 below, in front of the zone #161 stretched; the portals' zone is now their cells and their art as MegaMan stands on it (`pad_art.c`, as a layer's exit pad: Warp pads). AGENTS.md's town row walks it too, its last walk `0:place 171 -5 1,10:,12:RIGHT,250:`: MegaMan put on the left rim warps at once, and its last frame is layer 1; v0.12.0 and #161 leave him there. Found by reading those tables in Gregar, the links' flags by printing `0x16F0`-`0x16FF` each frame in the HP (`0x007A` with BN6's objects, the director's own with them gone) and the records of their objects. Verify: `--scene home --run-depth 7 --marks 08` shows two link markers lit beside the pink pad; MegaMan on the link up top warps to the other way's layer ("home: way 1 taken" with `CYBERWORLD_EMU_DEBUG=1`), R asks BN6's "MegaMan, jack out?", and its yes sets Lan down at his PC. |

| Requests' scripts (`src/world/job_words.c`, docs/HOME.md piece 5, issue #88) | BN6's text commands `EF 09` (ts_check_pack_chip_code), `EF 08` (ts_check_chip_code), `F4 11` (ts_item_take_chip), `EF 0E` and `EF 12` (ts_check_give_zenny, ts_check_give_bug_frags); bn6f `chatbox_8041818`, `GetChipCountOfCode`, `sub_8021C02`, `sub_8006EA4` | `EF 08` and `EF 09` share one routine: `EF 08` compares a chip's count in a code (`GetChipCountOfCode`) with the amount, `EF 09` that count less the folders' copies (`sub_8021C02` counts the folders' entries of that chip and code), so a request's hand-over never takes a Folder's chip; `F4 11` then takes one from the count. `GetChipCountOfCode` reads 0 for a chip whose mark fails BN6's check (`sub_8006EA4`: its byte in the marks at Toolkit `+0x7C`, as `own_folder_chips` writes it), so `--dev pack=N` marks the chips it adds. | Home's shops (`src/layer/shop.c` shop_order_install and shop_home_subs, `src/world/aster_land.c`, docs/HOME.md piece 6, issue #89) | the shop descriptors `0x08046B68` (16 bytes a shop); the Order Service's descriptor `0x08046C88`, shop `0x12`'s, currency 2, its list at ShopData `+0x480`, 0x140 slots; the Order Service's keeper text `0x087F0FE8`; the Order Service's list in ROM `0x080481F0`-`0x080489DF`; Central Town's SubChip shop `0x08048130`, shop `0x0F`; the shop screen's list `0x02019A00` (`BN6_SHOP_LIST`) | All addresses 0x30 below Falzar's, each checked by disassembling Gregar. The screen lists an entry where the chip's Library flag (`0x1E20` + chip) or a comparison's (`0x2020`, `0x2220`) is set (bn6f `sub_8048DF8`), sorted, a sold-out one dashed; its code and price are the entry's, no random roll; Yes checks the chip's mark (Toolkit `+0x7C`, or a comparison's at `+0x80`: bn6f `sub_8006F1E`) and its code, gives it (GiveChips) and lowers the stock by one, none restocking it: a chip once a save. The keeper's face in its text is `0x33`, list 6's sprite `0x33` (BN6's AsterLand clerk, whose NPC script's `ns_set_sprite` takes list 6: bn6f `NPCCommand_set_sprite`, category `0x18`); the SubChip shops' text (`0x087F0AFC`, all five) has `0x10`, the white-coated man. Shop `0x0F` holds MiniEnrg 100, Unlocker 4000 and LocEnemy 7000 (Central Town's street script opens it, `FB 05 0F`). The screen's own list at `0x02019A00` holds the entries as shown (bn6f `sub_8047B88`), 8 bytes each, the cursor's at row + scroll. Found by reading those routines and tables in bn6f and Gregar. Verify: `--scene town --dev zenny=8000`, at AsterLand's register the clerk's list with Cannon A at 1000 zenny; ordered, "I bought "Cannon A"!", 7000 zenny, and his "That's your order for today!" after; the seller beside him: MiniEnrg, FullEnrg, SneakRun, Untrap; the second screen names the chip under the order's cursor. |
| The Order Service's sort (`src/world/order_sort.c`, `src/emu/bn6_shop.h`, `src/emu/bn6_chips.h`, docs/HOME.md piece 6, issue #124) | RAM `0x0201AE00` the Order Service's entries as the screen opens (`BN6_SHOP_SOURCE`); the shop screen's state `0x02011C10` (`BN6_SHOP_STATE`): +0 its course, `0x08` the list, `0x1C` back to it after a line; +0x0D the list made again (`BN6_SHOP_REDRAW`), +0x12 its entries, +0x24 the keeper's archive; chatbox_runScript `0x08040358`, hooked (in AsterLand alone, map `0x01:04`: BN6's order screen elsewhere, the Net Square's Trade Central, keeps its words); BN6's sorts: IWRAM `0x030069A0` a key's way (`BN6_SORT_MODES`), `0x08121D3C` the Element sort's places (`BN6_SORT_ELEMENTS`); a Navi chip's power by time: RAM `0x020018C0` the times, `0x08010CB8` the steps, `0x08020E54` the powers | Read in bn6f and found in Gregar by the symbols' byte match (docs/SYMBOLS.md), each address checked in Gregar's bytes. **The screen:** ShopControl (Gregar `0x08046CF8`, its states' table `0x08046CEC`) opens with bn6f `sub_8046D4C` (Gregar `0x08046D1C`): `sub_8048DF8` (shop, `0x02019A00`, `0x0201AE00`; their literals at Gregar `0x08046D9C` and `0x08046DA0`) puts an order shop's entries the Library holds in `0x0201AE00` by IDSortPos, then runs its keeper's script 0. `sub_8047B88` copies them to the list shown, `0x02019A00` (`BN6_SHOP_LIST`; `0x0201AE00` its literal at `0x08047C6C`), whenever `sub_8047134`, run every frame, finds +0x0D set: the list's input (bn6f `sub_8047950`, Gregar `0x08047920`) sets it at each move and answers A, B, L, R and the pad, never START or SELECT. State `0x1C` (bn6f `sub_8047028`, Gregar `0x08046FF8`) waits for the line under way to hold, then runs script 1 and goes back to `0x08`. An order finds its entries by what they are, never by their place: `sub_8047B88` (Gregar `0x08047B58`), given the chip bought, lowers the stock of that chip's entry in `0x0201AE00` and the list, and bn6f `sub_8048DAE` (Gregar `0x08048D7E`) that of the shop data's entry of its kind, chip and code, so a sorted list orders, and marks sold out, the chip it shows. The Order Service's archive (LZ77 `0x087F0FE8`, unpacked to `0x02029A00`; +0x24 reads `0x02029A04`): script 0 the list's prompt as it opens, script 1 after each line, both `FC 06 F5 00 33 E8 02 F1 00 00`, three lines, `EE FF 00 00`; chatbox_runScript's first instruction `push {r4-r6,lr}` (`B570`). **The sorts:** the Pack's list (bn6f `sub_811FE7C`, Gregar `0x08121C58`) makes each entry eight words, a key << 16 | IDSortPos << 7 | the code: ID (IDSortPos), ABCDE (AlphabetSortPos), Code, Attack (bn6f `sub_811FF80`, Gregar `0x08121D5C`: effect flag 0x10 1, a power not shown 5, one past 999 computed, `sub_8010C76`), Element (bn6f `byte_811FF60` by ChipElement, Gregar `0x08121D3C`: `00 01 02 03 08 04 06 07 05 09 0A 00`), No., MB; bn6f `sub_8120018` (Gregar `0x08121DF4`) sorts by one, up or down (its IWRAM routines `0x030069A9` and `0x03006A5F`, the low half inverted for down, so ties stay by ID up); the Chip Trader's SORT window (START, bn6f `sub_804AAD0`; A, `sub_804AE04`, its literal `0x030069A0` at Gregar `0x0804BC0C`) gives a new key the way of its byte at `0x030069A0` XOR 4, read in a run `04 04 04 00 04 00 04 04`: Attack and No. down, the rest up; the same key again turns it. A Navi chip's power (1001 + n, bn6f `sub_8010C76`, Gregar's as Falzar's): its best time, u16 frames at `0x020018C0` + 2n, in BCD h:m:s and hundredths (bn6f `memory_bcd_8000D84`), its step the first of the ten times at `0x08010CB8` (`0x1000`, ten seconds, to `0x2800`) it is not past, the power the u16 at `0x08020E54` + 22n + 2 x the step (260, 250 .. 160 the first Navi's). Watched in a run with `CYBERWORLD_WATCH='030069A0:8,02011C10:48,0201AE00:48'` at the clerk (`home-order`'s input): twelve entries by ID at `0x0201AE00` (Cannon A first, `02 01 01 00 00 00 0A 00`), the state's +0x12 12, +0x1C `0x08046C88`, +0x24 `0x02029A04`. Verify: `build.py screenshots home-sort` (a Library of every chip of the list, the dev step's flags): "By Attack,high first!" over SumnBlk3, BlkBomb, GolmHit3; `build.py clips home-sort`, START through the seven keys and SELECT; in `tools/play.py` with the Library the run's own, an order from a sorted list ("...Yep,got 'em!!", the chip dashed), B and No back on the sort, and the list opening in it again. |

| Rush's gaps (`src/layer/rush.c`, `src/map/coords.c`, `src/map/netmap_extra.c`, issue #14) | `0x0809DEA0` the check (bn6f sub_809C968), `0x0809DF78` deployed? (sub_809CA40), `0x0809DFBC` the RushFood count's text (sub_809CA84), their table's literals `0x0809DF70`, `0x0809DFB8`, `0x0809DFF0` (BN6's table `0x0809D628`); `0x080AB404` handler 0x25 (Rush lying), its records' literal `0x080AB42C` (BN6's 13 at `0x080AB334`); OverworldMapObjects `0xD5`-`0xDD` the bones (`0x080A4F24` + id × 16, `0xD6`'s priority at `0x080A5C87`, its record's byte 3: 2, where `0xD5`'s is 3; `0x080A5887`, written before 4 October 2026, was `0x96`'s); flags `0x224` (Rush known), `0x12A`+g (gap g bridged), `0x18E`+g (its bones shown), `0x1BB` (No Rush Coil); events `0x16BF`-g; key item `0x2C` RushFood | Each frame the check takes A at section-3 trigger values 0x30-0x33 on maps of groups 0x80 and up: index (group − 0x90) × 4 into its table, then the map number, gives the map's gap records (24 bytes: x, y, z 16.16 where MegaMan stands, 8 in from the floor's edge on the lane's axis; direction 0 +X, 1 +Y, 2 −X, 3 −Y; length − 1, 0-2; the deployed flag; the bones' flag), the record the trigger value − 0x30. With RushFood held as many as the gap's panels and the gap not bridged, it runs the cutscene (its text `0x0873991C`: "MegaMan has thrown N pcs. of Rush Food."), which calls Rush, sets the deployed flag and takes one RushFood; too few, a sound alone. All three readers of the table take its literal, so the engine points the three at a table of its own from group 0x80 (`EMU_FREE` + 0x0400), every group but the layer's own pointing at a map list of zeros. Handler 0x25's 16-byte record: the deployed flag (shown while set), the event that switches the gap's mouth walls off (set each frame while shown), the bones' flag (cleared while shown), sprite 7:0x9E (uncompressed) animation 5, priority, a flip for gaps along Y. Rush and the bones are spawned per panel at the panel's spot + (64, −64, −64). Wall flag bytes 0x80+n are off while event 0x1640+n is set (`0x08030BD0`). Verified: live in romlab on Central Area 2 and with its gap moved to comp map 0x8C:0 through the repointed literals; on a generated layer (seed 8, depth 2) A with RushFood ran the cutscene, Rush lay in the gap, MegaMan crossed both ways and took the island's data; without RushFood the edge held. |
| Teleports and the pads' centrepieces (`src/map/mapslot.c`, `src/map/netmap.c`, `src/map/netmap_paste.c`, issue #44) | warp-list departure `0x0C`; second-layer tiles `0x379` (the gem), `0x375` (the cube), `0x372` (the ring) in the surface areas' tile set `0x085D0FC4` | A warp entry of departure 12 moves MegaMan within the map to its x, y, z, the camera scrolling along (the check `0x080058D0` takes trigger values 1-15 as entry value − 1). Over the 12 surface maps every centrepiece stands on a warp trigger: the 18 gems on departure-12 teleports in pairs (each pad's entry lands on the other pad: Central 1 one pair, Green 1 four, Green 2 one, Sky 2 two, Sky 3 one), the 5 cubes on departure-8 links to homepages (Central 1 to 88:0, Seaside 2 to 88:3, Green 1 to 88:5, Sky 1 to 88:6, Sky 3 to 88:1), the 6 rings on departure-8 links to comps and between Central 2 and Seaside 3; none is decoration. Found by decoding each map's second tile layer, placing each anchor tile's art at the floor height of the trigger under it and matching section-3 warp triggers within 20 units against the maps' warp lists. Verify: seed 12 at depth 2 in Green Area (`--net-biome 3`): stepping on the gem by the way beams MegaMan to the island's gem and back. |
| Link Navi obstacles and security cubes (`src/layer/blockers.c`, `src/map/netmap_extra.c`, issue #42) | `0x080A66A4` map-object handler 3 (the handler table `0x08004724`, entry 3), its records' literal `0x080A66D4` (BN6's 86 at `0x080A6144`, ids 0x00-0x55); `0x08030BD0` the wall test (literal `0x08030CA8` = 0x1640); `0x08034728` the internet maps' check tables, `0x08040798` their text archives' (per group from 0x80, per map); `0x08099890` the checks' cutscene | A record is 16 bytes: the present flag (the object stands while set), the walls' flag (0x1640 + n: set, walls off), the check's flag (0x16C0 + m: set, check off), then sprite list (category × 4), sprite, idle and open animations, a sprite field, palette, priority, a spare and its sound (0x74). Cleared, the present flag starts the open animation; at its end the walls go off and the object frees itself, and entering the map with it clear, it never stands. A wall cell whose value has bit 7 set is skipped while flag 0x1640 + (value & 0x7F) is set. A wall cell holds MegaMan back from the floor its type names alone (types 1-4: the floor at its −X, +X, −Y or +Y side): from the other side he walks through it, so a line across a walkway holds one way (issue #111: at seed 3, depth 3 in Central with `--dev pieces=16`, MegaMan stopped at a cube's line from the floor before it, and walked out of its pocket through it). The object id is read as a byte, so the engine copies BN6's 86 records to `EMU_FREE` + 0x0700 and adds its own after them (id 0x56 + variant × 8 + slot: the cube four ways, the cyber geyser 3:0x25, the cybertree 7:0x1F, the flames 7:0x0E, the cyclone 7:0x4B, the cloud 7:0x20, as BN6's ids 0x05/0x08/0x0D/0x2C and 0x36/0x38/0x3C/0x37/0x3B have them; then another game's looks, docs/MULTIROM.md), once a core; present flags 0x1458 + slot (none of BN6's Mystery Data flags; outside 0x1640-0x16FF, which entering a map clears). A check: section-3 cells of value 0xF0 + m run script m of the map's check table from the map's own archive (decompressed to `0x02033400`). BN6's Link Navi talk opens an obstacle only for the right Navi in the PET (Gregar: the geyser and the cloud ElecMan or EraseMan, the tree HeatMan or SlashMan, the flames HeatMan or ChargeMan, the cyclone SlashMan or ChargeMan; mugshots 0x47 HeatMan, 0x49 ElecMan, 0x4B SlashMan, 0x50 EraseMan, 0x4F ChargeMan, sounds 0xF7, 0xC6, 0xFA (0x164 at a cyclone), 0xC7, 0xE4). Found from bn6f's handler and BN6's records, verified in romlab on the originals (clearing the present flag opened Green Area 1's tree, Seaside Area 2's cyclone, Seaside Area 1's geyser, Underground 1's cloud, Underground 2's flames and Green Area 1's cube) and with records, walls, checks and archive moved onto comp 0x8C:0; on a generated layer (seed 11, depth 2, Green Area, `--setup short,0,0,0,1`): the tree stood in the pocket's mouth, A ran MegaMan's and HeatMan's words and it vanished, MegaMan walked in and took the pocket's data, and after a CONTINUE it stayed open. A security cube's talk (issue #45) is the same check: `EF 00` (ts_check_flag: a u16 flag, then the scripts for set and clear, 0xFF on) jumps to the script that opens it once the layer's P-Code teller has set his flag (0x1465), and a toll's `EF 0F` (ts_check_take_zenny: a u32 amount, then the scripts for enough, none and some) takes the zenny or turns to the script for too little. Verify: seed 6 at depth 2 in a comp (`--net-biome 8`): A at the cube names it and asks around, the teller eight panels on tells its code, then A at the cube enters it and it vanishes; seed 4 at depth 2 in Seaside (`--net-biome 1 --talk zenny:300`): the toll takes 200 of 10000 zenny, and with none MegaMan says so. The Undernet's doors (issue #47) are the same records (all 37 of BN6's cubes share sprite 7:0x03 and palette 0; its five WWW doors' present flags are `0x0FF9`-`0x0FFD`): a skull door's talk is `EF 07 44 01 equal more fewer` (ts_check_item07 on key item `0x44`, WWWID in Gregar's key item names at `0x0873B938`), a number door's a column of three options (`EB`, `ED 07 80 j0 j1 j2 jB`; a line break after the last option asked for a fourth line and left no cursor). Verify: `--dev pieces=16` at depth 14 (the Undernet's act): seed 4 a number door (eight braziers; the right number opens it, seed 5 a wrong one seals it), seed 3 a skull door (refused without the ID, opened with `--talk wwwid:300`), and the Net Dealer there lists WWWID. |
| Map sprite lists (`src/map/mapslot.c`) | `0x03093C` EnterMap_InternetMapGroupJumptable | Each group's loader passes a per-map list of `(0x1C, sprite)` pairs ending `0xFFFF` to uncompSprite; compressed overworld objects (pointer bit 31, like the Chip Trader 0x5C) only draw once listed. The table is the literal of the loader's `lsl r1, r1, #2; ldr r0, [pc]; ldr r0, [r0, r1]`; the engine replaces the map's list with the layer's own objects (the original's would fill the decompression buffer). |
| NPC and text scripts (`src/layer/npc.c`, `text.c`) | charmap and commands per bn6f `text_script_commands.inc` | NPCs are bn6f NPC bytecode (0x08 visible, 0x25 sprite `index, category * 4`, 0x14 position, 0x16 animation, 0x44 text script and archive, 0x10 pause, 0x02 jump). Layer dialogue is a text archive (u16 offsets, then scripts) using E8 open, E7 wait, F2 clear, E6 end, FC 03 13 full HP, FB 05 shop, FB 06 01 BugFrag trader, FB 06 03 Chip Trader. NPC command 0x13 keeps an object from turning to MegaMan when spoken to; objects have no such frames and would vanish. |
| Chip Traders (`src/layer/trader.c`) | `0x086C3758` TextScriptChipTrader, `0x0804CDE0` prize pools, `0x0809B7E4` trader kinds | The trader archive is stored uncompressed; script 0 is the Chip Trader (3 chips), 6 the Chip Trader Special (10), 12 the BugFrag Trader, each with the game's Yes/No, not-enough lines and "Try again?". The trade screen (`selectChipTraderRewardList`, Gregar `0x0804CDB4`) looks the current map (group << 8 \| number) up in five 12-byte entries (map key, chip list of 6-byte records ending with 0, weights per rarity): 0x104 70 chips of rarity 1-3, 0x501 130 of 1-4, 0x000 108 of 1-3, 0x400 163 mostly 3-4, 0x9301 60 of 3-5 for BugFrags. With no match it returns no list and draws garbage prizes. `0x0809B7BC` looks the map up in six 8-byte entries (map key, byte) for the trader's script, which picks the lines the screen shows; with no match it takes the map number. Each layer rewrites the second entry of both tables (Sky Town's `0x501`, a map no run visits) with its own map; the first stays AsterLand's `0x104`, whose own Chip Trader stands at home (issue #96), its list's records BN6's own. Falzar's tables are at `0x0804C01C` and in `asm03_2.s`. The prize itself (bn6f `sub_804BD00`): two weights at `0x0804BDCC` (`C0 40`, Falzar's `dword_804B008` 0xDC4 later, as the reward list routine is; the pool weights `70 60 18 10 08` follow) choose between the pool's chips whose Library flag (`0x1E20` + chip id) is set and those whose is not, falling back to the other when one is empty; then a rarity by the pool's weights (fallback orders at `0x0804BDF0`) and a chip of it by `GetRNG`. Layers write `00 40`: new chips first. A list record is 6 bytes: the chip id (u16), then up to four codes as the chip data has them (0-25 A-Z, 26 *, `0xFF` ends); the prize's code (bn6f `sub_804BF18`, Gregar `0x0804CCDC` by `tools/bn6f_match.py`'s map) is one of its record's codes, one the pack holds none of three times in four: two weights at `0x0804BE2C` (`C0 40`, read by the literal at `0x0804CD50`; Falzar's `byte_804B068`), the first the codes the pack lacks. Since issue #154 the layers' and home's traders keep their pools' records as BN6 has them, so that pick spreads a run's codes as BN6 spreads them (docs/ECONOMY.md); before, they rewrote the records in the core's copy to the folder's code and kept only those, which gave every run's trades its folder's letters. Verify by reading the two bytes at `0x0804BE2C`, by dumping a pool's first records beside the chips' own codes at `chip_data` (the same bytes, the core's copy as the file's), and by trading at a layer's trader three times with a pack of one code: most prizes come in another. |
| The BugFrag Trader's trade (`src/director/director_folder.c` bugfrag_trade, issue #12) | `0x0804B0AC`, `0x0804CAC4`, `0x08021AEE` GiveChips, `0x0803D09C` TakeBugfrags, `0x0803F76C`, `0x0200AC80`; the chat box's flags `0x02009F38` | Script 12's `FB 06 01` (ts_start_chip_trader, amount 1) only checks that MegaMan holds 10 BugFrags, then the chat holds (ts_wait_hold): the trade is the Undernet map's trader machine, a cutscene (bn6f `sub_809A078`, `cutscenescript_8099EEE`). While the chat box is open (flag `0x80` of `0x02009F38`) and holds (`0x08`) with its low bits (`& 7`) clear, the machine clears the trade's state (bn6f `sub_804A2E8`, Gregar `0x0804B0AC`: `0x0200AC80`, 0x58 bytes, and the submenu's), rolls the prize (`sub_804BD00`, `0x0804CAC4`: r0 the chip, r1 its code; the Chip Traders row), gives it (GiveChips: chip, code, count), takes ten BugFrags (TakeBugfrags), copies the GameState's map (+4) to its +0xC and saves the game (`sub_803F798`, `0x0803F76C`, which the Chip Trader's screen calls after its trades too), puts the chip and code in the chat box's words +0x4C and +0x50 and runs script 15: the hand-over, the prize, "Try again?". No, to either question, runs script 17: the box closes and holds with its low bits set, and the machine's scene ends the chat; the Chip Trader's No runs script 5, a bare end. Script 15 sets `EVENT_F6` for the machine's howl, which the machine clears. The routines were found from Falzar's by their bytes (`B520 4805 2158` for the first, `B5FE 2400 E001 B5FE 2401` for the save, 0x2C before Falzar's; the first two 0xDC4 after, as the Chip Traders row's are); the chat box's archive is its word +0x30. The engine takes the ten BugFrags before it gives the prize, and ends the chat with script 5 where TakeBugfrags took none (it returns 0 only when it took them, 1 at none, 2 at too few, the count left as it was): script 12's not-enough line ("Too bad, Lan. We don't have 10 BugFrags!") holds as its Yes does, and taken for a Yes it gave a chip for nothing, again at every "Try again?" (issue #21). Verify with `tools/play.py start bt --seed 11835737 -- --scene emu --net-biome 4 --run-depth 2 --talk bugfrags:2600,bugtrader:2800`, A pressed through Chaud's call first: Yes gives a Navi chip (ChrgeMan \*, in the pack), 50 BugFrags become 40 (the state's `bugfrags`), five trades end at 0 and the sixth Yes shows the not-enough line and ends the chat, no chip given; after No at either question the chat ends and the PET opens. |
| A Net Square trade settled (`src/online/online_trade.c`, docs/ONLINE.md, Trade Central) | `0x08021B93` TakeChips (`BN6_TAKE_CHIPS`), `0x08021AEF` GiveChips (`BN6_GIVE_CHIPS`) | TakeChips (bn6f `TakeChips`, Thumb, the same address in Gregar) takes r0 the chip, r1 its code, r2 the count, and lowers the code's count in the Pack (through getOffsetToQuantityOfChipCode, the chip registry row) as GiveChips raises it; found in bn6f's `asm/asm02.s` beside GiveChips and matched to Gregar by its bytes (`tools/bn6f_match.py`), then called on two fresh cores at the overworld hook: HiCannon L's count 3 to 2, the return at its own PC (PR #121, at `7b4b8ef`). A committed trade is applied on the gate's checkpoint as MegaMan comes home, TakeChips for the chip given and GiveChips for the chip got, one each, then the checkpoint saved again. (Trade Central's chip picker was the Order Service's own screen until issue #145, whose spares list draws each chip's card from its record's picture, palette and icon, the second screen's chip pictures row, and its name and description through the chip registry.) Verify: two games at the gate of a local server (docs/ONLINE.md, Two games at once), a trade made at Trade Central and accepted on both, then each one's Pack (the `state` step's `pack` line, or the folder editor) one copy fewer of the chip it gave and one more of the chip it got, `trade.pending` gone from both data folders. |
| Floor sprites (`src/layer/npc.c`) | NPC commands `0x1B`, `0x0C` | Overworld sprites share MegaMan's layer priority, which coordinate data section 2 sets per position (`checkLayerPriority_80318b0`), and the game orders them in OAM front to back by depth. `ns_set_layer_priority_override_to_3` puts a pad at priority 3, under MegaMan and over the floor layers. That alone fails from behind: between sprites the GBA draws the earlier OAM entry whatever their priority bits, so a pad sorted in front of MegaMan took the pixels over his feet and then lost them to the floor layer. Pads therefore stand 64 units further back in depth (+X, -Y) and 64 lower, which draws them in the same place but sorts them after MegaMan, and `ns_shift_center` (x, y, z signed bytes, stored in the object's CenterOffsetX/Y/Z) moves their collision and talk centre back to the pad. |
| Shops (`src/layer/shop.c`) | `0x046B68` shop descriptors, `0x047D70` initial shop data, eToolkit+0x54 ShopDataPtr | A descriptor is 16 bytes: currency (0 zenny, 1 BugFrags, 2 Chip Order), keeper text, offset into the shop data, entries. Entries are 8 bytes: kind (1 item: 0x70 HPMemory, 0x80+ SubChips; 2 chip; 3 NaviCust program), stock (0xFF unlimited), u16 id, code (program color), 0, u16 price in hundreds. The shop screen lists the entries with stock left (a last one bought, the keeper says "We're all sold out!" over an empty list), and empties the list unless each entry's id and code appear among that shop's entries in the ROM's initial data (bn6f `sub_80473E6`), so layers write their stock to both; the text command `EF 22 shop stocked soldout` (ts_check_shop_stock) jumps by whether any entry has stock left; shops 0 (the Net Dealer, keeper face 0x3E) and 3 (the NaviCust vendor, keeper face 0x42, zenny, 8 entries: descriptor `0x046B98`) are taken over. Shop 3's keeper text (`0x7F0420`, its descriptor's text pointer) is LZ77; its decompressed text says `F5 00 42` (the orange technician navi's face) seven times, the first from a literal at `0x7F046A` (`BN6_VENDOR_FACE`), the other six back-references that copy it, so that one byte, 0x45 in the core's copy, gives the window the vendor's own GirlNavi face (`vendor_face`). Verify: decompress the text with and without the byte changed: exactly seven bytes differ, each a face; a program shop's window then shows the face its chat does. The layers' picks come from the initial data as the player's ROM holds it, never from the core's rewritten copy, so a layer's stock does not depend on the layers built before it. Traced in `tools/romlab`. |
| Key items (`src/director/director.c`) | eToolkit+0x50 KeyItemsPtr (`0x02003134`), checks at `0x020004E0` and eToolkit+0x78 | One count per key item id; `CheckKeyItem` returns 0 unless byte id of the first check XOR 0x55 equals byte id of the second, which the game sets when it gives the item. ScrtData is key item 0x31 (the key item name archive); a blue Mystery Data with content `[4, 0x20, 0xFF, 0xFF, 0x31]` gives one, and the Secret Area gate checks three with `ts_check_item07`. |
| The sound's mix (`src/audio/sound_filter.c`, settings.ini's `sound`) | MP2K's SoundInfo `0x030045C0` (`BN6_SOUND_INFO`): +0x08 rate index 3, +0x10 176 samples a frame, +0x14 10512 Hz (`BN6_SOUND_PCM_FREQ`); timer 0's reload `0xF9C4` | Read in romlab 900 frames after power-on (23 October 2026): the BIOS's sound pointer `0x03007FF0` reads `0x030045C0`, whose first word is MP2K's ident "Smsh" (`0x68736D53`); its rate byte 3 is MP2K's 10512 Hz, the samples a frame 0xB0 (176 x 59.7275 = 10512) and the rate word 0x2910, and timer 0, which paces the sound's DMA, reloads at 0xF9C4 (1596 cycles a sample: 16777216 / 1596 = 10512). BN6's music and sounds are mixed at that rate and the GBA holds each sample to the next, so above 5256 Hz the mix holds only its images (a capture's highs some 20 dB under the music up to 22 kHz). Verify: romlab `run 900`, `peek32 03007FF0`, `peek32 030045D4` (`00002910`), `peek32 04000100` (`0080f9c4`). |
| BN6's sound engine, MP2K (Nintendo's "m4a"), which the engine's own player follows (`src/audio/mp2k*.c`, `gba_sound.c`; docs/EMULATION.md, The engine's own sound) | RAM: SoundInfo `BN6_SOUND_INFO`, its DirectSound channels `BN6_SOUND_CHANS` (+0x50, 4 of 0x40), the PSG's `BN6_SOUND_CGB_CHANS` `0x03005600` (4 of 0x40), the command table `BN6_SOUND_JUMP_TABLE` `0x03005570`. ROM: `BN6_M4A_JUMP_TEMPLATE` `0x1C1F68`, `BN6_M4A_SCALE_TABLE` `0x1C1FF8`, `BN6_M4A_FREQ_TABLE` `0x1C20AC`, `BN6_M4A_CGB_SCALE_TABLE` `0x1C20F4`, `BN6_M4A_CGB_FREQ_TABLE` `0x1C2178`, `BN6_M4A_NOISE_TABLE` `0x1C2190`, `BN6_M4A_CGB3_VOL_TABLE` `0x1C21CC`, `BN6_M4A_CLOCK_TABLE` `0x1C21DC`, `BN6_M4A_XCMD_TABLE` `0x1C2210`; code: `BN6_M4A_SOUND_MAIN` `0x0814F754`, `BN6_M4A_MPLAY_MAIN` `0x0814FDB0`, `BN6_M4A_PLY_NOTE` `0x081500A8`, `BN6_M4A_SONG_START` `0x08150474`, `BN6_M4A_CGB_SOUND` `0x08151058`, and `BN6_M4A_SOUND_MODE` `0x0800059C`, the word BN6's start gives m4aSoundMode | The routines are bn6f's (asm/libs.s, Falzar) located in Gregar by the full map (build.py symbols --bn6f; the library sits 0x1B50 past Falzar's), their logic read from that disassembly (Nintendo's standard MP2K, line for line). SoundInfo's own words name the RAM (its MPlayMainHead, CgbSound, MidiKeyToCgbFreq, MPlayJumpTable pointers, romlab 900 frames after power-on). The tables are each routine's literal pool read with capstone in Gregar: m4aSongNumStart (`0x08150478`) loads BN6_MPLAYS and the song table; ply_note the clock table; MidiKeyToFreq (`0x08150344`) the scale and frequency tables; MidiKeyToCgbFreq (`0x08150EF4`) the noise, PSG scale and PSG frequency tables; CgbSound the wave channel's volume table; the template's 36 words are the commands' routines (0xB6-0xB9, 0xC6, 0xC7, 0xC9-0xCB and 0xCD ply_fine; MPlayExtender puts MEMACC at 0xB9 and XCMD at 0xCD, whose twelve sub-commands the XCMD table holds). The mode word 0x0093040D sets reverb 13 (any reverb bits do), 4 channels and rate 3. Verify: `python3 tools/mp2k_verify.py` (the ROM and the romlab image): the plain ROM with its title's song pointed at each song, laid beside the engine's --mp2k-trace frame by frame (tools/mp2k_diff.py), every part the same for 900 frames of the title, the credits, the net and seven of the menus' sounds. |
| Map music (`src/map/mapslot.c`) | `0x0360E4` (3 chapter lists), GameState+0x0F BGMusicIndicator, music player `0x02010890` | `playCertainMapMusicBasedOnEventByte` (`0x08036E44`) picks the list by GameState+7 and finds (group, per-map song bytes) entries of 8 bytes ending 0xFF; a missing group plays 0x63 (none). At the boot state's chapter only Central has a song, so the engine points every list at one holding the layer's map. `PlayMusic` skips the song in BGMusicIndicator, so a warp clears it while the music player's status has bit 31 (stopped). Area themes: Central 0x13, Seaside 0x11, Sky 0x0A, Green 0x12, Graveyard 0x09, Undernet 0x14, Undernet Zero 0x20, Underground 0x21. The table has a pointer per chapter byte, most of them null: the later chapters' lists start at index 0x10, 0x20, 0x30 and 0x40 (bn6f `off_80360E4`), and there the towns' first maps play Seaside Town 0x06, Green Town 0x08, Sky Town 0x07 (0x0B and 0x0C in their chapters of alarm); read with the ROM's list entries (group, pointer to a byte per map). |
| The Net Square's music (`src/online/online_square.c`) | song `0x1E`, played at `0x08137590` | BN6's Navi Customizer theme, the owner's pick for the Square in whatever area it stands. Located by bn6f's `SONG_NAVI_CUSTOMIZER` (`constants/enums/SoundOffsets.inc`, 0x1E), played by Falzar's NaviCust screen code (`sub_81356F4`); in Gregar the only `mov r0,#imm; bl PlayMusic (0x080005D4); pop {pc}` with 0x1E is at `0x08137590`, in the NaviCust's screen code. Verify: `--render-song 0x1E:30:PATH` is the NaviCust's theme; open the NaviCust and GameState+0x0F reads 0x1E. |
| Run locks (`src/director/director_layer.c` lock_run) | event flags `0x1727`, `0x1706` | The jack routine (`0x08034CB6`) returns before jacking in or out while `0x1727` is set; `0x1706` (EVENT_PET_COMM_SAVE_DISABLED) makes the PET's Comm and Save buzz (`SOUND_CANT_JACK_IN`). Both are set on every layer and after CONTINUE. |
| Crosses and Beast Out (`src/director/powers.c`) | event flags `0xE0`, `0xE2`-`0xE6` | Found by setting ranges of event flags before a forced battle and halving them until CROSSSELECT appeared: `0xE2` HeatCross, `0xE3` ElecCross, `0xE4` SlashCross, `0xE5` EraseCross, `0xE6` ChargeCross. `0xE0` adds Beast Out (the emblem under OK and its counter); `sub_801D814` (`0x0801D814`) reads it, and `0x163` switches it off again. Set as the endless Nest's Cybeast falls (issue #109), it holds for the rest of the run: verify with `--scene emu --run-depth 20 --seed 7 --dev quiet --input "0:battle"`, a start past that Nest, whose first battle shows the emblem. Set as a run begins with the BeastOut helper (docs/META.md, issue #99), it holds from the first battle: verify with `--scene emu --seed 7 --setup short,0,0,16 --input "0:battle"`, whose first battle on layer 1 shows the emblem and the counter's 3 (with `short,0,0,0`, neither), and RIGHT five times, DOWN and A on the emblem, then UP and A, turn MegaMan into Gregar's beast for the fight, the counter at 2. |
| The Library (`src/director/director_folder.c` library_to_game, `src/core/meta.c`) | event flags `0x1E20` + chip id (`BN6_FLAG_LIBRARY`) | A chip is in BN6's Library when its flag is set: the Chip Trader's prize routine reads them (bn6f `sub_804BD00`, the Chip Traders row), and the PET's LIBRARY screen counts and lists them. Found from that routine; checked by listing the set flags at a new run's first layer (exactly the Standard folder's twelve chips: 1, 4, 5, 54, 71, 72, 89, 154, 163, 175, 184, 192, which the boot's NEW GAME gave), and by setting a profile's chips at a run's start and reading the PET's LIBRARY (StdChip 15/200, the Blade folder's LongSwrd, PanlGrab and Barrier among them). The engine's starting folders set their chips' flags as `GiveFolder` does. |
