| Navi overworld sprites (`src/layer/guardians.c`, `guardian_body`) | sprite list 6: `0x47` HeatMan, `0x49` ElecMan, `0x4B` SlashMan, `0x50` EraseMan, `0x4F` ChargeMan, `0x3B` ProtoMan, `0x51` BlastMan, `0x52` DiveMan, `0x53` Colonel, `0x54` CircusMan, `0x55` JudgeMan, `0x56` ElementMan; sprite list 0, `0x2E` + the guardian's navi number: the battle sprites | Rendered with `--sheet @6:0x38:40` and matched to the battle sprites (list 0, `0x2F`-`0x3E` HeatMan to ElementMan, `0x40` Colonel; `--sheet 0:IDX:ANIM:PATH` one each). BlastMan's and ElementMan's overworld sprites draw only animations 2-5 (right, down-right, down, down-left) and 25 (logging in); 0, 1, 6, 7 and their walks are empty (`--sheet 6:0x51:A:PATH`). The Falzar Navis' list-6 entries point at the placeholder dot, so they stand in their battle sprites, which the NPC command `ns_set_sprite_with_category` (0x25, index then list x 4) takes from list 0 as from list 6: their entries `0x34`-`0x38` are plain sprites (no bit 31), animation 0 standing and 3 BN6's warp-in (vertical lines into the Navi), facing left; `ns_toggle_sprite_mirror` (0x26) turns one right. Verify: `--guardian 6 --run-depth 3 --net-biome 0 --seed 3`, MegaMan placed at -229 219 and walked DOWN+RIGHT into the arena: SpoutMan warps in and stands in his battle sprite; `--net-biome 2` at -96 206, DOWN+LEFT: TenguMan faces right. A guardian's NPC script leaves with `ns_jump_if_flag_set` (0x04) to `ns_free_and_end` once the engine sets its flag. |
| Warp pads (`src/map/coords.c`, `mapslot.c`) | coordinate data section 3, warp lists per group (first `ldr r0, [pc]` literal of each EnterMapGroup, e.g. `0x08072410` for Central), `0x02011BB0` Warp2011bb0, event flags `0x16F0`+n | Section 3 has the walls' format; a pad is 3x3 cells with shape `[z, warp index, height, type]`, types 9-C at the corners and 0x11 elsewhere (Central Area 1's own pads). A layer's pad has every cell over its panel (5x5 round a centre in a cell's middle) and a row more towards the screen's foot, only its top corner cut: the originals' 3x3 reaches 12 units, and its cut corners are the ring's top, bottom and sides in the isometric view, where MegaMan stood on the rim without leaving; with the side corners cut as well a playtester walking right onto a pad stood on its left rim. Checked by placing MegaMan in the left corner cell of a layer's exit pad: he warps. `0x080058D0` checks the trigger under MegaMan each frame on the map: index 1-15 unless flag `0x16F0`+index is set, then sets Warp2011bb0 +0x10 to 1 and +0x11 to the index and runs the entry's departure (byte 2 of the 16-byte WarpData: group, number, departure, facing, x, y, z; 8 is the jack-out cutscene). The game clears the `0x16F0` flags when it enters a map. The pads as drawn (`src/map/pad_art.c`, session 75): a layer's exit pad (sprite list 7's `0x22`) and a way back's (`0x81`, the Net Square's pad home too) are a ring 56 by 30 pixels round their place (drawn alone with `--sheet 7:0x22:0:PATH`, x -28 to 27 and y -16 to 14 from the anchor; seen over a layer, its middle at the pad's place within a pixel), BN6's link marker (`0x88`) a diamond 56 by 28; on the screen a world step (dx, dy) goes dx + dy across and (dy - dx) / 2 down. The trigger cells reach past the ring at its sides, but a playtester walking right onto an exit pad stopped with his feet 23 pixels left of its middle and 22 below it (world 338, -57 against the pad's 372, -68), his sprite over the ring's left half, 150 frames without a warp; 8 frames further (346, -57) BN6's cells fired. So the director takes the pad's warp as its trigger does (`emu_warp_link(1)`, `exit_pad_step`) while MegaMan stands still with his feet on the ring, 6 pixels of shoe and sprite to either side, or in front of it as far as his sprite covers it (13 pixels down, the 14 and 12 world units Lan's HP's portals were stretched by in session 73), and the pad is open. Verify: `build.py shot --scene emu --seed 4034483 --frames 940 --input "$(python3 -c "print('400:,' + '4:A,6:,' * 30 + '60:,0:place -129 404 1,4:,22:RIGHT,150:')")" --shot 790:/src/.build/rim.bmp,935:/src/.build/next.bmp` (a fresh `--data-dir`): MegaMan walks onto the exit pad's left rim (-119, 414; the pad at -84, 404) and stops, and frame 935 is layer 2; v0.12.0 leaves him standing there. |
| Arrow panels (`src/map/props.c`, `src/map/netmap_extra.c`, issue #43) | section-3 values `0x48`-`0x4B` (start) and `0x4C`-`0x4F` (end) of the coordinate data | A start cell 0x48 + d carries MegaMan towards way d (0 +X, 1 +Y, 2 -X, 3 -Y), input held, 4 units a frame, until he crosses an end cell 0x4C + d; each cell height 8 at the floor's z, its shape fixed by the way (+X 0x13, +Y 0x15, -X 0x12, -Y 0x14). BN6 rings an arrow region: start cells on every edge but its exit side, end cells along that side, each centred on the edge, nothing inside; a lane a panel wide (Robot Control Comp 1, Green Area 1) has three of each across its near and far edges, between wall corners. Walked onto from the exit, the start cells carry MegaMan back. The arrows are floor tiles of each area's own, no objects. Found by decoding the maps' coordinate data (values 0x48-0x4F in Seaside 1-3, Green 1-2, Underground 2, Sky 2-3, Undernet 1 and 3, Robot Control 1, Judge Tree 1-3 and the Pavilion; Seaside Area 2's field all way 2, ends on its -X side) and drawing them; the ride seen in the game on generated lanes. Verify: `--dev pieces=32` at depth 2, seed 3 in the Undernet (`--net-biome 5`): the state's `lane` line (`CYBERWORLD_STATE_POS=map`) gives its ends; walked on from its entry, MegaMan is carried across to the far floor, and walked back from there, carried back. |
| Net maps (`src/map/area_src.c`, `netmap.c`) | `0x0329C4` map descriptors, `0x03354C` coordinate data, `RomLayout.net_area[]` | One pointer per map group (0x80 first) to 12-byte descriptors per map: a tile set (two headers of word count, offset to LZ77 data and VRAM offset), a palette (a 4-byte size, then 16 banks) and a tile map (width, height, 0, 0, u32 12, u32 offset of layer 2 in the decompressed data, then LZ77). Coordinate data is a 16-byte header of four section offsets and LZ77; section 0 lists wall cells (8x8 world units, key (y+127)*254+(x+127)) with 4-byte shapes (lowest z, flag, height, type 1-8). Each biome learns its tiles from one map (Central 0x90:0, Seaside 0x91:0, Sky 0x94:1, Green 0x92:0, Graveyard 0x96:1, Undernet 0x95:0, Undernet Zero 0x95:1, Underground 0x93:1, and from the computer and homepage groups a comp 0x8C:0, a homepage 0x88:3 and a comp 0x8C:1). Groups 0x80-0x85 are the story's comps (Robot Control 0x80, Aquarium 0x81, Judge Tree 0x82, Mr. Weather 0x83, Pavilion 0x85:0-3 in the other four's tiles, CopyBot 0x85:4); 0x88 holds Lan's (0), ACDC (1), Aquarium (3), Green (5) and Sky (6) homepages. The groups' descriptor tables run into each other (0x81's starts at 0x80's entry 2). Maps of a group share one tile set and palette, so an area also learns from its other maps (`net_area.more`), and each map at each floor height with nine panels or more (section 1 heights, type 0x11): a copy drawn that many pixels lower shows the raised floor where ground floor would be. CopyBot's comp (0x85:4) cannot be split by hue (its walkways' teal discs share the purple plateaus' bucket, their white panels the rims'), so it is learned by shape (`TILES_BY_SHAPE`): platform floor lies in 2x2 blocks with floor all around, walkways and rims are the other floor; its plateaus stand at height 32 on ornamental pods (faces of 43), reached by ladders (ramp cells, type 0x14) from walkways at 0; its tall spikes stand on the second tile layer in front of the plateaus' faces (their tips over the rims at the front corners), with its claws and ladders. Aquarium Comp's glass pads stand at height 16 on legs: stairs of ramp cells (types 0x13 and 0x14, rising 4 to 16 from its water at 0; section 1 of 0x81:2) lead up to them, drawn on the second tile layer, and its glass walkways meet their rims at 16; nothing at 0 joins a pad flush (`net_area.pads_apart`). The game shows a net map's second tile layer in front of its first, as in the real world: on every area's layers BG1CNT reads 0x1D03 (the first layer, priority 3) and BG2CNT 0x3E02 (the second, priority 2); read with `emu_read16` at 0x0400000A and 0x0400000C on a generated layer. Section 0 lists only walls (types 1-12); the walls ring every floor and again every hole in it, so a panel lies on floor when it is inside an odd number of rings. Battle backgrounds by id: 0x00 ACDC HP, 0x01 Green HP, 0x03 Aquarium HP, 0x04 sky, 0x05 Judge Tree, 0x06 comp grid, 0x07/0x09 Central, 0x08 lab, 0x0A Aquarium Comp, 0x0B Seaside, 0x0C/0x0D Green, 0x0E blue comp, 0x0F/0x11 Undernet, 0x10 Mr. Weather, 0x12/0x14 Graveyard, 0x13 Secret, 0x15 Nest; 0x02 is blank. A random battle's record leaves its background at 0xFF (the map's). Groups 0x8C and 0x8D (16 maps each) are the real world's computers, mostly two layouts copied; 0x88 holds homepages in four colour pairs. Their random battles give WindBox and most families; they play song 0x13, and the engine gives them battle backgrounds 0x06, 0x03 and 0x08. Of the 22 backgrounds, the surface areas and the Graveyard and Undernet alternate between two (Central 0x07/0x09, Seaside 0x0B/0x0A, Sky 0x04/0x10, Green 0x0D/0x0C, Graveyard 0x14/0x12, Undernet 0x0F/0x11). Overworld sprite list 6 has CircusMan at 0x54 and Colonel at 0x53 (navi 14 and 18): tiles are classed by their place in a 32x32 panel and the floor around it, keeping panels whose middle and four points around it are drawn (the computers' platforms hang legs into the gaps) and whose middle has the listed hue (`styles`, 12 buckets plus grey); walkways learn from the panels of a second hue (`walk_styles`: Central 6, Seaside 1-2, Sky 6 and 8, Green 0, Graveyard 8, Undernet 10-11 and 0: its striped bridges turn and end in joints with a yellow gem, in bucket 0, and every bridge meets a plateau through one; learned as floor of another style, no tile beside them was, and a generated bridge's turns and ends had none to take. Found with the atlas's text of the original's panels, `src_bXX_GG_N.txt`, which prints other styles by bucket. The stripes' middles turn magenta and rose by turns, and a joint's is red too, so a joint is told by its gem: four pixels or more of its top in buckets 1-2 (`joint_hues`), which mark every end, turn, T and crossing of Undernet 1's bridges and no stripe; its tiles are kept in a look of their own, as a pad's, and a generated bridge's ends, turns and crossings take it). Undernet-style maps draw their static in the second tile layer, so only the first marks floor there and their second-layer entries are not copied (`bg_in_map`). Elsewhere the originals set on the second layer what stands in front of a floor: Seaside Area 1's boardwalks where they cross its blue platform, Central Area 2's bridge over its green floor, the red ornaments on Green's and Central's pads, CopyBot's spikes and claws, the corner of a platform where it overlaps another on screen; and some floors, the Judge Tree's panel middles (its first layer draws only their frames) and Seaside Areas 2 and 3's blue platforms. Checked by drawing each layer alone (`area_src_render` with the other layer cleared) and both as the game shows them; `tiles.c` learns a tile's first layer alone where it draws the floor, and its second too only where the floor needs it. Judge Tree Comp 3's courts (0x82:2, and 0x85:1 in the same tiles) are flat red panels in hue bucket 0, its brick panels bucket 1: they count as floor but their tiles are left out (`net_area.skip_styles`). Robot Control Comp 2's (0x80:1) walls also ring the grey cubes that stand on its white platforms, map objects: rings inside the floor, around panels the map draws whole (on the white platforms in the same tile entries as the platforms' middles), which `tiles.c` learns as floor (`TILES_INNER_WALLS`); found by marking section 0's walls on the drawn map, and seen in the game by warping into 0x80:1, where the cubes stand in them. Its platforms' rims, two light bands, reach 16 pixels under their upper edges (`TILES_RIMMED`), and its one small platform, 3x4 panels before the robot's door, is a striped conveyor (`TILES_MORE_PADS`). The small platforms the Judge Tree's maps join by bridges are round tree stumps, Sky's round pods; a square pad takes neither look (`TILES_NO_PAD_LOOK`). Every map draws its floor 3-4 pixels below the panel edges its walls give, with side faces about 9 pixels tall under the bottom edges; the computers and homepages also hang a pedestal about 18 pixels deep under each panel's lower corner. `tiles.c` measures all three per map and tests each map's tiles with its own faces and legs (Robot Control's white platforms stand on 17-pixel faces, its circuit walkways on 5) but with the floor where the first map draws it: Sky's raised views measure their floor 0 pixels down, and their tiles left steps along the edges. A generated map also avoids setting side by side tiles no original map does (`seams.c`, `tilemap.c`), and tiles whose floor stops on a tile's own edge above or below an empty one. |
| Another game's maps: Battle Network 5 Team Colonel (USA) (`src/core/rom.c` xlayouts, `src/map/area_src.c`, docs/MULTIROM.md) | BN5 `0x0331B4` map descriptor lists (real-world groups 0x00-0x05), `0x0331CC` (internet groups 0x80-0x94), `0x033FAC` and `0x033FC4` coordinate data lists | BN5's maps are in BN6's formats: a pointer per map group to 12-byte MapBGDescriptors (tile set, palette, a tile map with its 12-byte header and LZ77), coordinate data with a 16-byte header of four section offsets and LZ77. Its tables follow each other as BN6's do, six real-world groups before the internet's (BN6 has seven), and the internet groups 0x85, 0x87, 0x89, 0x8B and 0x8F are empty in both. Found by their structure with `tools/rom_tables.py`, which finds BN6's own at 0x0329A8 and 0x033530 the same way; the header code is BRKE, the SHA-1 5f472f78d8de2df01d5039e045c043cb40969a39. Verify: with the ROM beside BN6's, `--atlas DIR:x0` draws its maps, ACDC Town at 0x00:0, Lan's room at 0x01:0, the harbour at 0x03:0 and the internet's from 0x80:0, with their walls, floor heights and triggers counted. Its whole internet is group 0x90, 16 maps in two tile sets (maps 0-9 and 10-15) and a palette per look, as BN6's surface areas share theirs. Its ACDC Area 1 (0x90:0) is another game's area 0, learned with ACDC Area 2 (0x90:1, the same tiles and colours): cyan platforms framed by rims (hue bucket 6, `TILES_RIMMED`), and green walkways (bucket 4) with the pale pieces that join them to the platforms (5); over nine atlas layers that gave 1821 seams and no tile drawing another floor, where platforms alone and green walkways gave 3386 and 747; its tile set's two blocks load at VRAM 0 and 0x56C0 (BN6's Central Area 1's at 0 and 0x5E60), 22208 and 8992 bytes, and its palette holds 13 banks (a size word of 0x1A0) where BN6's net maps hold 16. Group 0x90's maps are, by BN5's own map labels (the archive at `0x6E3F00`, its names in BN6's charset): 0-1 ACDC Area 1-2, 2-3 Oran Area 1-2, 4-6 SciLab 1, 2 and 4, 7-9 End Area 1, 3 and 4, 10-12 Undernet 1-3, 13-15 Nebula Area 2, 4 and 6 (the rest in group 0x93). A layer in another game's tiles must take over a map whose group keeps the palette its descriptor names: the Undernet's (0x95), the Graveyard's (0x96) and the Underground's (0x93) set their own over it (Nebula Area's floors drew in their colours under each; under Central, Seaside, Sky, Green, a comp and a homepage they kept BN5's). Verify with `--atlas DIR:a0:1` (`src_b19_90_0.txt` prints its panels by hue) and a capture with `--net-biome x0` to `x5`: its platforms in the game, in their colours. Its palettes run their banks alike: in a bank, colours 1-9 are a floor's ramp and 10-15 its rim's, and a surface drawn in two banks is one floor in two shades. Oran Area (0x90:2-3, BN5's 0x91:0-1, one palette) draws its orange octagon hubs and brown planks in bank 1 and its olive circuits in bank 2, both with a mauve-grey rim, and its first map's raised cream platforms and green walkways in banks 3 and 4 with a cyan rim, its mint ones in bank 5: the area reads 3 as 1, 4 and 5 as 2 before it learns (`recolour` 0x524231, read by `area_src_load_as`: the map's entries rewritten, the map drawn again). Its Undernet (0x90:11-12, BN5's 0x94:1-2) draws its plates and cobbles in bank 1 (mauve stone, lava) and its raised courts in bank 2 (pale stone, purple slime), read as bank 1 (0x21); its Undernet 1 (0x90:10) has a palette of its own, four zones of other stones in banks 1-4, and lends nothing. Oran Area's and SciLab's maps (0x90:4-6) hold no platform bigger than a 3 x 3 octagon hub with ears at its corners. Verify: with BN5 beside BN6, `--atlas DIR:a4:1` (`src_b23_90_2.txt` and `src_b23_90_2_z64.txt`, its raised view, print its panels: the raised platforms as hub floor) and `--atlas DIR:a5:1` (`src_b24_90_11.bmp`: the raised court in the plates' shades). |
| Another game's songs: Battle Network 5 Team Colonel (USA) (`src/core/rom.c` xlayouts, `src/audio/xsong.c`, docs/MULTIROM.md; the Jukebox's, `src/audio/mp2k.c` `mp2k_song_start_in`, docs/PET.md) | BN5 `0x155BF4` MP2K song table; `0x0361C8` its map-music lists; `0x0814C9FC` its MP2K library, `0x08000568` its sound mode word | The song table is the literal BN5's `m4aSongNumStart` (`0x0814D71C`, the same 22 bytes of code as BN6's at `0x08150478`) loads into r1, beside its player table (`0x08155A74`, 32 entries, player 31 music with 8 tracks, as BN6's); its music is 0x01-0x24 on player 31 and 0x25 on is empty, as BN6's. The map-music lists, one per chapter, are found as BN6's (`0x080360E4`) are read by its `playCertainMapMusicBasedOnEventByte`: entries of (group, three 0xFF, a pointer to a song byte per map), ending 0xFF; a pointer table at `0x0361C8` holds them (the first at `0x036388`). The four lists (picked by GameState+7, not the chapter) name groups 0x00 (ACDC Town: 0x03, its maps 1-6 0x04, 7 0x05, 8-11 0x0C; the third list 0x0C throughout), 0x01 (0x05, 0x0C), 0x90 (ACDC Area 1-2: 0x13), 0x94 (Undernet 1-3, Nebula Area 2, 4, 6: 0x14), 0x86 (ACDC Area 3, Oran Area 3, SciLab 3, End Area 2 and 5: 0x13; Undernet 4, Nebula Area 1, 3, 5: 0x14), 0x80 (its first comp: 0x10), 0x88 (homepages) and 0x8C-0x8E (object comps: 0x13): the themes its areas take along. Groups 0x91-0x93 (Oran Area 1-2, SciLab 1, 2, 4, End Area 1, 3, 4) are in none, so their entry sets song 0x63, which its PlayMusic (`0x080005A0`, BN6's at `0x080005D4`) turns into m4aMPlayAllStop (`0x0814D850`); verified in romlab by warping into each net map (BN5's warp routine, from a state in ACDC Area 1, story progress poked from 0x00 to 0x70): GameState+0x0F (its BGMusicIndicator) reads 0x13, 0x14, 0x10 or 0x63 as above, and the music player's MusicPlayerInfo (`0x02011470`, player 31) plays song 0x13's header `0x081689D4` in ACDC Area, 0x14's `0x08169954` in Nebula Area 2, and stands paused (status bit 31) in Oran Area 1 and End Area 1. Song 0x13 is 6 tracks and 5768 bytes of sequence, 0x14 6 tracks and 3936, both on voice group `0x081547A8`; their voices take 192 KB and 8 KB more (one drum kit's samples are most of it, shared). Verify: `--net-biome x0` with `CYBERWORLD_EMU_DEBUG=1` prints "xsong: song 0x13 ... in slot 0x26" and the music player's song in that space ("music t60 song 089cef4c"); its sound dumped (`CYBERWORLD_AUDIO_DUMP`) does not correlate with BN6's net theme on a Central layer (`--net-biome 0 --seed 2`). Its battles name their music as BN6's do, in byte 2 of their BattleSettings (bn6f `battle_settings_struct`): scanning the ROM for 16-byte records whose last word points at an entity list (MegaMan's entry first, 4-byte entries, 0xF0 at the end), 32 of BN5's random battle records read 0x15 and its boss records 0x16, BN6's own two numbers (BN6's records read 0x15). Song 0x15 is 4.5 KB with what its voices add, 0x16 5.8 KB (the samples shared with 0x13). Verify: with BN5 beside BN6, `CYBERWORLD_EMU_DEBUG=1` prints "song 0x15 ... in slot 0x28" and "0x16 ... in slot 0x29", and a random battle on `--net-biome x0 --seed 2` (the autopilot finds one by frame 2400) turns the music player's song to `089d1f68`, the end of 0x15's copy. Its MP2K library is BN6's: from SoundMain (BN5 `0x0814C9FC`, BN6 `0x0814F754`) the next 0x2104 bytes are the same but for 24 literal words (the scale `0x081BC74C`, frequency `0x081BC800`, PSG scale `0x081BC848`, PSG frequency `0x081BC8CC`, noise `0x081BC8E4`, wave volume `0x081BC920` and clock `0x081BC930` tables, the jump template `0x081BC6BC`, the player table, the song table, a RAM address, `0x02011770` for BN6's `0x02010B90`) and 35 pointers into the block, each moved by the block's own move (0x2D58); the seven tables equal BN6's byte for byte, and its m4aSoundMode word is BN6's `0x0093040D`, at `0x08000568` (BN6's `0x0800059C`). So the engine plays BN5's songs on its BN6 code and tables, reading the song, its voices and samples from BN5's ROM (a player's and a channel's ROM, `mp2k_read`). Verify: the block compared word for word, every difference a pointer moved by 0x2D58 or one of the literals above (`python3` over both ROMs, docs/SOURCES.md, The Jukebox's songs); `--render-song x0/0x01:30:PATH` against BN5's title recorded in romlab (`audio 1800`), envelopes at 0.90, bands within 0.7 dB. |
| Another game's Navis: Battle Network 5 Team Colonel (USA) (`src/core/rom.c` xlayouts, `src/layer/xnavi.c`, docs/MULTIROM.md) | BN5 `0x03272C` sprite lists; Gregar's list 6 and mugshots at 72, 74 and 76-78 | BN5's ten sprite lists are found as BN6's at `0x031CC4` are: ten pointers, the first pointing just past the list. Its list 6 holds its overworld Navis, its list 8 their mugshots, each mugshot at its Navi's number as in BN6: 60-61 the purple HeelNavi, 55-59 a green NormalNavi, 62-63 an orange Navi, plain sprites (no LZ77) of 45 to 53 animations, their frames' offsets all from the sprite's own start; the HeelNavi's takes 19127 bytes and its face 1278, which a walk of its frames finds, the gap to the next sprite agreeing within alignment. Gregar's list 6 points numbers 48, 49, 53, 72, 74, 76-78 and 89 at one placeholder sprite (`0x084DC040`) and its mugshots there at bn6f `mugshotBlack` (`0x084F6BBC`): Falzar's Navis' numbers, which no guardian takes (`guardian_sprite`); a Navi copied in takes one of 72, 74, 76-78 in both, so its face keeps its sprite's number, the convention `ta_say` speaks by. Its orange Navi (62) is a standing figure, 45 animations of one frame each, 4936 bytes (the next sprite starts 4940 on) and its face 1352; it stands among the bystanders of BN5's ACDC, Oran, SciLab and End Areas (none in Nebula Area, whose are HeelNavis), and is copied at Gregar's 74 for ACDC Area's and End Area's, taking turns with the HeelNavi. BN5's Net Dealers are its green NormalNavi (55-59, one sprite at `0x4151F4`; their shops' scripts show mugshot 0x37: ACDC Area 2, Oran Area 1, End Area 1), whose art BN6's GreenNavi (62, the Net Dealer's keeper here) shares, and in Nebula Area 2 a HeelNavi; its Mr. Prog (53-54) is BN6's 60-61. Verify: `--net-biome x0` with `CYBERWORLD_EMU_DEBUG=1` prints "xnavi: navi 60 ... at 72" and "xnavi: navi 62 ... at 74"; a bystander there stands and turns in the HeelNavi's shape and speaks with its face, and at `--seed 1 --run-depth 2` "bystander 0: list 6 74 at 52 -116" is the orange Navi, who speaks with its own face (`--talk npc`). |
| BN5 guest battles: Battle Network 5 Team Colonel (USA) (`src/emu/guest.c`, the differential's reference since the guest core left runs in issue #82's last round; `src/core/xrecords.c`, `src/emu/bn5.h`, docs/MULTIROM.md) | BN5 `0x0200A440` eToolkit, `0x02002940` game state, `0x080054F6` StartBattle, `0x080053FC` the encounter check, `0x0810F6B4` the roll (`BN5_ROLL`), `0x0801C968` the battle record tables (`BN5_BATTLE_TABLES`) | BN5 shares BN6's engine: found by BN6's code shapes in BN5's ROM. eToolkit by its setup (`ldr r0,=eToolkit; mov r10,r0` at `0x08006012`, BN6's at `0x08006BCE`); its +0x3C is the game state, whose +0 sub-mode (4 the map, 8 a battle beginning, 0xC in battle), +4 map group, +0xC last map group and +0x1C battle record match BN6's. StartBattle by its body (`str r0,[r5,#0x1C]`, the 0x4000 test, `ldrh r0,[r5,#4]; strh r0,[r5,#0xC]`, the sub-mode set; an entry 6 bytes before sets 0x2C instead of 8); the check by its one call of it (`0x08005456`), test for test BN6's; the roll by the check's `bl` before the `beq` (`0x08005452`), on net maps only (map group 0x80 up). The roll reads `[[[BN5_BATTLE_TABLES + 4] + (group - 0x80) * 4] + number * 4]`: 16-byte BattleSettings records in BN6's format, byte 0 0xFF ending them, +7 a condition the roll tests (`0x0810F818`). The guest patches the roll to `ldr r0,=record; tst r0,r0; bx lr` with the record in the literal 8 bytes on, so the next map frame begins that battle, and the literal 0 again once it has. Verify: in `tools/romlab` with BN5's ROM, a state in Lan's room (title, START, NEW GAME, A through the intro), the record `00 36 15 00 00 00 38 00 E2 49 00 00` with an entity list `00 22 00 00 11 25 01 00 F0` and the patch: the next frame is a battle with a Mettaur (enemy id 1, 40 HP); in the game, a BN5 layer whose area names its records (`xbattles`) fights BN5's own formations (group 0x80 map 0: Champy and Mettaur on a yellow field). Its net areas' battles by its game's numbers: the internet map descriptors lie one after another from group 0x90's (0x90 → `0x080337F0`, 0x91 → `0x08033808`, 0x92 → `0x08033820`, 0x93 → `0x08033844`, 0x94 → `0x08033868`), so the engine's 0x90:2-15 are the next groups' maps; warped into with BN5's own warp routine and read by the label each draws: ACDC Area 1-2 0x90:0-1 (lists `0x08121178`, `0x0812123C`), Area 3 0x86:0 (`0x0811DD90`); SciLab 1, 2, 4 0x92:0-2, 3 0x86:2; End Area 1, 3, 4 0x93:0-2 (`0x08121D98`, `0x08121E5C`, `0x08121F40`), 2 and 5 0x86:3-4; Nebula Area 2, 4, 6 0x94:3-5, 1, 3, 5 0x86:6-8; a group pads its unused numbers with its map 0's list. For the run in its battles (`BN5_*` in `bn5.h`): MegaMan's NaviStats `0x020052A8` (eToolkit +0x74, 0x60 bytes, BN6's offsets: +0x2D the equipped folder, +0x3E base max HP, +0x40 HP, +0x42 max HP); the folders at eToolkit +0x48 (`0x02002DF4`, 30 u16 each, chip | code << 9, 0x3C apart), owned in eToolkit +0x80's marks (`0x02005CC4`: a chip's key at `0x02001440` + id XOR 0x81; an unmarked chip draws blank); its chip names' text archives at `0x736084` (ids 0-255) and `0x736AB8` (256 on; 424 ids, BN6's charset); the battle's result at `0x0200AEE8` (+1: 1 won, 2 lost, 4 escaped; +4 the reward, BN6's encoding: bits 14-15 0 a chip, 1 zenny, 2 HP restored on the results screen, `BN5_REWARD_HP`: with a Mettaur's row written all `0x8032` in romlab its screen said "HP+50", MegaMan's 50 HP became 100 in his NaviStats and in BattleState +0x34, and +4 read `0x8032` until the map was back; in the game a guest battle won at 10 of 100 HP showed HP+50 and came back at 60, "reward HP+50" in the run log); BattleState `0x02034A90` (+1 its phase: 8 the Custom screen, 0xC fighting, 0x10 over; +0x34 MegaMan's HP to copy back); its reward rows at `0x081100D4` (20 u16 per enemy id, 0x28 apart; its chooser `0x0810FB18` matches BN6's `0x080AC150` instruction for instruction); a record's options word (+8) bit 0x100 plays GAME OVER after a loss (GetBattleEffects `0x0802B3F2`; BN6's same rule at `0x0800A892`); its byte +4 is its background's number, 0xFF the map's (`BN5_RECORD_BACKDROP`, the routine at `0x08CAE8` and its byte per map, battle backgrounds below), which the guest's copy sets to the dressed area's (`NetAreaDef.xbg`): left at 0xFF, every guest battle stood in front of the room the guest's boot state stands in. Found and verified in romlab (`.build/bn5/findings.md`'s method: forced battles in each area's maps, the record and its enemies on screen; a folder written and shown on the Custom screen; a loss with bit 0x100 cleared ends on the map with result 2). Verify in the game: with BN5 beside BN6, `CYBERWORLD_AUTOPILOT=1 CYBERWORLD_EMU_DEBUG=1 build.py shot --scene emu --run-depth 2 --net-biome x0 --seed 2 --frames 12000 --dev hp=300` prints "114 of BN6's chips by name", a battle with "26 of the folder's 30 in" (the Standard folder's Cannons in BN5's art on its Custom screen), "won ..., HP 240" and MegaMan back at 240/300 HP with its reward (30 zenny). Its chips' records at `0x01E210` (`BN5_CHIP_RECORDS`, 0x2C each in BN6's ChipData layout, +0..3 the four codes: 0-25 A-Z, 26 *, 0xFF none), read by its getChip `0x0801DF0C`; a folder chip whose code is not among them draws as nothing on its Custom screen (it can be picked and does nothing), so each chip goes in with a code its record has (the same letter, else *, else its first). Verify: Cannon (id 1) holds A, B, C and *; Thunder (19) B, L, P and *; with the Storm folder, the first hand of a guest battle shows every chip with its icon and code. Its Custom gauge at `0x02035700` (`BN5_CUSTOM_GAUGE`, u16, full at 0x4000), stored by its SetCustGauge `0x0801A88E`, found by BN6's at `0x0801DFA2` byte for byte (its struct `0x020356E0` + 0x20 where BN6's is `0x02035280` + 0x20); an L or R pressed before it fills is kept as BN6's battles keep one. Verify: in a guest battle it climbs to 0x4000 and drops as the Custom screen opens; an R pressed with the gauge at about 85% opens the screen as it fills. Its enemy tables as BN6's (`BN5_ENEMY_IDS` `0x08014C8C`: version, type and AI per enemy id, 3 bytes; `BN5_ENEMY_STATS` `0x0800D17C`: per type, per AI, 6 bytes per version, u16 element << 12 | HP and u16 element << 12 | damage at +4), found by BN6's GetVerActorTyAndAIIdx (`0x080182B4`: `movs r1,#3; muls r1,r0; ldr r0,[pc,#4]; adds r0,r1; mov pc,lr`) byte for byte at `0x08014C7C`, and BN6's stats lookup (`0x0800F23C`, its table right after it) by its body at `0x0800D15E`, its table right after it too; a record's byte 7 names a story condition (2752 of its net records hold 0; those that don't include its roaming Navis at 1600-2000 HP). Verify: Mettaur (1) 40 HP, 10 a hit; Mettaur2 (2) 70, 40; ACDC Area 2's records (`0x0812123C`) total 80-160 HP. |
| BN5's text: Battle Network 5 Team Colonel (USA) (`src/core/rom.c` xrom_text_bn6 and xrom_text, `src/core/xchips.c`, `src/emu/xvirus.c` names_set; issue #82) | BN5's font `0x6CCD44`, BN6's `0x6B5A2C` (64 bytes a code: an 8x16 glyph, 4 bits a pixel, as `src/gfx/gfx.c` reads BN6's) | BN5 does not spell its text as BN6 does past letters and digits: BN6 put its five version marks ([RV] [BX] [EX] [SP] [FZ]) at 0x40-0x44 and moved the rest on. Found by the chip names' pairing (issue #82's chip research: BN5's "M-Cannon" read as "MCannon" with BN6's table) and checked glyph for glyph against both fonts, every code 0x00-0xE4: 0x00-0x3F the same (space, digits, letters, *; BN5's J three pixels of shade apart, the same letter); 0x40-0x92 and 0x94-0x9A BN6's five on; 0xA0-0xAD (but 0xA5) three on; 0x93 and 0x9C a dash (BN6's 0x98, whose 0xA1 is the same glyph; BN6's names spell theirs 0x98), 0x9D "!" (0xA2), 0x9E the SP mark (0x43), 0x9B, 0xA5, 0xB1 and 0xB4 the empty box (0xA0), 0xB2, 0xB3 and 0xB8 on the same; 0x9F a DS mark, 0xAE, 0xAF, 0xB0 (alpha, beta, omega) and 0xB5-0xB7 none of BN6's glyphs. `xrom_text_bn6` writes BN5's text in BN6's codes by that map, the DS mark as the letters D and S (so "Roll" and its DS mark read "RollDS", which pairs with no chip of BN6's), a glyph BN6 lacks left out; every BN5 string read (`xrom_text`) or copied into BN6's archives (a moved family's virus names) goes through it. Before it the pairing read BN5's dash as nothing, its "+" (0x99) as BN6's "x" and its SP mark as BN6's 0x9E: M-Cannon, Navi+20, ProtoMan SP and Colonel SP sat out guest battles though BN5 has them, and BN5's Roll DS was taken for BN6's Roll. Verify: `build.py test` (test_xtext and test_xchips, on made-up bytes); the guest's line as its boot ends names how many of BN6's chips pair by name (114 before, 118 with it: those four). |
| BN5's results screen, its second reward and BugFrags: Battle Network 5 Team Colonel (USA) (the guest battles' until issue #82's last round, their code gone with them; `src/emu/bn5.h`, docs/MULTIROM.md) | BN5 `0x0200AEEE` the screen's second reward (`BN5_REWARD_FIND`), `0x08028E48` the results screen's end giving both, `0x08028E6C` a results reward given, `0x08028858` a reward word read, `0x0802865A` the results screen's setup, `0x08028C06` the battle's results summed up, `0x0801D62C` the battlefield Mystery Data's finds (`BN5_FIND_ROWS`), `0x08006874` a Mystery Data set on the field (`BN5_ENTITY_FIND`), `0x080DB608` its find kept as a battle is won, `0x0803C384` BugFrags given to its save | BN5's results code is BN6's, routine for routine: `tools/bn6f_match.py` located bn6f's functions (made from Falzar) in BN5 by their bytes, and each was read in BN5's ROM (capstone, Thumb). A battle's start clears +4 and +6 (`0x0800671C`). BattleState +0x36 is the find of a green Mystery Data on the field: a record's entity of kind 2 (`20 yx TC 00`) appears as its battle begins where its byte 2 beats RNG % 15 (`0x08006874`, bn6f spawnMysteryData_8007424: from 0x10 up always), its find drawn then from row T (byte 2 >> 4) of `0x0801D62C`, eight u16 picked by RNG & 0xE (`0x080DB692`, bn6f sub_80DA9FE, the table's literal at `0x080DB6A8`); it breaks at any hit, and still on the field as the battle is won, `0x080DB608` (bn6f sub_80DA974) writes its find to BattleState +0x36 (`0x080DB616`) and shows GET! over it. Its random battles name rows 0-5, an area a row: ACDC Area's 0 (byte 2 0x05 on its maps 0x90:0-1, a Mystery Data in a third of those battles, 0x07 on 0x86:0, in 7 of 15), Oran Area's 1, SciLab's 2, End Area's 3, the Undernet's 4 and Nebula Area's 5 (0x15 to 0x57: every battle that names one): row 0 1000 zenny four times and 1 BugFrag four times; row 1 1500 zenny three times, 1 BugFrag three times, Tornado L, AirHoc S; row 2 2000 zenny, 2 BugFrags, WideBlde A, LongBlde E; row 3 2500 zenny, 2 BugFrags, SloGauge \*, AntiDmg M; rows 4 and 5 3000 zenny, 3 BugFrags, Snctuary S and GunDelS3 D, FullCust \* and JustcOne J (rows 6 and 7 3000 zenny alone; past them other data). | BN5's random numbers: Battle Network 5 Team Colonel (USA) (`src/emu/guest.c` rng_in, the differential's reference's; `src/emu/bn5.h`, docs/MULTIROM.md, issue #102) | BN5 `0x02001D40` its primary RNG word (`BN5_RNG_PRIMARY`), `0x02001C94` its secondary (`BN5_RNG_SECONDARY`), `0x08001488` SeedRNG, `0x08001490` GetRNG, `0x080014A6` GetPositiveSignedRNG, `0x080014C0` GetRNGSecondary, `0x080014D6` GetPositiveSignedRNGSecondary, `0x08004C98` its game state's routine, `0x080098D8` the primary kept as a battle begins, `0x08009A0A` put back after its opening, `0x08008D82` the folder's shuffle, `0x08000C32` ShuffleHwordList_SecondaryRNG; BN6 `0x020013F0` and `0x02001120` | BN6's two words are bn6f's ePrimaryRngSeed and eSecondaryRngSeed (its `ewram.s`), at the same addresses in Gregar: the literals of its GetRNG `0x0800151C` and GetRNGSecondary `0x0800154C`, found by their bytes. BN5's routines are BN6's byte for byte: searched for in its ROM by their bodies (`lsls r2,r0,#1; lsrs r3,r0,#0x1f; adds r0,r2,r3; adds r0,#1; eors r0,r1; str r0,[r7]`) and their literal `0x873CA9E5` (`BN5_RNG_XOR`), GetRNG and GetPositiveSignedRNG load `0x02001D40`, the secondary pair `0x02001C94`, and SeedRNG writes `0xA338244F` to the first as the game starts (called at `0x080002C0`). Each step is `x = ((x rotl 1) + 1) ^ 0x873CA9E5`, the positive ones then clearing bit 31. Its game state's routine (bn6f cbGameState) calls GetRNG and GetRNGSecondary once a frame on a map and in battle (`0x08004CA8`, `0x08004CAC`), the main loop GetRNGSecondary once more (`0x0800031C`). As a battle begins, `0x080098D8` (bn6f sub_800B144, called from `0x08006D92`) keeps the primary at `0x0203C734`, and after the opening `0x08009A0A` puts it back from `0x0203EF14` (bn6f's link battles' shared seed; a battle of one's own): its rolls start from the primary's value as it began, the green Mystery Data's (`0x08006874`, RNG % 15) and its find's (`0x080DB692`, RNG & 0xE, by its call at `0x080DB69A`), the reward chooser's (`0x0810FB18`) and the viruses'. The folder is shuffled as the battle begins (`0x08008D82`, bn6f sub_800A570, by ShuffleHwordList_SecondaryRNG `0x08000C32`) from the secondary: the first hand. The guest's core began each session from its boot state, so every run's first battle in its engine drew alike: the same first hand on seeds 1-4, Cannon A, Sword S, \*, B, B. Each guest battle now writes both words from its seed on the frame BN5 begins it (`rng_in`, at the sub-mode's 8), whatever frames its map took before: a random battle's from the layer's seed and its battles so far (the record's pick's), a guardian's from the layer's seed; each word mixed (murmur3's finaliser) and kept off the step's shortest cycles, where every roll would come out alike: counted over all 2^32 states, the step parts them into 21 cycles, two of one state (`0x7D14675C`, `0x82EB98A1`) and one of nine (from `0x0FAD3BF3`), then 983 and up, SeedRNG's of 2,794,329,874; those eleven, their lowest bit flipped, lie on cycles of 170 million and more. Found and verified in `tools/romlab` with BN5's ROM, from Lan's room after the intro: the stores into both words logged a frame at a time (GetRNG from `0x08004CA8`, GetRNGSecondary from `0x08004CAC` and `0x0800031C`), and through a battle forced as in BN5 guest battles' row (a Mettaur and a Mystery Data `20 34 05 00`) no other store but `0x08009A0A`'s, the value the primary held as the battle began. With the words poked before it, the first Custom screen's hand followed the secondary alone (`0x22222222`: MiniBomb L first; `0x44444444`: Sword S; the same whatever the primary), and the Mystery Data stood where the primary's roll, 164 steps on, said it would (`0x11111111` and `0x55555555`: 4 and 1, there; `0x66666666` and `0xCAFEBABE`: 8 and 6, none). Verify in the game, with BN5 beside BN6: `CYBERWORLD_EMU_DEBUG=1 build.py shot --scene emu --net-biome x0 --seed S --dev quiet` with the arrival's words paged and `0:battle` (the layer's first random battle) prints "guest: its random numbers from seed ..." (seed 1: `85EBCA77`, words `9BB31115` and `DCB010DD`); seed 1's first Custom screen offers Vulcan1 D, seed 2's Recov10 A, seed 3's and seed 4's a Cannon A in other hands; seed 1 run again, or with RIGHT held as the battle comes, gives the same picture. In BN6's Practice NetBattle (the Arena's pair, docs/ONLINE_ARENA.md, A rematch in a second) both words are alike on the two GBAs all through the way in; 19 frames after Practice's last confirm the battle draws the first and keeps it, 71 frames on puts it back, and the folders are shuffled from the second the frame after: verify with `--dev arena,arenaboth,arenaseed=7` and `arenaseed=8` (the log's "side N's GBA deals" lines: other first hands, the same on both GBAs). |

movs r2,#0x2C; muls r0,r2; adds r0,r1; mov pc,lr`, no bound. The archives' 23 words: a scan for each archive's address, aligned, and the same with bit 31 (a compressed pointer): none more; the code takes archive (id >> 8) and entry (id & 0xFF) (`0x08027D10`, `0x08027D34`, `0x081200EC`, the chat box's table `0x08042034`), so archive 1 grown to 256 entries names ids to 511. The pack: every load of eToolkit `+0x4C` (a scan for `mov rY,r10` and `ldr rX,[rY,#0x4C]` within three instructions, less the six where rY is eToolkit `+0x2C`, the chat box): GetTotalChipCount, zeroFill_e2002230, getOffsetToQuantityOfChipCode, sub_8021CA8, bn6f sub_80AADA6 (the link battle's copy of the pack at `0x02033000`, 0xF00 bytes, which no run reaches) and sub_81376E8 (the order stamps' clear, a NEW GAME's: the extension's stamps start 0 from the power-on clear); no literal names the pack's RAM. TakeChips, GiveChips, sub_8021B2A, GetChipCountOfCode and the pack's list all go through getOffsetToQuantityOfChipCode; the order stamps' setters (`0x081394A8` on) work from the count's address. The folder editor's exit (bn6f sub_81339BC) clears the pack and gives it its list's chips again: a clear that left the extension doubled its counts, so the clear clears it too. The original halfwords each change reads first: `4657 6CFF 210C 4351 187F` (`0x08021C9A`), `4657 6CFF 210C 4341` (`0x08021BE4`), `4657 6CFF 210C 4351 187F 18F9 7809` (`0x08021CD6`), `F7DE FE36 BD00 0000` (`0x08021C70`), `429A DB00 2101` (both pseudo tests), the words 0x140, 0x13A, 0x170, 0x170. The extension's RAM: the engine RAM survey of issue #82 (static scans, 592 runs, every RAM byte logged): `0x02010CDC`-`0x020117FF` written by the power-on clear (`0x080001CC`) alone, read by nothing; the RFU library's globals below it, the battle sprite pool from `0x02011800` above. The support family runs the routine, marks AttackVars +1 and ends the attack at its next frame (`0x080ED130`), BN5's in the frame it runs it. The flashes: a 5-byte entry by the number the spawner keeps at +4 (r4), read at `0x080BA570`. The hand's merge (`0x08029224`) names Atk+10 0xC0, Navi+20 0xC1, Atk+30 0xC3, WhiCapsl 0xB8 and Uninstll 0xB9, adding a plus chip's power (+0x1A) to the chip before it where that chip's +9 has bit 2 (an attack) or 4 (a Navi chip). The tables' rows each end where code begins (a push {lr}). The panel claim: `0x080EA08C` calls `0x080090E8` and spawns its object only where it answers 1, else r0 0 with Z set and r1-r3 as they came (as `0x080EA0BA`); `0x080090E8` answers the battle state's +0x32 bit 0x40 (`0x0802D510`), which BN5 sets only at `0x0802D594` (where `0x0802B3E0` is not 1 and MegaMan's NaviStats +0x2A is 1 or more), BN6's twin gate reads BN6's bit, set only at `0x0802E112` for a battle of a type not 1, 6, 7 or 9, its settings without 0x200000, and the link's byte `0x0200AD04` set (written only by the link routines, from what the cable answers) or its settings' bit 8 with event flag 0x1722 (the Beast Link Gate); no other code calls the pool (`0x0802E920`, `0x0802E948`, `0x0802E954`, `0x0802E9CC`, `0x0802EA6E`: their one caller the object `0x080EA020`) or spawns the fourth kind's 0x83. The computed powers: a power of 1000 and up indexes the routines' table by power - 1000, BN5's 76 entries and more, BN6's 45. The deck: 30 u16 at `0x0203C830`, the equipped folder copied and shuffled at battle start (`0x08008CC8`). Emotion: BattleState +0x0D MegaMan's side, his battle mood at `0x0203C88E` (1-0x40 worried, 0 dark, 0xFF Full Synchro); a battle's first mood comes from NaviStats +0x0E unless 0xFF, else from the dark meter at NaviStats +0x44 (500 calm, below 470 dark), which eToolkit +0x94's word guards (the meter XOR the u32 at `0x02002338`, else it resets to 500). Gregar's list-7 numbers named point at its placeholder sprite `0x084DC040`, which no layer lists. A DarkChip that ran adds the NaviCust's HP bug to the battle's NaviStats +0x18 (`0x0800B79A`, chips 0x11E-0x122 by the id used: DrkSword +2, DarkThnd +1, DrkRecov to 7, DarkInvs none, DarkPlus +4, at most 7), the byte the NaviCust's own HP bug sets (bn6f sub_813CE26, sub_813CF4C): `0x08010230` takes 1 HP each 40, 35, 30, 25, 20, 15 or 10 frames by its level, never below 1 HP, and not in a time freeze or on the Custom screen; the next battle's NaviStats are copied fresh, without it. The same `0x0800F4B2` serves the Giga chips BugRSwrd and BgDthThd: their time-freeze object makes MegaMan's charged shot attack 0x21 or 0x22 (`0x080117D4`, `0x08011E40`, `0x08011E78`), 200 for a BugFrag in DrkSword's area or with DarkThnd's params, else a Sword's 80 or a Thunder's 40, as a bystander's rumor in BN6's text says; read, not seen. **The attacks:** DrkSword's area is the sword areas' entry 0x11 (4 bytes a subfamily), LifeSrd's (sub 5): 2 columns by 3 rows ahead. DarkInvs runs the time-freeze object (type 4, 0x84; `0x080EA908`, its param 1 = 3 to `0x080EAAC4`), which sets MegaMan's AI flag 0x20000000; `0x0802E4E4` turns it into his side's state +0x54 = 1, +0x30 = 480 frames, invulnerable; while +0x54 holds, `0x0802E4B8` gives him control mode 0xD, in which the game moves and attacks for him. DarkPlus never joins the chip before it: the hand's merge (`0x08029224`) names Atk+10 0xC0, Navi+20 0xC1, Atk+30 0xC3, WhiCapsl 0xB8 and Uninstll 0xB9 by id; alone it runs family 0x1C sub 3 (`0x08010488`), a puff. **The menus:** library flag 0x20 keeps a chip out of the pack's list (`0x08121C8C`, bn6f sub_811FE7C), so BN6's folder editor cannot take them in; effect flag 0x20 makes a chip a DarkChip there (`0x08136E5C`, bn6f sub_8135080: three in a folder at most, `movs r0,#3` at `0x08136E9A`, else the editor's archive `0x086CD12C` (bn6f CompText86CF1A8, the second word of its table at `0x08135D0C`) runs its script 9 in MegaMan's NAVIGATOR box), a purple card, and its frame palette 3 (`0x0811AB06`, bn6f sub_81197B4; read, not seen). Verify, as seen headless with a throwaway hook (not committed) that wrote the folder in code * and marked it owned as own_folder_chips does, set the BugFrags through GiveBugfrags or TakeBugfrags and raised the battle's enemies to 2000 HP (`--seed 13`, its first battle a Mettaur): with 5 BugFrags DrkSword dealt 400 in a magenta crescent (a Mettaur two columns ahead and a row down deleted, a Starfish three columns ahead untouched), the BugFrags 5 to 4 at the use, save and battle's copy alike, the HP bug 2; DarkThnd 200, a purple Thunder ball, the Mettaur paralysed, the HP bug 1; DrkRecov 50 to 1050 of 2000 HP, the HP bug 7, then 1 HP each 10 frames; DarkInvs a time freeze named DarkInvs, then 480 frames of MegaMan as a green phantom slashing and firing his held Cannon on his own, LEFT held for 200 frames not keeping him in the back column, untouched by the Mettaur's shockwaves; DarkPlus after a Cannon left the HUD at "Cannon 40" with two chip icons and dealt 40 (Atk+10 in its place: "Cannon 40+10"), and alone made a puff, took a BugFrag and gave the HP bug 4. With 0 BugFrags each was its base chip, nothing spent, no HP bug: Sword 80 (the HUD still reading "DrkSword 400"), Thunder 40, Recov10 (50 to 60), Invisibl (the freeze named Invisibl), Atk+10 alone. Four DrkSwords on 4 BugFrags dealt 400, 400, 800 (Full Synchro after a counter hit) and 400, the HP bug 2, 4, 6, 7; the next battle began at 0; past the last BugFrag the next DrkSword was a Sword and the count stayed 0. The Custom screen shows the name, the element, the power and the blank picture (a purple square) over the "11E"-"122" icons; CHIP FOLDER and FOLDER EDIT list them at 80 MB, the pack never (spares counted in it). With effect flag 0x20 set and library flag 0x20 cleared in the core's copy, the pack lists them, their cards turn purple, and a fourth DarkChip is refused in MegaMan's NAVIGATOR box ("You can use only 3 of the same DarkChips."); a second DrkSword in the place of the third DarkChip was taken (the editor's copy rule by MB, `0x081372DC`, one of a chip of 50 MB or more, did not stop it; traced since: the sort keys, the row after). |

AlphabetSortPos is given the place of the chip whose name sorts just before. **Effect flag 0x20 in BN6's code** (every reader of +9 against 0x20 in bn6f): the folder's three and the NAVIGATOR line (sub_8135080), the purple card (sub_81197B4's palette 3: seen in FOLDER EDIT and the pack), the Custom screen's cursor put on the hand's first DarkChip (sub_802806C: seen), the Chip Traders' list leaves it out (sub_811FE7C called with r1 1 by the trader's routine, read, not seen), the pack's total does not count it (sub_8021CA8), the Library counts it in a category 6 no screen asks for (sub_81207F8: callers ask 0, 1, 2, 4 and 5); the Custom screen's fade and music for a DarkChip under the cursor (sub_802A2E8) were not seen in our captures. **The BBS:** bn6f's off_813E04C (12 words, the BBS's text archives) found by its shape, 12 pointers into `0x087E`-`0x087F` whose ninth and eleventh, tenth and twelfth are equal, at `0x0813FE2C`, its one reference the literal at `0x081403A8`; its fourth, LZ77 at `0x087E84CC` (Falzar's CompText87E9578), unpacks to 8129 bytes: the 4-byte header `0x001FC100`, then 48 scripts; script 27 is the post (ts_position_text, ts_position_arrow, five pages of text, ts_wait_hold), which ta_rom_pages runs in a chat box without its positioning, to its hold. **The flame:** list 7's `0x3C` points at `0x084B9B54`, a plain sprite of 1612 bytes (two animations of four frames, one palette, blue to white) that no map object lists (OverworldMapObjects `0x0A4F24` and handler 3's records read); `0x54` points at the placeholder `0x084DC040` and no map object lists it. **Verify** (BN6's ROM alone, `CYBERWORLD_EMU_DEBUG=1`): `build.py shot --scene emu --run-depth 2 --seed 1 --frames 60` prints "xnavi: BN6's list 7 0x3c, purple, at list 7 0x54, 1612 bytes", "dark: BN6's flame of darkness with DrkSword (Sword without a BugFrag), list 7 84" and "rumor: BN6's own words on DarkChips, bystander 0"; `--talk npc:800` with the arrival paged has the bystander say BN6's post, `--talk dark:800` MegaMan the flame's words. With `--dev folder=286` and the battle step the Custom screen reads "DrkSword 80" over Sword's picture, the cursor on it; used, Sword's cut and "dark: DarkChip 0 ran as its base chip, no BugFrag". With `--talk bugfrags:200` it reads 400; used, the magenta crescent, "dark: DarkChip 0x11e's dark power ran, a BugFrag paid, 49 left in the battle", MegaMan's HP draining, and after the battle "dark: a DarkChip used: max HP 100 -> 80". With `--talk bugfrag:200`, one BugFrag, the Custom screen after that use reads "DrkSword 80". `--dev darkchips=0xC6,folder=286`: FOLDER EDIT's pack lists DarkThnd, DrkRecov and DarkInvs ("dark: BN6's DarkChip 287 to the Pack"), purple cards, and refuses one into the folder with MegaMan's NAVIGATOR line. |

Verify: the same capture, `tools/liberation_bn5.py`'s reader, a frame's BG2 drawn from these and compared. The events: `0x08055D68` stores r0, r1, r2, r3 into the 12-byte slot r2's low byte names at `0x02001AB0`; the item dispatch `0x0804D848` passes the item kinds' table's +4 word (its next byte the find's text script), and a liberated item panel shows its find's icon over the panel with the Navi's line (recorded). The board's drawing read in the code: `0x0804EEE2` copies the look's tiles (its +0, +4, +8 to `0x08000870`), then for each panel takes its look from `0x0804F0D4` (`ldr r0,[look,#0x10 + kind*4]`: the kind is the look's index) and draws its edges (`0x0804F204`: per side one of its look bytes indexes that side's table at `0x0804FB94`, lists of tile 0x8800 + n at x, y). |
| BN5 map objects: Battle Network 5 Team Colonel (USA) (`src/layer/xnavi.c`, `src/layer/blockers.c`, docs/MULTIROM.md) | BN5 `0x0043CC` map-object handlers, `0x0AD930` OverworldMapObjects, `0x0AE2FC` handler 3's records, `0x035060` the internet groups' spawners (`0x034EA4` the real world's), `0x034F64` checks, `0x03F364` map text archives (`0x03F360` the real world's), `0x759B0C` key item names; its list 7's `0x01`, `0x5D` and `0x69`; Gregar's list 7's `0x92`, `0x8A` and `0x87` | BN5 runs BN6's map-object engine. The handler table is the one word pointing at its handler 0 (`0x080AE041`), whose literal names its OverworldMapObjects (113 entries of 16 bytes in BN6's layout: sprite list 7, sprite, animation, priority, +0x0A a flag that shows it, +0x0C and +0x0E flags its spawn sets); 46 handlers, 3 the obstacles' at `0x080AE5DD`, its records' literal naming `0x0AE2FC` (BN6's layout: present flag, walls' flag 0x1020 + n, check's flag 0x1060 + m, then sprite list, sprite, idle and open animations, field, palette, priority, a spare, sound). The spawners are the jump table of BN6's `0x0803516C` (mapObject_spawnMapObjectsForMap), found by its shape at `0x0803562C`; each group's spawner's first `ldr r1, [pc]` names its per-map lists of BN6's 20-byte spawn records. The checks by BN6's check routine's shape (`subs r4,#0xF0`, the flag 0x1060 + m, at `0x08035440`): per group from 0x80, per map, 16 script numbers of the map's text archive (per group, per map, LZ77; `0x0803F334` reads it). The key item names: 128 scripts, 0x26 NebulaID, 0x50-0x55 the P-Codes (MaylCode, DexCode, YaiCode, SciPCode, CasPCode, OldPCode). Its net areas hold few map objects, nearly all locks: list 7 `0x01`, the Security Cube (plain, 4335 bytes; animation 0 standing with its X eyes, 1 opening them and flickering out over 23 frames; sound 0x99), two in ACDC Area 1 (MaylCode, YaiCode: "The Security Cube for Mayl's HP... It can't be unlocked without its P-Code"), one in ACDC Area 2 (DexCode), End Area 3 (CasPCode) and SciLab 3; list 7 `0x03`, the laser barrier with a skull, the sprite BN6's cubes are (its four ways at the same animations, four wide ones after them), which the NebulaID opens ("A door bars the way. It won't open." without it: ACDC Area 1, Oran Area 2, End Area 4), what MegaMan has shown ("A voice speaks out of nowhere... Power's been shown!": Nebula Area 4's eight, by the Library's counts, HPMemory and DarkChips), a Navi's battle (Nebula Area 6's six) or nothing ("Complex security prevents this door from opening", End Area 4); list 7 `0x5D`, a wall of dark flames (LZ77, 4264 bytes decompressed: animation 0 standing across a walkway along world Y, 1 along X, 2 and 3 burning out over 10 frames), Nebula Area 6's (handler 3's id 0x24, its check script 7 clears its present flag 0x01B2 once the six flames of darkness' battles are won: "Power's been shown!") and End Area 5's, the Undernet's door ("Access to the general public is prohibited"). Its one sight besides: list 7 `0x69`, a dark hole (plain, 1620 bytes, one animation of 5 frames), a purple vortex standing past a platform's edge in Undernet 2 and Nebula Areas 1, 3 and 4 (handler 0's ids 0x6C and 0x6D, shown once flags 0x01BC and 0x01BD are set) and Nebula Area 6 (0x56, always). The Security Cube is copied whole into the Navis' space and listed at Gregar's list-7 `0x92` (146, the placeholder `0x084DC040` before), the wall decompressed at `0x8A` (138), the dark hole at `0x87` (135), at the first layer once BN5 is read. Verify: with BN5 beside BN6, `CYBERWORLD_EMU_DEBUG=1 build.py shot --scene emu --net-biome x0 --run-depth 2 --dev pieces=16 --seed 6` prints "xnavi: list 7 0x1 of ... at list 7 146, 4335 bytes" and "blockers: lock 0, kind 5, variant 9, at world 120,108"; a capture with MegaMan put down at 70 100 shows the green cube in a walkway's mouth, and with flag 0x1465 set (`0:flags 0x1465 0x1465 1`) A at it enters the P-Code: it opens its eyes and flickers out. `--net-biome x3 --run-depth 2 --dev pieces=8 --setup short,0,0,0,1 --seed 16` prints "xnavi: list 7 0x5d of ... at list 7 138, 4264 bytes" and "blockers: lock 0, kind 2, variant 11, at world 300,-148": the wall stands across the walkway (seed 11 one along the other axis), MegaMan names it, and with HeatMan's Cross it burns out. `--net-biome x3 --run-depth 2 --seed 1` prints "prop: BN5's dark hole at 27,33 (world -12,-116)", and a capture with MegaMan put down at 18 -66 shows the vortex past the platform's edge (seed 3 at world -172,-52; about half of Nebula Area's layers have a room for it). In the ROM, group 0x90's spawner (`0x0807FFD9`) names its lists at `0x0807FFF0`: ACDC Area 1's (`0x0807FFF8`) holds handler 3's ids 0x0A and 0x0C (the Security Cubes, present flags 0x0122 and 0x0124) and 0x0D (the skull barrier, 0x0125), and its checks 0-2 run scripts 0-2 of its archive (`0x08771D3C`), which test key items 0x50, 0x52 and 0x26 and clear those flags. |
| GiveZenny and GiveBugfrags (`src/director/director_dev.c`, `--talk bugfrags`; the guest battles' rewards until issue #82's last round) | `0x0803CFCD` (`BN6_GIVE_ZENNY`), `0x0803D055` (`BN6_GIVE_BUGFRAGS`) | bn6f's GiveZenny (Falzar `0x0803CFF8`), 0x2C earlier in Gregar as GiveItem is: its bytes there match (`push {r4-r7,lr}`, the zenny check's `bl`, `bne`, `mov r2,r10; ldr r2,[r2,#0x3C]; ldr r1,[r2,#0x5C]`, the literal 999999 at `0x0803CFFC`). r0 the amount: ProtectedZenny and its checks, capped at 999999. GiveBugfrags (Falzar `0x0803D080`) is 0x2C earlier too, at `0x0803D054`, where `tools/bn6f_match.py` located it by its bytes: the BugFrags' check's `bl`, `bne`, `mov r2,r10; ldr r2,[r2,#0x3C]; ldr r1,[r2,#0x60]` (ProtectedBugfrags, `BN6_BUGFRAGS`), r0 added and capped at 9999 (its literal `0x270F`). Verify: a guest battle won on a BN5 layer adds its results screen's zenny to the PET's, and its BugFrags to the state's `bugfrags` (BN5's results screen, above); `--talk bugfrags:FRAME` adds 50. |
| Sprites behind the second layer (`RomLayout.net_area[].host`) | maps 0x91:0 and 0x83:2; the player object's +0xA5 (`0x02009FE5`) | The game draws the player and the other objects there with OAM priority 3, behind the second tile layer (BG2, priority 2): Seaside Area 1 has its boardwalks on that layer where they cross its blue platform. Every other map of the net areas, 0x91:1 and 0x83:0 among them, draws them with priority 2, in front of both layers. The object's +0xA5 holds attr 2's high byte (0x0C for priority 3, 0x08 for 2), rewritten every frame: a poke of 0x08 was undone the next frame, and poking every RAM byte in which the two maps differed from three others did not change it, so it follows the map itself. A generated layer draws some floor, the pads' rims and centrepieces and the Net Dealer's counter on the second layer (the originals' own pairs of tiles), which hid MegaMan in Seaside, so those two areas' layers take over another map of their group (0x91:1, 0x83:0: the same tile set and palette) and still learn from theirs. Found by logging OAM entries near the screen's middle and the object's bytes per area (`--net-biome 0`-`17`); check: +0xA5 reads 0x0C on a layer in 0x91:0 and 0x08 in 0x91:1. |
| Real-world maps (`src/map/area_src.c`, `src/world/townsrc.c`) | `0x0329A8` map descriptors, `0x033530` coordinate data (groups `0x00`-`0x06`) | The two tables before the internet's (bn6f `initMapTilesState_803037c` and `decompressCoordEventData_8030aa4` pick them for groups below `0x80`); same formats. Their tile sets are 256-colour: the two blocks of Central Town's add up to `0xCD40` bytes, 821 tiles of 64, and the palette header gives 256 colours. The tile map's first layer is the ground, the second what stands on it (the game shows them on BG1 at priority 3 and BG2 at priority 2). While the game runs, BG colours 208-255 hold its interface's palettes. Checked by drawing all 37 maps (`build.py world`) and comparing the game's VRAM and palette RAM on Central Town with the decoded tile set: tiles 0-819 match, colours 0-207 match. |
| Real-world map takeover (`src/map/mapslot.c`) | `0x08034638` NPCList_maps00, `0x080345E4` map script lists, `0x08034654` object spawners, `0x08030904` EnterMap_RealWorldMapGroupJumptable | Found beside their internet counterparts in bn6f `asm03_1_0.s` and `asm03_0.s` (the real-world tables come first, 7 entries each); each pointer read from the Gregar ROM leads to its group's code or lists. A group loader's warp, sprite and jack-in tables are literals in its code, found as for the internet (the jack-in table is the literal loaded before `str r0, [r5, #0x64]`). |
| Map objects (`src/world/town.c`, `townsrc.c`) | spawn records per map (Central Town `0x0804F5D0`, the list the group's spawner loads with its first `ldr r1, [pc, #imm]`), `0x0A4F24` OverworldMapObjects | 20 bytes: spawner (5: a map object), a handler (0: look the id up), 2 bytes, x, y, z (16.16), object id. An id's 16-byte OverworldMapObjects entry starts with the sprite's category (`0x1C`, list 7), sprite and animation (checked: `0x7D` and `0x7E` give 7:51, `0x16` 7:16, as the game draws them). Central Town's: `0x7D` its trees on their round planters (5 on the plaza, 2 on the verge between the gates), `0x7E` the same tree at fixed priority 3 (between the houses), `0x16` the blue bird statue, and story objects (`0x15` the Expo barrier, `0x65`/`0x6A`/`0x6F`/`0x70` the bus terminal, `0x73` the robot dog) that event flags show. bn6f's `map_object_spawn_data_struct` names the first byte `index: 0x00`; the Gregar data holds 5 there, and records with 0 spawn nothing. The game runs at most 56 map objects and 16 NPCs at a time (bn6f `NUM_OVERWORLD_MAP_OBJECTS`, `NUM_OVERWORLD_NPC_OBJECTS`). |
| Map buffers (`src/emu/bn6.h`) | EWRAM `0x02013A00` tile map, `0x02027A00` coordinate data | bn6f `decompAndCopyMapTiles_8030472` decompresses a map's tile map to `0x02013A00` (12-byte header, then the entries) after `decompressCoordEventData_8030aa4` has put the coordinate data at `0x02027A00`, which the game then reads in place: a tile map's entries may take `0x13FF4` bytes (`BN6_TILEMAP_MAX`), no more. |
| Warps back from a building (`src/world/town.c`) | Lan's house `0x01:1`, warp list `0x0804F274` | Its first warp leads to Central Town at world (-40, 266), facing 5: Lan's front door, where the town starts him. Central Town's door triggers (section 3 values 1-5) index its own warp list from 1: Lan's house, the Academy, the Expo, Aster Land, the bus to ACDC. |
| Checks (`src/map/mapslot.c`) | `0x0803461C` check tables (bn6f `off_803461C`), `0x08040794` map text archives (bn6f `mapPtrs80407C0`) | A section-3 trigger `0xF0` + n under Lan when A is pressed (and no NPC before him) is check n (bn6f `HandleCoordinateInteractionCutscene`): skipped if event flag `0x16C0` + n is set, else byte n of the map's 16-byte check table (per real-world group, per map; `0xFF` none) is the text script `CutsceneScriptObjectInteract` runs from the map's text archive at `0x02033404`. That archive is the map's own, which `chatbox_uncompMapTextArchives` decompresses to `0x02033400` on entering the map from `mapPtrs80407C0` (a real-world and an internet list of per-group, per-map LZ77 archives; the decompressed data starts with a word holding its size). Found in Gregar as the literal (pool word `0x08040790`) of the second of three look-alike routines (`0x08040768`, bn6f `chatbox_selectCompTextByMap_8040794`); Central Town's table `0x0804F800` gives scripts 0-4, none, 6-10, 12, 13 for checks 0-12, ACDC Town's 0-7. Tested by pressing A at Lan's door in a headless capture. |
| Jack-in (`src/map/mapslot.c`) | `0x08034CB6` the jack routine, `0x08099A00` jack-in destinations | With R in the real world the routine reads the trigger under Lan (`checkCoordinateTrigger`, the z offset per map from `0x08034D6C`): a value `0x40` + n is jack-in point n unless flag `0x16D0` + n is set; byte n of the map's jack-in table (GameState `+0x64`, `0xFF` none) is one of 43 20-byte destinations, which the jack-in cutscene (bn6f `CutsceneScriptJackIn_8098824`) warps to: WarpData (group, number, departure, facing, x, y, z), then the "Jack in!" line to show. The table was found in Gregar by its first records (Falzar `0x080984C8`). Real-world triggers in section 3 are 0x40-0x44 over 8 to 16 cells in front of the object (Central Town's 0x40 4 x 4 before the statue and 0x41 5 x 3 before Aster Land; ACDC Town's 0x40 2 x 5 by the squirrel statue and 0x41 4 x 2 at the doghouse), types 9-12 at the corners and 0x11 elsewhere. Tested by jacking in from the town in headless captures and autopilot runs. |
| Real-world music | chapter lists at `0x0360E4` | Their real-world entries (per map of the group): ACDC Town `0x24`, `0x05`; Central Town `0x03`, then `0x04` for Lan's house, his room, the bathroom and Aster Land; Cyber Academy `0x05` (`0x0C` in the second list). |
| Height (`src/map/stairs.c`, `netmap.c`, `coords.c`) | coordinate data sections 1 and 2 | Section 1 holds floor heights in the walls' format: type 0x11 raises a whole cell to z, types 0x13 (climbing towards world +X) and 0x14 (towards world -Y) are ramp cells with the foot's z, value 1 and the height above it, rising 4 per cell. Section 2 marks cells of each level for sprite priority (type 0x11, height 8). Walls beside a ramp or around a raised floor start at the ground and are taller (32 around Seaside's 16-high platform, 42 beside Sky's 32-high stair); a ramp's top meets its raised floor with no wall. Past a ramp's top the originals set a step before the raised floor: type 0x11 from the foot's z with the rise as its height (the Undernet's 0x95:0 and Sky's 0x94:3: z 0, height 32), then the raised floor's own cells at z 32, height 0. The game takes a floor cell only within its heights, and a player coming up the ramp is under the rise until he crosses that row: without the step, MegaMan fell to the ground at the ramp's top and walked on under the room (issue #22). Verify: a layer with a stair (`--net-biome 5 --run-depth 5 --seed 4`, its ramp's foot at world (165, -28)) walked up its middle with `CYBERWORLD_STATE_POS=1`: z climbs 0 to 32 and stays 32 on the room. |
| Counters (`src/map/props.c`, `RomLayout.net_area[].counter`) | Sky Area 3 (0x94:2) around world (-300, -144); Green Area 2 (0x92:1) around (-28, -468) | The Net Dealer's capsule of Sky Area 3 and the NetCafe desk of Green Area 2 (docs/LEVEL_DESIGN.md, Props). Each is a clean block of second-layer tiles (the capsule tiles 0x22D-0x35A in bank 3, 23 of them; the desk 0x313-0x32D in bank 0xC, 32) over an untouched floor, and a ring of section-0 wall cells (the capsule 5 x 3 cells, X -320 to -288, Y -152 to -136, its left corners of the unlisted types 9 and 0x0B beside 5 and 7; the desk 7 x 3, X -56 to -8, Y -480 to -464). The ring's back row lies on a panel edge (Y - 4 a multiple of 32 from the map's edge offset) and the counter reaches half a panel or more towards world +Y. Its navi stands one cell behind that row (NPC scripts at 0x07D630 and 0x079B6C: set coords (-296, -154, 0) and (-24, -484, 32), 0x0A radius 8, 0x0C shift centre (0, 8, 0) and (0, 12, 0)), and bn6f's OverworldNPCObject keeps that shift at +0x11-0x13, where the interaction and collision circles are measured from. Green Area 2's NetCafe floor, its desk and navi stand 32 high: the desk's art is cut from its own floor's height. Central, Seaside, Green and Sky share one tile set (0x5D0FC4) in their own palettes, so the capsule draws in Central's and Seaside's colours too, as Central Area 2's (0x90:1) does in bank 3. Located from the maps' second layers by those tiles, their rings by the walls around them and the navis by their scripts' 0x0C; checked by drawing each map's second layer alone and in the game, a generated counter in each of the four areas with the dealer behind it, answering A across it. |
| Framed pads (`src/map/props.c`, `RomLayout.net_area[].pad_hues`) | Central Area 1 (0x90:0), Seaside Area 1 (0x91:0), Sky Area 2 (0x94:1), and their areas' other maps | A 3x3 island of floor (its 12 side neighbours bridges at most, its corners void, by the walls) whose middle's hue is the recess's (Central's blue in bucket 6, Seaside's yellow in 1-2, Sky's lavender in 8-11; Seaside's first island is a blue platform, bucket 7): the first layer's 180 tiles over its diamond (the floor drawn 4 below the panel edges) and the 12 pixels of faces under its lower edges, anchored on its corner of lowest world X and Y. The islands of the area's maps are laid over each other, the one with the fewest bridges beside its lower sides (world -X and +Y) first, each tile taken from an island where it draws the island alone (no first-layer pixel on another floor's panel, at z 0 with the floor 4 below its edges), and the rim past the diamond along its upper edges and corners too: Seaside Area 1's pads both have a bridge below them, and its stamp had hung a yellow piece of boardwalk off every pad (`pad stamp area 1 ok 1 tiles 200, 0 panels beside its lower sides` with `CYBERWORLD_EMU_DEBUG`). A placed stamp's tile goes only where it covers the pad and the void alone. Checked with `CYBERWORLD_EMU_DEBUG` ("pad stamp area 0 ok 1 tiles 180"), in `build.py atlas 0` and in the game, MegaMan arriving in a pad's recess. |
| Floor shades (`src/map/netmap_learn.c`, `netmap_paste.c`, `RomLayout.net_area[].rebank`) | The Graveyard's maps (0x96:0-2), first-layer palette banks 1 and 2 | Its pale platforms are its dark slabs' own tiles (0x0C4, 0x0CE, 0x04C, 0x012 and the rest) in palette bank 2, the slabs in bank 1: 6147 first-layer entries in bank 1 and 2455 in bank 2 over Graveyard 2, the second layer all bank 2 (its ramps). Learned as one grey floor, the classes mixed the two shades panel by panel; a generated map draws a bank-2 floor tile in bank 1 wherever its maps draw that tile in bank 1 too (the links' and ramp ends' own bank-2 tiles stay). Found by printing the bank of the tile under each panel's middle with its brightness (pale 185, dark 140); checked in `build.py atlas 4`. |

The Graveyard's cyan crosses are drawn in the floor's own layer (its second layer holds only the three ramps' slats, tiles 0x2F5-0x317, 24 of each), in a colour its floors show nowhere else: (0, 181, 173) on screen, 3556 pixels over Graveyard 2. `props_learn_floor_emblem` marks the first-layer tiles drawing it, joins them 8 ways and takes a group of 2 to 24 tiles lying on one panel with floor all round.
