| Props' map objects (`src/layer/layer_objs.c`, `RomLayout.net_area[].looks`) | OverworldMapObjects `0x0A4F24` (16 bytes an id) | The originals' own objects by id, their 20-byte spawn records (5, handler 0, X, Y, Z in 16.16, the id) and entries (sprite list 7, sprite, animation, priority 0xFF from the coordinate data): `0x3D` a cybertree (7:0x1F; Green Area 2's avenue, ACDC Area's rows), `0x7F`/`0x80`/`0x81` the giant cybertree's body, base (+24, -24, -24 from the body) and crown (-24, +24, +154) (7:0x53, animations 0-2; Green Area 2), `0x9C` the Undernet's statue (7:0x71; Undernet 1, 12 below its plateau), `0x9F` a brazier (7:0x72), `0xD3` the Graveyard's monument (7:0x9B, animation 1), `0xCA` a gravestone of its rows (7:0x8E, animation 1), `0x3E` Central Area 1's WELCOME sign (7:0x2B, priority 2), `0x47` Seaside Area 1's BBS (7:0x34, uncompressed, 24 below the floor). None of them is shown or hidden by an event flag (entry +0x0A zero). The table holds 244 ids (0x00-0xF3; handler 0's code follows it at `0x080A5E64`, reading an object's id as a byte through the literal at `0x080A5E8C`, the table's one reference), so it has no room for an id of the engine's: another game's prop stands as one of handler 3's objects instead (`blockers.c`, docs/MULTIROM.md). The originals set them 14 and 18 from their panel's corner (the panel's centre moved (-2, +2)): in the void past a floor's edge, 6 to 30 past it, or on floor inside a walled hole of one panel (16 wall cells on the panel's edges, as the game's walls ring any non-floor panel) or of three in a row (the Graveyard's). The map's sprite list takes 12 entries and 0x8800 bytes of decompressed sprites (the loader at `0x002906`: `cmp r0,#0xC` at `0x002940`, its buffer `0x02037800` to `0x02040000`). Found in each map's object list (the group's spawner's first `ldr r1, [pc, #imm]` literal) and checked by spawning each in a generated layer and in its own place. |
| The NaviCust's Spins (`src/director/director_board.c` grant_spins, `src/layer/layer_objs.c`, `src/layer/navicust.c`) | key items `0x50`-`0x55`; eToolkit `+0x50` KeyItemsPtr (`0x02003134`), `+0x78` the checks (`0x02004A8C`), seeds `0x020004E0` | BN6 turns a program with L and R only while it holds its colour's key item: `0x50` white, `0x51` yellow, `0x52` pink, `0x53` red, `0x54` blue, `0x55` green (their descriptions, "Lets you rotate white parts with the L and R Button", are the key item archive's scripts 80-85, ExpMemry's 113 = `0x71`). The NaviCust's one handler for a held program (bn6f `sub_813612C`) turns it with L and R only when `sub_8136364` answers: the program under the list's cursor, its record's colour at +3, plus `0x4F`, to CheckKeyItem. For a program picked up from the board that is the list's cursor's program, not the held one's (BN6's quirk: it only ever lets a player turn more than the Spins held, and the drafts count on none of it). CheckKeyItem (bn6f `0x0803CE2C`) answers 0 for an item whose check byte (`+0x78` array) differs from its seed (`0x020004E0` array) XOR `0x55`: Gregar's code at `0x006E3C` writes the check as the game gives an item, `0x006E50` tests it (Falzar's key is `0x6F`). Found by granting the counts alone (R did nothing), printing the three arrays, and reading Gregar's bytes where Falzar's routine stands; checked by turning a blue Shield placed from the list with R and L. A run holds the profile's Spins (docs/META.md) and no other: the engine writes the count and the check for those and zeroes the rest; the run's own is a key item Mystery Data (`04 20 FF FF id`, as a ScrtData's). Verify: with SpinWhit alone, a white program from the list turns with L and R and one of another colour does not. |
| Guardians (`src/director/boss.c`, `src/layer/stage_npc.c`, `guardians.c`) | songs and sounds per bn6f `SoundOffsets.inc`; mugshots per text command `F5 00` | Boss prelude song 0x1C, boss battle 0x16; sounds: BN6's beam 0x76 (a Navi's log-in and log-out on the net alike; 0x77 is no Navi's), appear 0x94. A Navi logs in and out on the net as BN6's scenes play it (Colonel in JudgeTreeComp, BlastMan, DiveMan and another in SkyACDCArea, CircusMan in Underground; the command `0x52` does the same in one): its overworld sprite's own log-in, animation `0x19` + its facing (9 frames blank, 4 in its lighter palette bank, 4 itself), or log-out, `0x21` + its facing (the reverse), beside a beam actor at its place, map object sprite list 7's `0x00` (uncompressed), its animation 0 rising in and 1 out, which plays 0x76 (bn6f `npcscript_809F86C`, Gregar `0x080A0D4C`: `28 76 00 08 2E 00 2A C0 03`, and `byte_809F880`, `0x080A0D60`: `28 76 00 08 2E 01 2A C0 03`, written out in our own script). The standard block is in Gregar's 0x47, 0x49, 0x4B, 0x50, 0x4F, 0x3B, 0x54 and 0x53 and BN5's six; BlastMan (0x51) logs out by 0x1B (facing 2-3) or 0x1C (4-5) and DiveMan (0x52) by 0x1C, neither with a log-in; JudgeMan (0x55) has 0x1B in, 0x1A out; ElementMan (0x56) neither. BN6 never fades a Navi by alpha: bn6f's own note on the command says it "doesn't actually work" (asm/npc.s, ns_set_alpha). After a scenario Navi's battle BN6 instead hides him behind a white with a sound (DiveMan's scene, bn6f cutscenescript_8085CD0), which the super bosses' falls follow. Found by reading those scripts in bn6f and matching their bytes in Gregar, the animations' frames read from Gregar's sprite tables. Verify: `CYBERWORLD_AUTOPILOT=1 build.py shot --scene emu --run-depth 3 --seed 7 --net-biome 11 --guardian 1 --dev god,onehit` and frames 1450-1475 (the beam, then HeatMan in his lighter colours) and 4280-4300 (his log-out); `--guardian 12` BlastMan's. Text commands `FD 01 song` (0xFF stops) and `FD 0A` (the area's music) play music without opening the box, `F5 01` hides the mugshot. NPC commands: 0x04 jump if flag set, 0x09 invisible, 0x0D/0x0E talking on/off, 0x1F/0x20 collision off/on, 0x28 sound, 0x2A 0xC0 wait for the animation's end, 0x2E an animation started afresh, 0x31 alpha (0x10 opaque down to 0x02, 0 off: BN6 never uses it for a Navi). Mugshot indexes equal the Navi's overworld sprite in list 6 (HeatMan 0x47 .. ElementMan 0x56, ProtoMan 0x3B, MegaMan 0x37); overworld Navi sprites have 8 standing animations (0-7), the log-ins at 0x19-0x20 and log-outs at 0x21-0x28 (above) and, for Gregar's own five, a signature move at 24. |
| Net maps' backdrops and animations (`src/gfx/xbackdrop.c` xbackdrop_map; RomLayout `map_enters`, `map_anims`) | `0x03093C` the internet groups' EnterMapGroup routines (bn6f EnterMap_InternetMapGroupJumptable), `0x030998` their LoadGFXAnims routines (InternetMapGroupLoadGFXAnimsJumptable), one a group from 0x80; BN5 `0x031414`, `0x031468` | bn6f's EnterMap clears the GFXAnim states (`sub_8005F40`), calls LoadGFXAnimsForMapGroup (`0x08030A30`, through the second table) and then the group's EnterMapGroup (`0x08030A00`, the first): both read the map's entries in three tables of the group. Its LoadGFXAnims (`push {lr}; lsls r1, r1, #2; ldr r0, =lists; ldr r0, [r0, r1]; bl LoadGFXAnims`) starts the map's list of GFXAnim scripts (LoadGFXAnims `0x08002354`; a list ends 0xFFFFFFFF, the scripts are the battle backgrounds' below, each in the slot its +9 byte names of the 18 at `0x020094C0`). Its EnterMapGroup's first call is its LoadBGAnim (`push {r4-r7, lr}; mov r5, r10; ldr r5, [r5, #0x3C]; ldrb r1, [r5, #5]; lsls r1, r1, #4; ldr r7, =scroll`), which takes the map's 16-byte scroll entry (two callbacks for SetBGScrollCallbacks, the HBlank's, display bits OR'ed into DISPCNT: 0x800, BG3 on) and its BGAnimData record (`ldr r0, =records; ldrb r1, [r5, #5]; lsls r1, r1, #2; ldr r0, [r0, r1]; bl LoadBGAnimData`, `0x080304E8`): the backdrop on BG3, its tiles at VRAM `0x06008020`, its 32x32 map at offset 0x1800 of the BG maps and its palette into bank 0 (`0x03001960`), over the map's own bank 0. Central Area (0x90) keeps its lists at `0x08072A84`, scroll entries at `0x08072AF4` and records at `0x08072B28`; Seaside Area (0x91) `0x0807661C`, `0x08076690`, `0x080766C4`; Green Area (0x92) `0x08078F2C`, `0x08078FA8`, `0x08078FCC`; Sky Area (0x94) `0x0807BAF8`, `0x0807BBA4`, `0x0807BBD8`. Central Area 1 runs four scripts: its backdrop's tile animation (36 tiles to `0x06008040`, staged in buffer 5) and colour cycles in banks 9, 10 and 7. BN5 keeps all of it so, found by the same code: LoadGFXAnimsForMapGroup `0x080314F8`, the EnterMapGroup dispatch `0x080314C8`, LoadBGAnimData `0x08031038` (its records name the map's absolute destination, `0x03001800`), LoadGFXAnims `0x080022C4`, palette RAM `0x03003960`, and one staging buffer for every number (`0x0200E960`, its table at `0x08001A28`). Its groups' lists, scroll entries and records: ACDC Area (0x90) `0x0807FF08`, `0x0807FF6C`, `0x0807FF90`; Oran Area (0x91) `0x080817AC`, `0x0808181C`, `0x08081840`; SciLab (0x92) `0x08083AD0`, `0x08083B44`, `0x08083B78`; End Area (0x93) `0x080861B8`, `0x08086244`, `0x08086278`; the Undernet and Nebula Area (0x94) `0x08088800`, `0x080888F0`, `0x08088954`. These are in BN5's own map numbers, which the engine's 0x90:n (its internet groups' descriptor lists following each other) reach as the group whose list starts nearest before n's descriptor: 0x90:7 is End Area 1, 0x93:0, and 0x90:13 Nebula Area 2, 0x94:3. ACDC Area 1 (0x90:0) cycles banks 7 and 8 and turns its backdrop's diamonds (21 tiles to `0x06008040`, eight frames of 16), End Area 1 banks 5, 8 and 12 and 56 backdrop tiles, Nebula Area 2 banks 1 and 5 and its static's two tiles at `0x06008020`; ACDC Area's and End Area's backdrops scroll as BN6's surface areas' (BN5's `0x08001952` is BG3's diagonal scroll, as BN6's `0x080019D0`; `0x08001A24` a bare return, as BN6's `0x08001AB4`), Nebula Area's stands still. A copy moves a palette script's RAM by the two records' palette addresses, stages 50 tiles or fewer in buffer 0 and more in buffer 3 (BN6's table at `0x08001AB8`), takes the host record's map offset and palette RAM, and for each of the other game's two scroll callbacks the host entry's one with the same instructions (else the host's own), with the host's HBlank callback (IWRAM code: BN6's `0x03005CD9`, BN5's `0x03005DD9`). Verify: in romlab, warped into each BN5 map, its BG palette (`0x05000000`, dumped every 32 frames) changes in those banks and no others; with BN5 beside BN6, `CYBERWORLD_EMU_DEBUG=1` prints "xbackdrop: ... map 0x90:0's backdrop and animations for 0x90:0" (0x93:0 for 0x91:1, 0x94:3 for 0x92:0), a capture of `--net-biome x0`, `x2` or `x3` over a few hundred frames shows BN5's backdrop moving and the GFXAnim slots at `0x020094C0` naming the copies, and BN6's own areas (`--seed 11` dresses none) draw frame for frame as without BN5. |
| Battle backgrounds (`src/gfx/backdrop.c`, `xbackdrop.c`; RomLayout `battle_bgs`, `battle_bg_anims`, `battle_bg_scroll`, `battle_bg_refs`) | `0x082058` BGAnimData per background, `0x0822E0` animation lists, `0x081EF4` scroll callbacks | bn6f `sub_8080DA0` loads the battle background by id through three tables (Falzar `0x08080F98`, `0x08081220`, `0x08080E34`); in Gregar each sits 0x10C0 bytes later, found by the literal `0x08082058` at `0x082054` and by searching for 7-word BGAnimData records (tiles, VRAM `0x06000020`, map, `0x800`, palette, `0x03001960`, `0x20`). A record's tiles are a word count and the offset of their LZ77 data; the map is width and height bytes (32, 32) with LZ77 at +12; the palette a size word then 16 colours. Ids 0x03, 0x10, 0x14 and 0x15 have no palette and take it from their animation. The animation lists (ending `0xFFFFFFFF`) hold GFXAnim scripts: +0 and +4 parameters, +8 command (0 palette copy: destination, size; 4 tile copy: ROM tiles, VRAM destination, +10 tile count), then (parameter, delay) pairs ending 0 or looping at 1; a tile copy's parameter is a list of u16 tile numbers with flips in bits 10-11, which is how the digit blocks and the Undernet's static move. Each id's scroll is the second word of its 16-byte callback entry: `0x080019B5` diagonal (BG offsets -8/16 and -4/16 a frame), `0x080019ED` up, `0x08001A11` down, `0x08001A35` slow right, `0x08001A59` fast left, `0x080019B1` none. Every id decoded with its animations shows its area's battle background (Central's rings of digits, the Undernet's static, the Aquarium's fish). The loader reads the three tables through literal words, the same three in both its routines (Gregar `0x081E60` and `0x081EAA`) and nowhere else: `0x082054` the BGAnimData table, `0x081EEC` the animation lists, `0x081EF0` the scroll entries; another game's backgrounds go after BN6's 22 in copies of the tables, the words pointed at them (`src/gfx/xbackdrop.c`). A tile animation stages its tiles in the EWRAM buffer its fourth byte names, through the table at `0x08001AB8` (the same in Gregar and Falzar): 0 and most others `0x0200DF40` (1280 bytes before `0x0200E440`, and BN6's own largest animation, 50 tiles, runs on into it), 2 `0x0200E580` (192), 3 `0x0200E640` (2304, 72 tiles), 11 `0x0200E440` (bn6f `ewram.s`). BN5 keeps its backgrounds the same way, its loader at `0x08C338` (found by the same instructions: push, a BL, `mov r4, r0`, `lsl r1, r4, #4`, `ldr r7, [pc]`, `add r7, r7, r1`, the three loads from r7): scroll entries at `0x08C39C`, BGAnimData at `0x08C5E8` (and `0x08C570`, which a mode check picks instead; the two differ only for ids 12-14, 18, 20, 26 and 28), animation lists at `0x08C9DC`. Where a battle's record leaves the background at 0xFF, its routine at `0x08CAE8` takes it from a byte per map, a list per internet group at `0x08CB1C`: ACDC Area (0x90) 8, Oran Area (0x91) 9, SciLab (0x92) 10, End Area (0x93) 15, the Undernet and Nebula Area (0x94) 24 and 25. Its records name the map's absolute destination (`0x03000800`, where BN6's add 0x800 to a base) and its own palette RAM (`0x03003960`, BN6's `0x03001960`), so a copy takes BN6's for both; its scroll callbacks are its own code, the same as BN6's (the diagonal scroll, the still one), so a copy takes BN6's entry whose callbacks match instruction for instruction. Verify: with BN5 beside BN6, `CYBERWORLD_EMU_DEBUG=1` prints "xbackdrop: background 8 ... as 0x16" (10, 15 and 25 as 0x17-0x19), and a battle on `--net-biome x0` to `x3` (`--seed 2`, the autopilot finds one) shows BN5's background: ACDC Area's blue diamonds, SciLab's scrolling binary, End Area's red field of diamonds, Nebula Area's still stars. |
| The PET menu (`src/director/pet.c`) | `0x0200DF20` ePETMenuData; `0x08120B1C`, `0x08120F26`, `0x08120F2A`, `0x0200A272` | The PET's data (bn6f `ePETMenuData`): +0 its state (4 taking input, 0x10 an entry chosen), +4 the cursor (0-7 the entries, ChipFolder to Save, 8 EXIT), +5 bit 0 open, +9 a count that holds input off, +0xF unused (zeroed as the PET opens), where the engine leaves the entry it took. Its state table's entry for state 4 (bn6f `off_811ED3C`; Gregar's literal at `0x08120B1C`, holding `0x08120B91`) is the input handler, which tests flag `0x1706` on Comm (6) and Save (7) and only plays sound 0x69; a hook on that handler's first instruction (`0x08120B90`, issue #33; until then the literal pointed at a routine of the engine's own at `EMU_FREE` + 0x300), with the cursor on Save, or on Comm where its label is Options (the row below), input not held off and A among the keys just pressed (bn6f `eJoypad` +2, the u16 at `0x0200A272`, `BN6_KEYS_PRESSED`), clears A there and writes the cursor (7 or 6) to +0xF, so the game sees no A there. The grey (bn6f `sub_811F11C`; Gregar `0x08120F18`: `17 20 06 21 0E F7 22 F9 04 D0 E2 69`) stores a grey colour for Save (`0x03001B58`) and then for Comm (`0x03001B56`); the engine makes the first store, `11 80` at `0x08120F26` (after `E2 69` and a load), a no-op (`C0 46`), so Save is drawn lit, and with Options the second, `11 80` at `0x08120F2A` (`BN6_PET_GREY_COMM`, after `A2 69`, `ldr r2,[r4,#0x18]`), so Options is too. The Gregar code is Falzar's shifted by 0x1DDC here. Found by reading the PET's jump tables in bn6f (JumpTable811F7A0: Comm `HandleCommMenu81291E8`, Save `HandleSaveMenu8132B88`) and matching the bytes in Gregar. Verify: in a run, the PET shows Save and Options lit; A on Options opens Options over the PET, A on Save closes the PET and "Run saved" shows. |
| The PET menu's labels (`src/director/pet_options.c`, `src/gfx/letters.c`; docs/PET.md, Options) | `0x086C8058` (`BN6_PET_LABEL_TILES`), `0x08120A9C` (`BN6_PET_LABEL_LOAD`), `0x08121394` (`BN6_PET_LABEL_MAPS`); VRAM `0x0600D340`, `0x0600DE80` | The menu's entries' labels are pictures: 90 tiles, 4bpp, uncompressed (`0xB40` bytes; bn6f `dword_86CA10C`), which the PET's load list (bn6f `off_811ECB4`, Gregar `0x08120A90`, its literal at `0x08120A8C`; built into RAM and queued by bn6f `sub_811EC68`) copies by its second entry (`0x08120A9C`: source, `0x0600D340`, size `0xB40`) to tiles `0x29A`-`0x2F3` of BG character block 2 (`0x06008000`, which BG0 reads in the PET: BG0CNT `0x1C08`). Each entry's label is 9 x 2 tile entries, palette 15, through a table of 9 pointers (bn6f `off_811F5B8`, read by `CopyBackgroundTiles_onNaviSelect_811F54C`; Gregar `0x08121394`): ChipFolder `0x086C9168` to Save `0x086C9264`, Comm `0x086C9240`, and ninth Status `0x086C9288`, which BN6 draws in MegaMan's row for another Navi. Column 0 is the box's left edge (tile `0x29A` in every label), columns 1-7 the text (a short label's free columns the blank tiles `0x2A1` above and `0x2AA` below), column 8 its right edge; the text starts at the label's x = 8. Label n's letters are colour 5 + n of the palette bn6f `sub_811F11C` writes at `0x03001B40` (`0x537F`, the cursor's `0x7BFE`, a greyed one's `0x5A7A`; Status shares MegaMan's 8), shaded in colour 3 on the box's 2: a pixel right of, below or below-right of a letter's colour is shaded (all 9 labels measured: 9 of their 882 shaded pixels differ from that rule, hand-drawn). Cut where a column holds none of a label's colour, ChipFolder is 10 letters, SubChip 7, MegaMan 7, KeyItem 6 (its ey one), Comm 4, Save 4, Status 6. The engine sets Options from them (O Comm's C closed by its mirror image and shaded by the rule; p and i ChipFolder's 4th and 3rd; t and s Status's 2nd and 6th; o Comm's 2nd; n MegaMan's 7th), five columns where Comm has four: a copy of the 90 tiles at `FREE_PET_OPTIONS` (`EMU_FREE` + `0x169000`) with Comm's eight tiles (`0x2DC`-`0x2E3`) the word's and two after them (`0x2F4`, `0x2F5`, VRAM `0x0600DE80`-`0x0600DEBF`), the load list's entry pointed at it with size `0xB80`, and Comm's fifth column's two entries (`0x086C924A`, `0x086C925C`) those two tiles. VRAM `0x0600DE80`-`0x0600DFFF` read zero in the PET's menu and on the map after it closed (`tools/play.py`'s `dump`, 23 October 2026), the menu's own tiles end at `0x0600DE80`, BG0's map starts at `0x0600E000`, and closing the PET restores only `0x0600D400`-`0x0600DBFF` (bn6f `copyMemory_8001850`), as BN6 leaves its own tiles from `0x0600DC00` on the map. Found from bn6f's `sub_811EC68`, `CopyBackgroundTiles_onNaviSelect_811F54C` and `sub_811F11C`, then Gregar's bytes: the tiles' first 16 bytes (`00 00 00 00 00 11 11 11 00 21 22 22 00 21 22 22`) once in the ROM, the label table by the shift of the code (`0x1DDC`), the data's (`0x20B4`) by the tiles' place. Verify: the word at `0x08120A9C` is `0x086C8058` and `0x08120AA4` `0xB40`; the 18 entries at `0x086C9240` are `F29A F2DC-F2DF F2A1 F2A1 F2A1 F2A2 F2A3 F2E0-F2E3 F2AA F2AA F2AA F2AB`; in a run START shows Options in the menu's own letters on Comm's row, lit. |
| The PET's KeyItem and E-Mail words (`src/director/pet_text.c`) | `0x0873B938`, `0x0873BD88`, `0x086D10D8`, `0x086CE598`, `0x0812A2F8`; literals `0x08042034`, `0x0804761C`, `0x081265FC`, `0x0812A870`, `0x0812A9C4`, `0x08129E90`, `0x0812A12C` | The key items' names are an uncompressed archive, an entry per id (bn6f `TextScript873D9FC`, Gregar `0x0873B938`), read through four literals (the KeyItem screen's `0x0812A870`, the text scripts' `0x08042034`, the shop's `0x0804761C`, SubChip's `0x081265FC`); their descriptions an LZ77 one (bn6f `CompText873DE4C`, Gregar `0x0873BD88`) that the KeyItem screen unpacks to `0x0201C1B0` and runs from the literal at `0x0812A9C4` (bn6f `off_8128BE8`). The mails' senders (script 2n) and subjects (2n + 1) (bn6f `CompText86D3154`, Gregar `0x086D10D8`, unpacked to `0x02025700`, the list's rows reading the literal at `0x08129E90`, bn6f `off_81280B4`) and bodies (bn6f `CompText86D0614`, Gregar `0x086CE598`, unpacked to `0x0201C700`, run from the literal at `0x0812A12C`, bn6f `off_8128350`) likewise. BN6's unpacking (`decompAndCopyData`) skips the first 4 bytes of an unpacked archive; the script runner (`chatbox_runScript`) reads its offsets at the pointer it is given, so each literal can point at an uncompressed archive in the free space instead. A mail's body is in the mail screen's own form, not the map chat's: a face set up with `FC 06 F5 00 face F5 02 m F5 03 00` (m 01 draws it as a photo, 03 green), the box opened with `E8 10`, pages `E7 00 F2` apart, the end `E7 00 EE FF 00 00` (bodies begun with the map chat's `F5 00 face E8 00` drew in the chat's style, the MESSAGE band garbled; compare story mail 0x30 on a fresh profile). A mail's row (bn6f `byte_812851C`, Gregar `0x0812A2F8`, 4 bytes an id: its icon, bit 7 Lan's HP's mailbox; its places in the sender and subject sorts); a mail given (bn6f `addMail_802f238`, done by the engine) sets flags `0x1CA0` + id (received) and `0x1D20` + id (unread), pushes its id onto the 128-byte list at `0x02006530` (newest first) and adds one to the u32 at `0x02001140`; `0x1DA0` + id is read. A key item's count (eToolkit KeyItemsPtr, a byte an id) shows only with its check (the pointer at eToolkit +0x78: `0x020004E0`[id] ^ 0x55 in Gregar, `CheckKeyItem`); the list walks ids 0x00-0x6F in the order of bn6f `byte_81286C4`, and ids 0x47-0x4F, 0x56-0x5F and 0x61-0x67 are nameless and unused. Found by reading the PET's screens in bn6f and matching each address's bytes in Gregar. Verify: in a run, KeyItem lists NaviCode and LibCard with their words, and E-Mail a mail from Dad per guardian met, its body the guardian's warning. |
| Field objects and the in-battle Mystery Data (`src/core/formations.c`, `src/director/encounter.c`) | entity kinds; `0x0211A0` reward rows | A BattleSettings entity's high nibble is its kind (bn6f `off_80073A0`): 0 MegaMan, 1 an enemy, 2 a green Mystery Data, 3 a rock (500 HP), 8 a rock cube or ice cube (its byte 2: 1 rock, 3 ice; 200 HP), 9 a guardian statue, A a metal cube (never broken); rocks and metal cubes share two slots a battle. A Mystery Data's entry is `20 yx TC 00`: at the battle's start (bn6f `spawnMysteryData_8007424`) it appears where byte 2 beats `RNG % 15` (the whole byte is compared: tier 1 and up always), and takes one of four rewards from row T (eight u16, each twice, picked by `RNG & 0xE`) of the table at `0x0211A0` (bn6f `byte_80211A0`, at the same offset in Gregar, found by its bytes: row 0 `E8 43 E8 43 01 C0 01 C0`, 1000 zenny, 1 BugFrag, Boomer J, Tornado T; read by the literal at `0x0DC284`). A u16 reward is a chip (bits 14-15 0: id bits 0-8, code bits 9-13), zenny (1) or BugFrags (3). It has 1 HP and breaks at any hit from either side; still there when the battle is won, its reward is the battle's second (`sub_80AA8E0`). BN6's random battles hold one in 61 of 779 records, none in the story's fixed battles. The engine keeps an original's objects on its list, a Mystery Data in about one battle of three that hold one, its row the record's own (0 or 1) with the run's rewards (a blue Mystery Data's). Verify: `--dev gem` puts one in every random battle; win without touching it and the results give a second reward (980 zenny on layer 3), hit it and it breaks. |
| Battlefields and their panels' types (`src/emu/xfields.c`, `src/core/xbattles.c`; issue #82) | BN6 `0x0800D730` the battlefields (`BN6_FIELDS`, `BN6_FIELD_COUNT` 237, `BN6_FIELD_SIZE` 12), named by the one literal `0x0800C8D4`; BN5 `0x0800BD64` (`BN5_FIELDS`), by its literal `0x0800AF84`; the flags each panel type gives, BN6 `0x081D7E20` (`BN6_PANEL_FLAGS_OF`, 13 types; Gregar's IWRAM code `0x03007920`, copied from there) and BN5 `0x0800BCE8` (`BN5_PANEL_FLAGS_OF`, 11) | A BattleSettings' byte 0 names its battlefield. BN6's battle setup (bn6f `sub_80079F0` -> `sub_800BF88` -> `sub_800C4BC`, the same addresses in Gregar) takes `BN6_FIELDS` + 12 x n (`sub_800C6D6`: `movs r1,#0xC; muls; ldr r1,=table; adds`, found in both ROMs by those bytes; a whole-ROM word scan finds each table named by its one literal) as three u32 rows (rows 1-3), a nibble a column (columns 1-6, the low nibble first), and stores the nibble as the panel's type (+2, `BN6_PANEL_TYPE`, and +6) with no table between: the number in a field is the type. BN6's 237 fields end at its code (`0x0800E24C`). Both games' type numbers compared: their flags by type (`_object_updatePanelParameters` ORs the type's word into a panel's flags) agree for 0-4 and 6-8 (none 0x18000, hole 0x14000, plain 0x10010, cracked 0x10050, poison 0x10110, 6 0x10410, 7 0x10810, 8 0x11010); BN5's 5 carries 0x10210 (BN6's 9-12, its conveyors), its 9 0x12010 (BN6's 5, holy: both games' damage halving checks it, BN5 `0x0800C80C` / BN6 `0x0800E3F0`), its 0xA 0x30010, which no type of BN6's carries. The code agrees by number for 4 poison (BN5 `0x08016C9A` / BN6 `0x0801A1AA`: 1 HP every 7 frames), 6 grass (a Wood object on it healed, BN5 `0x08016CDC` / BN6 `0x0801A1D2`; a Fire hit burns it plain, BN5 `0x08016D32` / BN6 `0x03007704`), 7 ice (an object not of Aqua slides, BN5 `0x08013548` / BN6 `0x08016754`), 3 cracked (object_crackPanel writes 3 then 1, BN5 `0x0800AFF0` / BN6 `0x0800C938`). Not for 8: BN5's is lava (`BN5_PANEL_LAVA`: a non-Fire object on it takes 50 and it cools plain, `0x08016DA2`; an Aqua hit cools it, `0x08016D48`), BN6's 8 another panel, a volcano (an Aqua hit cools it, but in the differential MegaMan stepped onto it unhurt where BN5's took 50, and an attack object of type 7 rose from it); and BN5's 0xA is sea (`BN5_PANEL_SEA`: a Fire object on it hurt as on poison, `BN5_PANEL_STEP` at `0x08016C9E`; `0x08016EAC`, which tests it too, is called by nothing), none in BN6. Both are BN6's free types 13 and 14 where `src/emu/xpanels.c` brings them into BN6's engine (BN5's lava and sea in BN6's engine, below), and BN5's fields with them, the Undernet's and Nebula Area's 0x73-0x76 (lava and plain) and End Area's 0x42-0x45 (sea and grass), are written as BN6's 0x56, 0x5F, 0x61, 0x65 and 0x66, 0x67, 0x69, 0x6A (the next of the free fields below; none where xpanels.c could not install); the 25 others its areas' records name are each BN6's own of the same panels (0x00, 0x1B as BN6's 0xC9, 0x20 0xBD, 0x52 0xDE, 0x54 0xDF, 0x5B 0x71, 0x5F 0xE7) or written, as the core starts, over a field of BN6's that no battle names (0x15 as 0x09, 0x16 0x0D, 0x21 0x0E, 0x22 0x0F, 0x24 0x11, 0x27 0x1D, 0x28 0x22, 0x29 0x23, 0x5C 0x24, 0x5E 0x25, 0x62 0x28, 0x63 0x2F, 0x64 0x30, 0x65 0x31, 0x6B 0x46, 0x6E 0x55, 0x9B 0x63, 0x9C 0x64; and, with lava and sea, 0x73 0x56, 0x74 0x5F, 0x75 0x61, 0x76 0x65, 0x42 0x66, 0x43 0x67, 0x44 0x69, 0x45 0x6A; docs/EMULATION.md). Free fields found by every BattleSettings of BN6's: its random battles' four tables (`RomLayout.encounters`, real-world and internet groups), the two lists of fixed battles (getBattleSettingsFromList0's literal `0x0800A940`, 26 records at `0x080B06E0`, and List1's `0x0800A944`, 1), and a whole-ROM scan for 16-byte records whose +0xC names an entity list (MegaMan first, 0xF0 ending it): 76 of the 236 past field 0 named by none (the run's own battles name field 0 or a formation's). Verify: `build.py xdiff field_poison field_grass field_ice` (BN5's own battle and BN6's on the field, every panel's type and flags compared each frame: poison and grass PASS over 600 frames, MegaMan on poison losing 1 HP each 7 frames in both; on ice one press slides MegaMan two rows on the same frames in both, a smoke spec); the lava trial, a spec of `"field": "73:8B"` (BN5's 0x73 against BN6's own 0x8B of the same nibbles), `"mm": "12"`, `"keys": [[40, "RIGHT", 2]]`: BN5's MegaMan 50 HP down at t46, BN6's none (`field_lava`, the same against BN6's 0x56 with lava brought in: PASS); `build.py xrecords` names each record's field as BN6's; the xrecord step on `811DE10` (holes at columns 3 and 6 of rows 1 and 3) and `812153C` (grass), each a picture of BN6's battle in BN5's layout. |
