| Enemy stats (`src/core/data.c`, `enemy_stats`) | `0x00F260` | bn6f `enemy_getStruct2`, whose literal at `0x00F25C` points here: three pointers by actor type (virus, navi, player), each to a pointer per ai, each to 6-byte records per version: u16 element << 12 \| HP, version, flags, u16 element << 12 \| damage (the value the AIs read into `oBattleObject_Damage`). Checked: Mettaur 40/80/120/160 HP, 10/20/40/60 damage; BlastMan 400/800/1400; Colonel (navi ai 18) 1200/1600/2000. The engine sizes random battles and guardians by it (docs/PROGRESSION.md). |
| Enemy traits (`src/core/data.c`, `enemy_traits`) | `0x00F230` | bn6f `enemy_getStruct1`, whose literal at `0x00F22C` points here (the routine at `0x00F214`, the same bytes as Falzar's there): three pointers by actor type (virus, navi, player), each to a pointer per ai, each to 8 bytes: two sprite indexes, three unknown, then at 5 its element of BN6's second wheel and at 6 the ones that hit it twice as hard, a bit each (bn6f `ELEM_*` flags: 0x10 Breaker, 0x20 Wind, 0x40 Cursor, 0x80 Sword). An enemy's spawn (bn6f `sub_8016F56`) ORs byte 5 into its object's element and writes byte 6 to its collision data's `SecondaryElementWeakness`; `getSecondaryElementWeaknessMultipler` doubles a hit whose element shares a bit with it. A chip's attack carries the second-wheel element its icon shows (its record's byte 6, bn6f `CHIP_ELEM_*`: 5 Sword, 6 Cursor, 8 Wind, 9 Break; byte 4, the first wheel's, is 0 on those chips). Checked: navi ais 3 SlashMan 0x80/0x10 (Sword, weak to Breaker), 4 EraseMan 0x40/0x20 (Cursor, Wind), 8 TenguMan 0x20/0x80 (Wind, Sword), 9 GroundMan and 10 DustMan 0x10/0x40 (Breaker, Cursor), every other guardian 0/0, as the Rockman EXE Zone wiki's BN6 boss list has them; virus families 25 DarkMech 0/0x40 and 26 SnakeArm 0/0x20, the rest of the run's 0/0. In a battle (a throwaway hook on `object_subtractHP`, r0 the damage), a Sword (80) took 160 of TenguManEX's HP, his element byte 0x20, and the buster 1. The Net Dealers answer a guardian by it (issue #39, docs/PROGRESSION.md). |
| Starting state | `0x0213AC` folder, `0x0210DD` navi base stats | `initNewGameData_8004DF0` copies the 30 u16 entries (chip \| code << 9) at `0x0213AC` (literal at `0x0050E8`) into folder 0: Cannon A/B ×2 each, AirShot ×2, Vulcan1 ×3, MiniBomb B/L ×2 each, Sword ×4, WideSwrd ×2, CrakShot ×2, AreaGrab, Atk+10 ×2, Recov10 A/L ×2 each, BusterUp, WhiCapsl. Base stats are 16 bytes per navi (literal at `0x013CB4`), byte 0 × 2 the HP: MegaMan 100. Read for docs/PROGRESSION.md only. |
| Navi chips (`src/core/data.c`, `navi_chip`) | chip ids | Each navi has V1, EX and SP chips in a row: ProtoMan 224, HeatMan 227, ElecMan 230, SlashMan 233, EraseMan 236, ChargeMan 239, SpoutMan 242, TomahawkMan 245, TenguMan 248, GroundMan 251, DustMan 254, BlastMan 257, DiveMan 260, CircusMan 263, JudgeMan 266, ElementMan 269, Colonel 272 (names from the chip archives; Falzar's have library flag 1). A beaten guardian gives the one of its version through `ts_item_give_chip` (`F4 10`, u16 chip, code, count). |
| Chip folders (`src/director/director_folder.c` set_start_folder and note_folder_codes, `src/core/meta.c`) | eToolkit `+0x48` (`0x02002178`); ROM FolderTable of 6 preset folders at `0x0213AC`, `0x0213E8`, `0x02158C`, `0x021604`, `0x0215C8`, `0x021640` | A folder is 30 u16 entries, chip record number in the low 9 bits and code in the high 7 (26 for *), 0x3C bytes; the game's first folder starts at the pointer, the next ones follow. bn6f's `GiveFolder` (`ts_start_give_folder`) copies a preset folder there with `sub_8021AB4` (`CopyWords` of 0x3C bytes to `oToolkit_S_Chip_2002178_Ptr` + slot * 0x3C), then registers each chip in the Library. The six presets decode to BN6's starting folder (the first) and five others (a stronger start, a tutorial one, a Navi-chip one, a support one and a Program Advance one). Located from `GiveFolder` in bn6f's `asm36.s` and the toolkit's struct; checked by decoding the presets through the chip records and names, and by writing a folder at a run's start and reading it back on the PET's CHIP FOLDER screen (30/30, the chips and codes as written). The engine reads the first folder back for its codes as each layer is made (a run's MegaMan has only that one). |
| CircusMan's tent (`src/director/director_see.c` see_tent) | a battle object's +0x09 (`CurAction`), +0x12/+0x13 (`PanelX/Y`); the field's panels at `0x02039AE0` | bn6f's BattleObject: +0x08 `CurState`, +0x09 `CurAction`, +0x0A `CurPhase`, +0x10 `CurAnim`, +0x12/+0x13 the panel it stands on (1-6 across, 1-3 down). CircusMan's actions: 0x08 standing, 0x0A the clap, 0x0B the lion, 0x0C the tent. As his action turns to 0x0C, BN6 lights the panel MegaMan stands on then (its `PanelData` +1, set by `object_highlightPanel` and cleared within the frame, so it never reads set between frames; the panels are 32 bytes each at `0x02039AE0` + (y × 8 + x) × 32, the literal of `_object_getPanelDataOffset`, Gregar `0x1D7E54`, the same address as Falzar's), for a few frames under his feet; 28 frames in, CircusMan's panel becomes that one, and the tent drops on it. Measured in god mode with seed 3, biome 8, depth 3 (a replay is frame for frame the same), a step UP begun at each delay after the action's first frame: at 0, 28 and 32 frames MegaMan left the panel and took nothing; at 36 and later he was held in the tent (its 3 damage every 8 frames for three seconds), from 40 on without moving. His fade, the tell the briefing named, shows at 26-30 frames. The director marks the panel over MegaMan's sprite for 36 frames of the battle's clock. The field's panels are 40 by 24 pixels, the first row 72 down the picture (a capture at 3x). |
| Battle rewards (`src/director/encounter.c`, `src/dev/economy_report.c`) | `0x080AC718` rows, `0x08020B9C` busting table, `0x0200A00C` the reward, `0x0203489E` the busting level | As a battle ends (its state's first frame, `0x0800B428`, then `0x0802C8FA`), the chooser `0x080AC150`/`0x080AC180` (bn6f `sub_80AA8E0`/`sub_80AA910`) takes one of the enemies spawned at the start (BattleState `[0x020093C8]`: +9 their count, +0x54 their u16 ids), then an entry of its row: `0x080AC718` + id x 0x28 (the literal at `0x080AC714`), 20 u16 entries, 0-9 while MegaMan's HP is over 3/8 of his max and 10-19 below, five pairs each; the busting table (12 levels x 16 bytes, 1-3 pair 0 up to S pair 4) picks the pair, a coin (the RNG's bit 8) its entry. An entry is `0xFFFF` for none, else bits 14-15 its type: 0 a chip (id in bits 0-8, code in 9-13, 26 for *), 1 zenny (bits 0-13), 2 an HP recovery, 3 BugFrags. The pick lands at `0x0203CBE4` (+8) a frame later, then at `0x0200A00C`, which the results screen shows and battle's exit gives (GiveChips `0x08021AEE`). Found in romlab from the results screen's reward back to the chooser; verify: two Mettaurs at Busting S give Rflectr1 A, and with their row's chip entries recoded to P in the core's copy, the results screen and the pack show Rflectr1 P. The engine rewrites the second entry of each pair of a battle's enemies, from the ROM's own, in the folder's codes (TankCan1 G became A, and a battle paid TankCan1 A), at the chooser's entry, by a hook (Battle events by hook, above). The busting level the chooser is given is BattleState +0x1E (`BN6_BATTLE_BUSTING`, `0x0203489E`: 1 to 10, 11 for S), which bn6f's sub_802C8FA reads and passes in r2 to sub_80AA8E0, whose sub_80AAA98 takes the table's row of that level (16 bytes), one byte of it by the RNG's bits 16-19, the pair's offset in the row (0, 4, 8, 0xC or 0x10), the RNG's bit 8 the entry within it, and 0x14 more with MegaMan's HP at 3/8 of his max or less. Read in Gregar, its rows give level 1-3 pair 0; 4 pair 0 or 1, half each; 5 pair 1 or 2; 6 pair 2; 7 pair 2 or 3; 8 pair 3; 9 pair 3 and one in 16 pair 4; 10 pair 3 or 4; 11 (S) pair 4. The economy report (docs/ECONOMY.md) picks rewards by it. Verify: the run log's `bust N reward XXXX` after a won battle (docs/DEVTOOLS.md, Run log): twelve autopilot runs read levels 1 to 10, and 11 with `--dev god,onehit`; a guardian's reward was his act's chip-entry zenny (`4258`, 600 zenny, act 1) or his row's own (`43E8`, 1000). |
| The battle's DeleteTime (`src/director/director.c`, `director_see.c`, the rival's duel, docs/RIVAL.md) | `0x020348C0` | A u32 counting the frames a battle has run: 0 as the first Custom screen opens, then one a frame while the battle runs, held while the Custom screen is open and in the pause (and through "BATTLE START!"). The results screen shows it as the DeleteTime, seconds and hundredths cut: 616 frames read "0:10:26". Found by dumping EWRAM (0x02000000-0x0203FFFF) in a battle at the Custom screen and twice 60 frames apart after it, and keeping the words that rose by 60 in both steps and were 0 at the first; of the six, this one alone had counted 84 of the 256 frames the Custom screen and the battle's start had taken, and it held while the Custom screen was open again. Verify: win a battle and compare its frames / 60 with the DeleteTime the results screen shows. |
| Chips owned (`src/director/director_folder.c` own_folder_chips) | Toolkit `+0x7C` (a byte per chip id), keys `0x020008A0`, XOR `0x17` | BN6 marks each chip it gives (bn6f `encryption_applyPack_8006e70`, Gregar `0x08006E70`: the chip's byte in the table Toolkit `+0x7C` points at becomes its key at `0x020008A0` + chip XOR a constant, `0x17` in Gregar where Falzar has `0x81`), and tests it (`encryption_testPack_8006e84`, `0x08006E84`, the same XOR). A chip whose byte does not match is taken for a cheat's: the battle draws it blank, with no name, no code and no effect. GiveFolder (`0x08021AB4`) marks the folder's chips as NEW GAME gives it; a chip only written into the folder has no mark. Found from GiveFolder's per-chip step (`sub_8021B2A`); verify: a folder of LongSwrd, Barrier and PanlGrab written into RAM plays blank in every hand, and with their marks set they draw and play. |
| Running from a battle (`src/director/encounter.c`) | BattleSettings +8 bit `0x20`; `0x08026E98`, `0x08026EC8` | L on the Custom screen (`0x08028C80`, unless the battle mode is 1) runs the escape talk: it calls GetBattleEffects (`0x0802D246`, the record's options word) and tests `0x20`: set, "Lan, should we run?" with Yes and No (the roll after Yes at `0x08026F1A`, from record byte 1 against the max HP, per-try chances at `0x08027020`; an escape sets the battle's result to 3); clear, "Lan, this is no time to run away!" and back to the chips (TextScriptBattleRunDialog `0x086ED710`). BN6's 779 random battles use `0x49E2` or `0x1C9E3`, with the bit; its story bosses `0x1C9C3`, without; its roaming SP Navis `0x1C9E3`. Nothing else reads the bit (every GetBattleEffects call checked). Found in romlab on a story BlastMan EX battle: its word (`0x189D7`) refuses, patched to `0x189F7` or `0x49E2` it asks; verify: a guardian's battle says "no time to run away". |
| Custom gauge (`src/director/director_keys.c` custom_buffer) | `0x020352A0` | u16, full at 0x4000; an L or R pressed in battle up to 90 frames before it fills is given when it does, and again every 20 frames until the gauge drops (a press the game let pass while MegaMan fired or flinched). Located by bn6f `SetCustGauge` (ROM `0x0801DFA2`): its literal pool holds `eStruct2035280`, and it stores the gauge at +0x20. Verify: in battle it climbs from 0 to 0x4000 in about 512 frames and drops to 0 as the Custom screen opens. |
| The autopilot's fight (`src/emu/autopilot.c`, `CYBERWORLD_AUTOPILOT=1`) | `0x02034880` eBattleState (`BN6_BATTLE_STATE`, +1 `BN6_BATTLE_PHASE`); `0x020364C0` the Custom screen (`BN6_CUSTOM_SCREEN`: +6 the hand's size, +7 the cursor, +8 the chips picked); `0x0203CDB0` the battle's folder (`BN6_BATTLE_DECK`); `0x02039AE0` the field's panels (`BN6_FIELD_PANELS`, 0x20 each: +2 type, +3 alliance, +0x14 flags); `0x0203CFE0` the T3 BattleObjects (`BN6_T3_OBJECTS`, 32); a BattleObject's +0x09 action (MegaMan's `0x08` ready), +0x14/+0x15 future panel, +0x2A chip | Named after bn6f (`ewram.s`: `eBattleState` at `0x2034880`, `eT3BattleObjects` at `0x203cfd0` before its 0x10-byte link header; `include/structs`: S20364C0's `ChipSelectCursorPos` and `NumChipsSelected`, PanelData's `Type`, `Alliance` and `Flags`, BattleObject's `FuturePanelX/Y` and `Chip`), and checked in EWRAM dumps of seed 3's first battle taken at the Custom screen, BATTLE START!, the fight and the pause. eBattleState +1 reads 8 while the Custom screen is open and slides out, 0x0C from BATTLE START! on (the pause too). The Custom screen's cursor is 0-4 along the top row and 10 on OK, the hand its first `+6` entries of the folder (u16 chip \| code << 9, 26 the *; the chips it showed, S D * B D, were those entries' codes), which drops a chip once used and keeps the ones not picked at its head. A panel's type is 1 a hole (where CrakShot broke one), 2 plain, 4 poison (MegaMan lost 1 HP every 7 frames on one), 0x0B and 0x0C the conveyors that push left and right; flag `0x40000000` is set on the panel an enemy's attack is on (a Mettaur wave's, a torpedo's, SpoutMan's water) and on no other. A T3 object of alliance 1 is an enemy's attack: DiveMan's mines carry the panel they land on as their future panel from the throw (seed 1's guardian, the one MegaMan stood on), StarFish's bubbles run down a row and turn onto MegaMan's. MegaMan's +0x2A is the chip he uses next (0xFFFF none), kept until the chip's action ends (a MiniBomb's 35 frames in the air); his action reads 0x08 when he can act, 0x10 in a step, 0x12-0x16 using a chip (an A pressed in a step was taken as the step ended, so the autopilot presses A and R at 0x08 only). Verify: `CYBERWORLD_AUTOPILOT=1 python3 build.py shot --scene emu --seed 3 --frames 15000` wins seed 3's depth-2 Piranhas at 100 HP (`runlog.txt`), where the button rhythm the autopilot had used, still `weak`'s, picked an Atk+10 alone and was deleted. |
| Chat font (`src/layer/scripts.c`) | `0x6AACAC` glyphs, `0x043C74` widths | The chat box's font: 16x12 4bpp glyphs of 0x60 bytes per character code (12 rows of 8 bytes, low nibble first), and a width byte per code. Codes 0x40-0x44 are the version marks [RV] [BX] [EX] [SP] [FZ], two letters stacked in one cell; the engine redraws them as the two capitals side by side in the core's ROM copy. Located by the chat renderer (bn6f `sub_3006F8C`, copied to IWRAM from ROM `0x1D7488`): its literal pool holds the font, `unk_200BEA0` and the width table. Verify: code 0x0F is an `E` whose top row reads `0x11`, and its width byte is 8. |
| Talking reach (`src/layer/npc.c`) | `0x0809F164` the player's 8 facing probes (bn6f `byte_809DC2C`) | 24 bytes each: s32 x, y, z offsets (16.16), u8 radius, u8 z reach, two u32 flags; in the order UP, UP+RIGHT, RIGHT, DOWN+RIGHT, DOWN, DOWN+LEFT, LEFT, UP+LEFT, 8 units ahead with a radius of 8. Located by the first entry's offsets (0x0005A826, 0xFFFA57DA), the only match; verify: the next entry reads 0x00080000, 0. The engine makes them 24 ahead and 20 wide in the Net, and puts BN6's own back in the real world: A's checks look along the same records (bn6f `sub_809D9E0`: on A, the record of Lan's facing, its offsets added to his place, then `checkCoordinateTrigger_8031a7a`), and 24 ahead read past a check a cell deep (AsterLand's request board, every check in Lan's room: issue #96). Verify: in Lan's room `0:place -16 -12 7`, a step up and A, BN6's line on the pajamas. |
| Map-name label (`src/director/director_layer.c` map_label) | `0x08033F34` literal of RenderMapName (bn6f `RenderMapName_8033EE8`), `0x08120E9C` the PET's (bn6f `off_811F0C0`), `0x086C92AC` TextScriptMapNames | The routine runs on every map frame and draws name `sub_811F290()` of the archive its literal points at, twelve characters at the bottom right (short names padded with `0xB2`, which draws as `_`). Located by the literal pool's neighbours `0x02027400`, `0x0600D000` (the only match in the ROM); verify: the word at `0x08033F34` is `0x086C92AC`, an uncompressed archive of 244 twelve-character names (`CentralArea1` is 99). The engine points both at an archive whose every name is the run's label (the map's corner and the PET's PLACE line). |
| Mugshots (`src/layer/text.h`, `scene_title.c`) | `0x032598` mugshot pointers (bn6f `mugshotSpritePtrs`), text command `F5 00 id` | The id `F5 00` takes indexes this table, which is also sprite category 8, so `--sheet @8:0:96:PATH` draws every face. People: 0x00 Lan, 0x01 Mayl, 0x04 Chaud, 0x05 Dad; townsfolk faces are their list-5 sprite minus 0x20. Navis: 0x37 MegaMan, 0x3C Mr. Prog, 0x3E the Normal Navi (the Net Dealer), 0x42 the orange technician navi, 0x43 HeelNavi, 0x58 Beast MegaMan; other list-6 navis have the face of their sprite's number. Ids that point at bn6f `mugshotBlack` (0x84F6BBC) draw an empty black box in Gregar: 07, 1F, 21, 23-25, 28, 29, 30, 31, 34-36, 48, 4A, 4C-4E, 59, 60 (Falzar's navis among them). Verified by the decoded sheet and by counting `F5 00 id` in the Gregar text archives. The command takes the face's palette bank from a table of (id, bank) pairs ending in 0xFF at `0x044214` (bn6f `byte_8044244`, Falzar's address less 0x30; its literal sits beside 0x18C6 in the command's pool at `0x041D70`): 3D 3, 5C 9, 5D 12, 5E 15, 5F 18, 3F 1, 40 2, 57 3, 44 1, 46 1, 29 1, 31 1; any other id draws in bank 0. The portraits of Falzar's Navis (`src/layer/portrait.c`) take 30, 28, 34, 35, 36: a portrait at 31 drew in a second bank its one-bank palette lacks. A portrait is the HeelNavi's face (0x43: one frame, 30 tiles in four objects over 40 x 48, one 16-colour palette) copied, its tiles a 40 x 48 window of the Navi's battle sprite standing (list 0, `0x2E` + the navi, animation 0's first frame) and its palette that sprite's, one index given to a ground of the guardian's title-card colour, written at `EMU_FREE` + `0x166000` (docs/EMULATION.md). Verify: `--guardian 6`-`10 --run-depth 3 --talk intro:500`: each speaks with his portrait in his own colours. |
| The ROMs screen's faces (`src/launcher/launcher_art.c`; `sprite_get_rom` in `src/gfx/gfx.c`; `XRomLayout.face` in `src/core/rom.c`; issue #97) | BN6 sprite list 8 (the mugshots), 0x37; BN5 list 8 (its sprite lists at `0x03272C`, as the row on BN5's Navis has them), 0x45 | A cartridge's label shows its game's face, decoded from the player's own ROM as the screen opens and kept only as a texture: BN6's MegaMan (0x37, as the Mugshots row has it) and BN5's Colonel (0x45, the first of his two faces; 0x5B is the other). BN5's list 8 holds its mugshots as BN6's does: people from 0x00, Navis from 0x30 (0x30 MegaMan, 0x34 ProtoMan, 0x45 Colonel). The browser's player draws the same two from the ROMs it keeps (`web/play/app.js`, `FACES`: the same lists and indexes, its own LZ77 and sprite decoding). Verified by drawing BN5's list 8 as a sheet, each index labelled (0x45 and 0x5B Colonel, the empty black boxes at 0x07-0x0A, 0x2A, 0x2C, 0x40-0x44 and 0x51-0x55), and by `--scene launcher` with both ROMs: the BN5 cartridge shows Colonel, the BN6 one MegaMan. |
| MegaMan deleted (docs/RIVAL.md, the netbattle; nothing written) | BattleSettings +8 bits `0x08`, `0x10`, `0x800000`; event flag `0x1733`; bn6f `sub_8007CA0` | What a lost battle does, looked for to make the rival's netbattle a loss without GAME OVER, and not found: bit `0x08` is a link battle (GiveChips and TakeChips after it; set, the battle waits on a white screen for a partner), `0x10` makes the HP full after any battle, `0x800000` after a lost one (result 2), and the deletion sets `EVENT_1733` and `EVENT_1735` unless bit `0x08`. With `0x800000` set, or `0x1733` cleared each frame, BN6 still starts its GAME OVER as MegaMan falls. Tested in headless captures (`--dev duels=2`, act 3). |
| Text commands for gifts (`src/layer/scripts.c`) | bn6f `text_script_commands.inc` | `F4 00 item count` gives an item (HPMemory 0x70), `F4 10 chip code count` a chip, `EF 1B program count color` a NaviCust program (from the game's own shop data, so the pair exists), `FC 03 13 00` sets full HP, `FA 00 index 05` prints a NaviCust program's name, entry `index` of its 75 names, which is the program id (shop data, `EF 1B`, both counting in fours: the game gives item id + 0x90) divided by 4 (bn6f `chatbox_8041FB4`: a buffered value goes (value - 0x90) >> 2; the id itself printed garbage past entry 74 and broke the gift's menu), `EF 07 item amount equal more fewer` jumps by a key item's count (the gate's none/one/two ScrtData lines). A choice of three is three `EB` options in a column (`EB 00 left<<4\|right up<<4\|down`) and `ED 07 flags j0 j1 j2 jB` (0x80 clears the box, 0x40 ignores B). Checked in headless captures: each option gives its item. |
| NaviCust (`src/layer/navicust.c`, docs/NAVICUST.md) | `0x13B22C` program records; RAM `0x0200431C` bug counts; key item `0x71` ExpMemry | Program records, 16 bytes per colour variant (program x 4 + variant): +0 category, +1 program part 0 / plus part 1 / either 2, +3 colour (1 White, 2 Yellow, 3 Pink, 4 Red, 5 Blue, 6 Green; 0 absent), +4 bug type, +8 and +0xC the shape and compressed shape: pointers to 49 bytes, seven rows of seven cells, nonzero where a block stands (SuprArmr 7 blocks, one over two rows of three; Custom1 6; HP+50 2 in a column; `navicust_shape`). The boards at `0x13CFCC`, `0x13D0AD` and `0x13D18E` are 15x15 maps of the 4x4, 5x4 and 5x5 boards (1 a cell, 3 the frame); the command line is their third row. Checked against the NaviCust screen: SuprArmr and Custom1 cannot share the 4x4 board without a bug (a playtester's, and `navicust_pack`), and share the 5x4. Found through the literal pool at `0x13D56C` in Gregar's NaviCust code (Falzar's `StructArr_813944C`, the block shifted by 0x1DE0); verified by SuprArmr's variant 4 reading Red and Attack+1's variants 140-142 Pink, Red, Blue, and by the program a draft gives showing in that colour on the NaviCust screen. The bug counts are one byte per type (eToolkit +0x60, Gregar's toolkit offset table at `0x6CC8`); a part left hanging past the board's edge counted an HP bug there while RUN still said OK. `F4 00 71 01` gives an ExpMemry and the game regrows the board: the act 2 guardian's showed a 5x4 board afterwards. |
| The Custom screen's window (`src/director/director_see.c` director_draw_tent, issue #52) | RAM `0x02035292` (`BN6_CUSTOM_WINDOW`, eStruct2035280 + 0x12) | The window's slide: as the Custom screen opens it counts up by 0x0C a frame to 0x78 (its width, 120 pixels), and it is 0 while the battle runs; SELECT, which BN6 lets the player press to hide the window and see the field, sets it to 0 at once, and SELECT again back to 0x78. Found by printing eStruct2035280's first 48 bytes each time they changed through a battle's opening Custom screen and two SELECTs: it was the only byte to follow them. Verify: the same, in any battle. |
| NaviCust compression (`src/layer/navicust.c` navicust_shape_as, `src/director/director_board.c` shape_now, issue #54) | event flags `BN6_FLAG_COMPRESSED` (`0x2660`) + variant; Gregar `0x0813D580` the shape lookup (bn6f sub_813B7A0), its flag literal at `0x0813D5C8` | A program record's +8 points at its shape, +0xC at its compressed one (Custom1 6 blocks to 4, SuperArmor 7 to 4, UnderSht's program 7 to 5; plus parts the same). The shape lookup tests flag 0x2660 + the variant and reads +0xC where it is set, else +8; entering a program's compression code on the NaviCust screen (the toggle after its tenth button, Gregar's literal at `0x0813E180`) sets or clears the flags of all four of its colour variants at once, 0x2660 + program x 4 on, so entered again it undoes itself. A run starts from a fresh game, its flags clear. Found in bn6f's NaviCust code (`sub_813B7A0`, the code input's `SetEventFlagRange`), and the same bytes in Gregar (`d0b5 0e1c 041c ... 0822 0e49`, the literal 0x2660). MegaMan's fit checks read the shapes so (the draft, made with the layer before a CONTINUE's state is back, keeps the uncompressed ones). Verify: with Custom1 in the PET, `play.py` dev step `flags 9832 9835 1` (0x2668-0x266B; the step reaches every flag of eEventFlags' 1448 bytes, `BN6_EVENT_FLAG_BYTES`, bn6f's ewram.s) draws it in its four-block shape on the NaviCust screen; with it placed and SuperArmor's flags 0x2664-0x2667 set too, MegaMan says SuperArmor needs a program moved, where uncompressed it would not fit the 4x4 board at all. |
| Program Advances (`src/layer/rumor_lines.c`, the net's rumors) | `0x02BA60` (RomLayout `program_advances`) | 64 entries, then a table of pointers to some of them (`0x0802BB98`...). An entry: its chips' count (3, or 4 for MstrCros), its kind, its result's chip id (u16, 320-349, the Program Advances' records: GigaCan1, LifeSrd 339, H-Burst...), then kind 4 its chips (u16 each), sent in that order in one code (LifeSrd: Sword, WideSwrd, LongSwrd; MstrCros: FireHit3, AquaNdl3, ElcPuls3, RskyHny3), kind 0 one chip (u16), three of it in codes in a row (GigaCan1: Cannon). Found by searching the ROM for LifeSrd's id (read from the chip names' archive) beside Sword's, WideSwrd's and LongSwrd's (71-73) and reading on while the entries held their shape. The first 13 entries list their chips (kind 4) and the next 30 are codes in a row (kind 0): BN6's own recipes. The 20 after them list chips again, for results the first 43 already give: the BattleChip Gate's recipes (the Japan-only reader of physical chips), which the folder never makes (issue #56: a rumor told Cannon, Cannon, TankCan1 for GigaCan1, where the folder's is Cannon A, B, C), so the rumors read up to the first listed one after the codes in a row. Verify: the first entry is `03 04 53 01 47 00 48 00 49 00` (LifeSrd from 71, 72, 73), the fourteenth `03 00 40 01 01 00` (GigaCan1 from three Cannons in a row), the forty-fourth `03 04 40 01 01 00 01 00 0C 00` (GigaCan1 from Cannon, Cannon, TankCan1: the Gate's). |
| The All * helper (`src/director/director_folder.c` star_records and star_folder_pack, `src/core/data.c`, docs/META.md, issue #18; BN5's copy the guest battles' until issue #82's last round) | `0x08029606` (`BN6_PA_STAR_LIMIT`, written `0x2A03`, `BN6_PA_STAR_ANY`); BN5 `0x080252B2` (`BN5_PA_STAR_LIMIT`); the chip records' codes (`0x021DA8` + id x 0x2C, +0 to +3; BN5's at `0x01E210`); the pack, eToolkit `+0x4C` | The Program Advance check is bn6f `sub_8029520` (Gregar's at the same address): it splits the chips picked into ids and codes, and for each start and each entry of the table above calls the entry kind's checker. Kind 4's (`sub_802961A`) compares the ids alone: the codes were the Custom screen's to match, so chips in * make LifeSrd. Kind 0's (`sub_80295C8`) wants its chip three times and walks the codes back from the last, one lower each, a * taking the code expected and counted in r2; `cmp r2, #1` (`2A01`) at `0x08029606`, then `bgt` (`DC05`) to the failure, refuses more than one *. Gregar's bytes there are Falzar's; BN5's routine has the same instructions (`3E02 3D01 D1ED 2A01 DC05 2800 DB03` at `0x0252AC`, found by searching BN5's ROM for them). `cmp r2, #3` takes three. The records: GiveChips (`0x08021AEE`) counts a chip in the pack through getOffsetToQuantityOfChipCode (bn6f `0x08021C7C`), the code's place among the record's four codes, else the first: a pack entry is 12 bytes a chip, a count per code of the record's, then four u16 order stamps (bn6f `setUnkFieldOfChipCodeInChipPack`). A record of `1A FF FF FF` has one place, so a chip given in any code is counted in * and the folder's EDIT moves it in as *. Verify: with All *, three Cannon * staged as a first hand's first picks show "PrgAdvance" and GigaCan1 (with `0x2A01` left there, three Cannons fire one by one); the gift's TimeBom2 (codes C D E) reads TimeBom2 * in the PET's pack, and a folder's Sword moved to the pack and back reads Sword * there and in the folder. |
| NaviCust compression codes (`src/layer/navicust.c` navicust_code, Dad's Compression mail in `src/director/pet_text.c`, issue #50) | `0x13D302` (RomLayout `navicust_codes`): ten bytes a program, programs 0-47; Gregar's pointer to it at `0x0813E178`, beside the flag literal | Each byte one button of the code, as the offset of its joypad mask in the table after the reader (bn6f `byte_813C3A4`): 0 L, 2 R, 4 A, 6 B; a program with no code has 0xFF throughout (program 0, and the plus parts 35-37 and 41-46: Attack+1, Speed+1, Charge+1 and the HP+ programs). The NaviCust screen's code input (bn6f `sub_813C334`, with RIGHT held) steps through the program's ten on each new press and toggles its four compression flags on the tenth. Found as bn6f's `byte_813B522` (data/dat36.s), the table that routine reads, and the same bytes in Gregar (`FF` x 10, then SuperArmor's `04 06 00 00 04 04 00 02 06 04`). Verify: Custom1's row spells L B B R B A A L R R; held RIGHT on Custom1 in the NaviCust's list, those ten buttons draw its four-block shape. |
| Reg memory and RegUps (`src/net/net_pieces.c` layer_regup, `src/layer/layer_objs.c`, `src/director/director_board.c` reg_watch, issue #51) | RAM `BN6_NAVI_REG` (MegaMan's NaviStats +9, eNaviStats0 `0x020047CC`); key items `0x72`-`0x74` RegUP1-3 | A new game sets +9 to 4 (bn6f `initNaviStats_WithDefaultStatsMaybe_8013438`); giving a RegUp makes it again from the key items: 4 + RegUP1 + 2 x RegUP2 + 3 x RegUP3, at most 99 (bn6f `sub_803CFB0`). The folder screen shows it beside the folder's name ("4MB"), and its EDIT's Regular Chip takes a chip of at most that many MB. Mystery Data give a RegUp as a key item (`4 20 FF FF id`), as BN6's blue ones do. Verify: dev talk `regup` (RegUP3) on a fresh run: MegaMan says 7 MB, and the folder shows 7MB. |
| The TagChip system (`src/director/director_board.c` grant_spins, issue #51) | key item `0x0B` (`SCRIPTS_TAG_CHIP`) | BN6's key item names list it eleventh (bn6f TextScript873D9FC); held, the folder EDIT's SELECT offers "Choose TagChip" after "Choose Regular Chip". Given each run with the key item's check, as the Spins are, once Chaud's first clearance is earned. Verify: `--dev duels=1`, PET, ChipFolder, EDIT, SELECT: the menu holds both. |
| NaviCust's board (`src/director/director_board.c` program_off_board) | RAM `0x02004190` the placed programs; key items `0x90` + program x 4 + variant | A program MegaMan has is a key item, `0x90` + program x 4 + its colour variant (bn6f `sub_803D148`, which `GiveNaviCustPrograms` calls), and its count stays when it is placed. The board's programs are a list at `0x02004190`, 8 bytes each: +0 u16 program x 4 + variant, +3 column, +4 row, +5 turns (0-3); a 0 ends it. Found by comparing a playtester's saved states: before a session UnderSht's key item `0xC4` was 1 and the list empty; after placing it, SuperArmor and HP+100 the list held `0x04` (turned 180, turns 2), `0x34` at column 5 and `0xAA`, as his notes describe, with the key items unchanged. Check with `tools/romlab`: load a state with programs placed and dump both. The board itself is RAM `0x0200414C` (eToolkit +0x58 in Falzar's `sub_813B9B4`), a 7x7 grid, a byte a cell: the placed program's index in the list + 1, 0 none. BN6's compile (bn6f `sub_813BBD4`) lands a shape's 7x7 pattern at its column and row less 3, reads the command line as the grid's row 3, counts a bug for a part of record kind 1 (a plus part) there, and sees colours touch only inside the grid's inner 5x5 (the mask at `sub_813C624`). The 4x4 board stands at x 1-4, y 1-4 of it, the 5-wide at x 1-5. Found by printing it in a replay of a playtester's session: his pink HP+100 (variant `0xA9`, column 2, row 3) filled x 2-3, y 2-3, and the NaviCust screen showed it in the board's second and third columns and rows, over the command line, with a light HP bug counted. `navicust_bug_cause` names the rule from it. |
| Virus families moved into the battle engine: BN6's tables (`src/emu/xvirus.c`, docs/MULTIROM.md, Transplanted families; issue #82) | `0x0810A378` the eleven per-family tables (`BN6_FAMILY_TABLES`, `BN6_FAMILY_STEP` `0x80` apart, `BN6_FAMILIES` 32 words each); their literals `0x0810A370` (`BN6_FAMILY_REF_STATES`), `RomLayout.enemy_traits` and `RomLayout.enemy_stats` (`0x0800F230`, `0x0800F260`), `0x0810A374` (`BN6_FAMILY_REF_FRAME`), `0x0800F36C` (`BN6_FAMILY_REF_SPAWN`), `0x0800F388` (`BN6_FAMILY_REF_SPAWNED`), `0x0800F3A4` (`BN6_FAMILY_REF_PARALYSIS`), `0x0800F3C0` (`BN6_FAMILY_REF_FREEZE`), `0x0800F3DC` (`BN6_FAMILY_REF_BUBBLE`), `0x0800F3F8` (`BN6_FAMILY_REF_FLINCH`), `0x0800F414` (`BN6_FAMILY_REF_DRAG`); `0x0810A953` the hooks' empty entry (`BN6_FAMILY_NULLSUB`, `mov pc, lr`) | The battle engine finds a virus's code and records by its AI index (AIData +1, the third byte of its enemy id's triple in `RomLayout.enemy_ids`) in eleven tables of 32 words, 0x80 apart: 0 its state table (a Thumb pointer per CurAction: generic states 0-7, the family's own from 8), 1 struct1 (8 bytes: sprite list and slot, palette, element), 2 struct2 (36 bytes: HP and damage per version), then the hooks the engine calls by AI index: 3 each frame, 4 and 5 as it spawns and once spawned, 6 paralysed, 7 frozen, 8 bubbled, 9 flinching, 10 dragged. Each table is named by one literal (table 0's and 3's the dispatcher's, the others the virus word of a triple of tables, viruses', Navis' and the player's), each holding `0x0810A378` + k x `0x80`, read from the ROM; a scan of every word of Gregar finds no other naming tables 0-2. Found from bn6f's dispatcher and its tables' readers; they are grown to 256 entries past `EMU_FREE` (docs/EMULATION.md; the engine's room, below) and the literals pointed there, each checked first. Verified: in romlab a family at AI 32 with the tables grown spawned, hit MegaMan and was deleted (issue #82's research), and the control below plays frame for frame as BN6's Mettaur. |
| Virus families moved into the battle engine: ids, names, held chips and the delete count (`src/emu/xvirus.c`) | `RomLayout.enemy_ids` `0x0182C4`, the filler ids `0xBB`-`0xCC` (`BN6_FILLER_AI`, `BN6_FILLER_ID`); `0x0800ECEC` and `0x0802B3F0` naming `RomLayout.enemy_names[0]` `0x6EE400`; `0x080B05F0` (`BN6_HELD_CHIPS`); RAM `0x02000070` (`BN6_DELETE_COUNTS`); `0x0802D644` (`BN6_DELETE_COUNT`), `0x0802D652` (`BN6_DELETE_COUNT_READ`), `0x0802D65E` (`BN6_DELETE_RARE`) | An enemy id's triple (version, actor type, AI index; 3 bytes an id) gives a family's six versions consecutive ids (BN6 bumps an id by its version). Ids 0xBB-0xCC read (version, virus, 1), Mettaur's, and their names are blank to 0xC0 and none of the 875 battle records names them (issue #82's research): the first family took 0xBB-0xC0, room for one; the families now take ids from 0x200 in the grown triples, each checked to read the filler 0xBB's triple `(0, 0, 1)` first (the engine's room, below). The viruses' names archive (235 entries, `E6`-ended) is named by the two literals alone (a whole-ROM word scan) and stays BN6's: the families' names are in an archive of their own, by a hook on the name lookup (below). `BN6_HELD_CHIPS` holds a word per AI index for 35 of them (AI 1's is Mettaur's lists at `0x0810AF2C`, AI 32-34's TestVirs' at `0x0810ADD4`); two more 35-word tables at `0x08010E18` and `0x08011068` hold `0x080114D5` for all; all three are grown to 256 (below). The delete count: `0x0802D644` adds one to the byte at `0x02000070` + r0 (the AI index; its caller in the dying state, `0x0801662E`, gives the triple's third byte) up to 255, `0x0802D652` reads it (the rare versions' check at `0x080ABFA6`) and `0x0802D65E` (`push {r4,lr}`, from `0x08016636` for versions 4-5) returns unless event flag 0x1D8 is set (`0x0802F164`, a `tst`), then unless event flag 0x196 + AI is clear (`0x0802F168`) and AI is neither 10 nor 13, and then puts the AI index at `0x0203EAEA` through `0x0800AB2E` (r0 0, r1 0xA: byte 0xA of a 16-byte record at `0x0203EAE0`); it sets no flag. After the results `0x0802C2AE` reads that byte and, when it is not 0, `0x0802C2E8` runs the chat of new virus data by it (the archive at `0x08736058`). For AI 32 the count's byte lies past the 32 counts and the chat's index past BN6's, so hooks return from all three for 32 and up (found by a BL scan of Gregar for their callers, the rare path by its disassembly; in romlab AI 32's deletion wrote `0x02000090`). Verified: the control's differential, whose deletion leaves AI 1's count at 0 and `0x02000090` as BN6's; `tools/xfamily.py`'s delete spec with flag 0x1D8 set, where BN6's own Mettaur version 0 (id 0x01) raises `0x02000071` and version 4 (id 0x05) puts 1 at `0x0203EAEA`, and each CanGard version leaves both as they were (without the hooks, versions 0-3 raise `0x02000090` and versions 4 and 5 put 32 at `0x0203EAEA`). |
| Virus families moved into the battle engine: the ice block's size, and every other read by AI index (`src/emu/xvirus.c` ice_set) | `0x080EAF64` (`BN6_ICE_SIZES_REF`), the triple's virus word, naming `0x080EAF70` (`BN6_ICE_SIZES`, `BN6_ICE_VIRUSES` 30 bytes); its reader `0x080EAF46` | The ice block a frozen enemy is held in (spawned by `0x080EAF1C`, spawn index `0x89` among the battle's fourth kind of objects, RAM `0x02036870` on, `0xC8` each) takes its size as it starts: `0x080EAF46` reads the frozen object's enemy id, its triple (`0x080182B4`), and a byte at `[0x080EAF64 + kind x 4] + AI index` into the block's animation (+0x10, +0x11). The viruses' table holds 30 bytes (0 for most, 1 or 2 for eight of them); AI 30 and 31 already read on into the Navis' table at `0x080EAF8E`, and AI 32 read its third byte, 1, so a frozen moved Mettaur got another block than BN6's (0): seen in the differential's status spec at its first freeze (the block's animation `0x02036880` 0 against 1) and in its pictures. It is grown to 256 bytes at `EMU_FREE` + `0x332C00` (each of BN6's 32 AI indexes as BN6 reads it now, AI 0's in the rest; the engine's room, below) and the virus word pointed at it, checked first; a family's own goes in its slot, which must hold AI 0's: the recipe's number, or its game's byte for its AI index there. Found by the review of the control: every caller of the triple's reader `0x080182B4` (15 BLs; the triple table's one literal is in it) and every read of AIData +1 (the AI index) after a load of the object's AIData pointer was looked at: the per-AI reads a virus reaches are the eleven tables, `BN6_HELD_CHIPS` and the two 35-word tables beside it (`0x08010E18`, `0x08011068`: `0x080114D5` for every AI index to 34), the delete count (hooked) and this table; the others are for Navis or the player (kind 1 or 2) or keep the index as it is. Verify: `python3 tools/xcontrol.py --spec status`: both of its ice blocks size 0 in the native run and the moved; with the control's `.ice = 1` the run differs at the first freeze. |
| Virus families moved into the battle engine: the engine's room for them (`src/emu/xvirus_grow.c`; issue #82, every family at fixed numbers) | `0x080182C0` (`BN6_ENEMY_IDS_REF`) naming `RomLayout.enemy_ids` (`BN6_ENEMY_IDS_COUNT` `0x1C4` ids); `0x080AC714` (`BN6_DROP_ROWS_REF`) naming `BN6_DROP_ROWS` (`BN6_DROP_ROW_COUNT` `0x191` rows); `0x080B05E4` (`BN6_HELD_CHIPS_REF`), `0x08010E0C` (`BN6_AI_HOOKS_REF`) and `0x0801105C` (`BN6_AI_HOOKS2_REF`), triples whose virus words name `BN6_HELD_CHIPS`, `0x08010E18` (`BN6_AI_HOOKS`) and `0x08011068` (`BN6_AI_HOOKS2`), `BN6_HELD_AIS` 35 words each; the sprite lists' words `0x08031CC8`, `0x08031CCC`, `0x08031CD4` and `0x08031CD8` (lists 1, 2, 4 and 5: `BN6_SPRITES_VIRUSES_COUNT` 32, `BN6_SPRITES_NAVIS` 2 and `BN6_SPRITES_NAVIS_COUNT` 23, `BN6_SPRITES_ATTACKS_COUNT` 92, `BN6_SPRITES_LIST5` 5 and `BN6_SPRITES_LIST5_COUNT` 32, read past them into list 6's table to 0x7F, `BN6_SPRITES_LIST5_THROUGH` 0x80: the real world's people, Lan's family and the town's folk, are list 5's 0x20-0x7F, list 6's table read through list 5's word, so a grown list 5 keeps BN6's words to 0x7F and the families' list-5 sprites start at 0x80. Measured with a probe on `sprite_load` (`0x080026E4`: r1 the list × 4, r2 the number) over a walk through the town and two autopilot runs (seeds 3 and 5, 15000 frames each): BN6's code read a grown list past its copied words only there, list 5's 0x2C-0x38 from `0x0809FB58`; with list 5 cut at 32, every townsperson drew the placeholder, a white blob, on the New 3DS and on the desktop alike); `BN6_EFFECTS_REF`; the kinds' types: `0x08003C9C` (`BN6_T1_TYPES`, its word `0x08003224`, `BN6_T1_TYPES_REF`, `BN6_TYPES_T1_COUNT` 95), `BN6_T3_TYPES` (`BN6_TYPES_T3_COUNT` 213), `0x080042C8` (`BN6_T4_TYPES`, its word `0x08003230`, `BN6_T4_TYPES_REF`, `BN6_TYPES_T4_COUNT` 146), their table by kind `0x08003220` (its literal `0x0800321C`); `0x0800EC56` (`BN6_NAME_OF`); `0x080029CE` and `0x080029D0` (`BN6_PRELOAD_ID_LSL`, `BN6_PRELOAD_ID_LSR`); `0x080170A4` (`BN6_TRAITS_MODES`, its literal `0x080170A0`, `BN6_TRAITS_MODE_COUNT` 32) | Every table a virus family is found through by its AI index, its enemy ids, its sprites, effects or attacks is grown once a core, before any family's install, at fixed places past `EMU_FREE` (docs/EMULATION.md), so that each family has room at numbers of its own: AI indexes 32-255, ids 0x200-0x3FF (six one after another a family), sprite numbers, effect ids and types to 255. Each table is named by one word of the ROM, which is pointed at its copy, checked first: a whole-ROM scan of aligned words for each table's address finds `0x080182C4` only at `0x080182C0`, `0x080AC718` only at `0x080AC714`, `0x08010E18` only at `0x08010E0C` and that triple only at `0x08010E08`, `0x08011068` only at `0x0801105C` (its triple at `0x08011058`), `0x080B05F0` only at `0x080B05E4` (`0x080B05E0`), each sprite list at its word of the lists' list, `0x080E16D4` at `0x080E1970`, `0x08003C9C`, `0x08003EC4` and `0x080042C8` at `0x08003224`, `0x0800322C` and `0x08003230`, `0x080170A4` at `0x080170A0`; every read goes through it: the ids' reader `0x080182B4` (`movs r1,#3; muls; ldr r0,=table; adds`, no bound, 15 callers), the rows' `0x080AC708` (`movs r1,#0x28; muls; adds`, no bound, its four callers the reward pick's), the triples' readers (`0x08010DDA`, `0x08010DF6`, `0x0801102A`, `0x08011044`, `0x080B05C0`: the triple by the id triple's kind, then AI index x 4), the battle objects' dispatch each frame (`0x080031F0`: the kinds' table by the header's +2 & 0xF, then the type +1 x 4). Their ends: the ids where code begins at `0x08018810` (id 0x1C4 reads `70 B5 CD`, a push), the rows where the held chips' reader begins at `0x080B05C0`, each 35-word table where its triple's Navi table begins (0x8C bytes on), the first kind's types at 95, the third's at 213 and the fourth's at 146 (each followed by code, `push {r4-r7,lr}`), each list where the next begins. The copies hold BN6's entries, each checked against the player's ROM, and a fill past them: AI 0's entry by AI index, the placeholder sprite in the lists, effect 0's row, 0 (an empty type) in the types, and for the ids and rows past BN6's the filler id 0xBB's triple `(0, 0, 1)` and row (100 and 60 zenny, else Cannon *). A family's ids are past every id of BN6's (its Navis 0x100-0x190, the player's forms 0x1A0-0x1C3, the NameIDs its code tests, all below 0x200: issue #82's research, every load of an object's +0x28). The battle's spawn reads 12 bits of an id (`0x080073F4`: the entity's byte 2 and byte 3's low four bits) and a battle object keeps it in a u16 (+0x28), but two readers take fewer. The battle's sprite preload (`0x080029A8`, its one caller `0x08007A32`) reads `lsls r2,r2,#0x1F; lsrs r2,r2,#0x17`, 9 bits, and decompresses each entity's battle sprite where it is compressed into a cache of 12 below `0x02033000` (`0x0200DCA0`: the count, then the u16 keys, list offset << 8 | number), so an id from 0x200 is read as id & 0x1FF (0x200 as TestVirs, whose sprite is Mettaur's; 0x201-0x205 as Mettaur's versions) and that sprite decompressed for nothing: the two shifts are made `lsls #0x1C; lsrs #0x14`, the spawn's own 12 bits. The name lookup `0x0800EC56` hands back the viruses' archive, or the Navis' (`0x086EED00`) for an id past 0xFF (`cmp r0,#0xFF; ble`), and the id's low byte: a hook on its first instruction (a leaf ending `mov pc, lr`) hands an id from 0x200 the families' archive (`XV_NAMES`) and its entry there, id - 0x200; its one caller is the battle's name plates (`0x0801E59A`, the NameIDs `0x0800A998` reads with `ldrh`). Not grown: `BN6_TRAITS_MODES`, which a byte of BN6's families' own indexes (a family's mode past it is given 0) and the delete counts (32 bytes of RAM: hooks keep AI 32 and up out, above). Verify: `python3 tools/xcontrol.py`: with the control installed BN6's own Mettaur fights the same at every byte as with nothing installed, no difference at all in either spec, so BN6's battles read the grown tables as BN6's own; and the control, moved to AI 32 and ids 0x200-0x205, the same as BN6's Mettaur to the results, every byte accounted for (its id's three copies as u16s, its name plate's archive the families'); `python3 tools/xfamily.py --spec native`: BN6's Mettaur the same at every byte with CanGard installed; a battle of CanGard at 0x200 beside Piranha, Swordy and Champy (`0:foes 200@62/07@41/13@43/2B@51`, CYBERWORLD_BTRACE): the preload's cache holds their three sprites' keys (`0x402`, `0x404`, `0x408`), and with the two shifts left as BN6 has them a fourth, Mettaur's (`0x401`), decompressed for CanGard's id read as 0; its name plate reads "CanGard" (the hook's archive), and the results screen of CanGard V1 deleted its own row's 200 zenny (the grown row of 0x200, which before held the filler's). |
| Virus families moved into the battle engine: the second-level dispatch (`src/emu/xvirus_dispatch.c`, `src/emu/xvirus_grow.c` dispatch_make, `src/emu/xvirus.c` types_set; issue #82) | BN6: an object's type (`BN6_T1_TYPE`, +1) and slot (`BN6_T1_SLOT`, +3); the spawners `BN6_SPAWN_1` `0x08003320`, `BN6_SPAWN_1_LAST` `0x0800333C`, `BN6_SPAWN_3` `0x08003358`, `BN6_SPAWN_3_LAST` `0x08003374`, `BN6_SPAWN_3_FIRST` `0x08003390`, `BN6_SPAWN_4` `0x080033AC` (their common part `0x08003278`); `BN6_OBJECT_FREE` `0x08003458`; the dispatch types' entries `0x08003E0C` (the first kind's 0x5C), `0x0800420C` (the third kind's 0xD2), `0x0800450C` (the fourth kind's 0x91); RAM `BN6_DISPATCH_SIDE` `0x0203FF00` (`BN6_DISPATCH_SIDE_STEP` 0x20). BN5: `BN5_T1_TYPES` `0x08003958` (`BN5_TYPES_T1_COUNT` 95; `0x08003044` the first kind's types' word), `BN5_T3_TYPES` (`BN5_TYPES_T3_COUNT` 204), `BN5_T4_TYPES` `0x08003F60` (`BN5_TYPES_T4_COUNT` 145; `0x08003050` the fourth kind's types' word); BN5's spawners `BN5_SPAWN_1` `0x08003140`, `BN5_SPAWN_1_LAST` `0x0800315C` and `BN5_SPAWN_1_LAST2` `0x080031E8`, `BN5_SPAWN_3` `0x08003178`, `BN5_SPAWN_3_LAST` `0x08003194`, `BN5_SPAWN_3_FIRST` `0x080031B0`, `BN5_SPAWN_4` `0x080031CC` | The battle runs an object by its type, a byte: the runner (`0x080031E8`) takes the kinds' table by the header's +2 & 0xF and the routine at the type (+1) x 4, then `mov lr,pc; bx r0` with r5 the object (BN5's `0x08003014` the same). A moved family's own objects bring types of their game's that BN6's tables either run as another routine or lack room for, so one type of each kind runs whatever moved routine its object was spawned as. The type is written by the spawn alone (`0x080032BE`: `ldm r7!,{r0-r4}; strb r0,[r5,#1]`) and read by the runner and by the third kind's type 0x10, which looks for its own among the third kind's objects (`0x080C802E`, `cmp r2,#0x10`); the slot is written once a slot as the battle sets them up (`0x080034BE`) and read by the free (`0x0800346A`) and a few objects' own code. The spawners each take r0 the type, r1-r4 the object's four words and hand back r5 the object or 0: a common part (`0x08003278`) takes a free slot of the kind's 32, then each links the object into the run order after the object running, at the list's end or at its start (BN5's `0x08003098` and its seven, the same places: found as BN6's by their bodies, BN5's two of the first kind's end the same code). The dispatch types are dead in BN6 (issue #82's engine research): all 399 BLs to the spawners take an immediate type (394 in located code, 5 in helpers nothing calls), none of them the first kind's 0x5C, the third's 0xD2 or the fourth's 0x91, and no code writes a live object's type; 0x5C's and 0x91's entries hold their kind's type 0 routine (`0x080B9A5D`, `0x080E1885`; 31 and 17 other entries do too), 0xD2's is 0. Their entries in the grown types (the engine's room, above) are pointed at the dispatch's routine of the kind, each checked first to hold that placeholder. Its code is the project's own Thumb, made field by field (`xvirus_dispatch_code`): a dispatch type's routine reads the object's slot, its game's type from the kind's side table at that slot, and goes on to the kind's MOVED table's routine for it with r1 the type x 4 and lr the runner's, as BN5's runner calls; MOVED holds every entry the stray's routine (a tail call to `BN6_OBJECT_FREE`, which lets the object go as one ends itself) until a family names its type. Each call a moved family's code makes to one of its game's spawners goes to the dispatch's spawner of the same kind and place (`XV_SPAWN_*`, the recipe's calls), which reads the type's twin from the kind's TWIN table (a byte a type: BN6's own number, or `0xFF` where the type is moved), spawns that by BN6's spawner, or the dispatch type for a moved one, and then writes its game's number into the side table by the new object's slot (for a twin too, where nothing reads it). The side tables are 32 bytes a kind at `0x0203FF00` (the first kind's), `0x0203FF20` (the third's) and `0x0203FF40` (the fourth's), in the RAM the battle's setup clears every battle (`0x080071F2`, a fill of `0x0203CDA8`-`0x0203FFFF`) and nothing of BN6's reads or writes from `0x0203FB50` on (the engine research: watched over its runs, and no code literal points past `0x0203F7E0`; the fourth kind's list at `0x0203F750`, its count a byte, reaches `0x0203FB4F` at most; docs/EMULATION.md, BN6's RAM). A moved block that reads its own object's type (`ldrb rX,[r5,#1]`) is refused, so nothing of the moved code sees the dispatch type. Verify: `tests/test_emu.c`'s dispatch test runs the code on mGBA with a driver of its own: a moved type spawned as the dispatch type with its number in the side table and run as its MOVED routine (r1 its number x 4), a twin spawned as BN6's number, a stray let go as it first runs; `build.py xdiff` (CanGard's reticle, BN5's 0x25, through the third kind's dispatch in every `cangard_*` spec, the trace reading the side table: docs/DEVTOOLS.md); `--mutations`: a wrong MOVED entry, a wrong TWIN and a spawn left aimed at BN6's spawner each FAIL `cangard_v1`; `tools/xcontrol.py` and `tools/xfamily.py --spec native`: BN6's own battles the same at every byte with the dispatch made. |
| Statuses as a hit leaves them, for the status step (`src/director/director_dev.c` director_dev_status, issue #82) | battle objects' `BN6_T1_COLLISION` (+0x54), the collision data's `BN6_COLL_STRUCK` (+0x40), `BN6_COLL_PARALYSIS` (+0x1C), `BN6_COLL_FREEZE` (+0x2A), `BN6_COLL_BUBBLE` (+0x2C); `BN6_STRUCK_PARALYSIS` `0x08`, `BN6_STRUCK_SLIDE` `0x10`, `BN6_STRUCK_DRAG` `0x100`, `BN6_STRUCK_FREEZE` `0x10000`, `BN6_STRUCK_BUBBLE` `0x20000` | A hit's resolution leaves bits in the struck object's collision data (+0x40) and the status's timer; a virus's every frame (`0x0801B1C4`, the routine its state table's dispatcher calls with it) acts on them: `0x100` dragged (CurAction 5, the state word kept at +0x5C to come back to), `0x10` pushed along a panel (it slides, its state kept), then `0x0800E730` counts each timer down and acts on its bit while it runs: `0x08` paralysed (CurAction 4), `0x10000` frozen (CurAction 6, an ice block spawned round it), `0x20000` bubbled (CurAction 7). Found in bn6f's code at the same addresses in Gregar (the symbol map's byte-for-byte rows). CurAction 3 has no way in for a virus: all 32 of BN6's state tables hold `0x080166AF` there, which only calls the family's table-9 hook, and a scan of Gregar for a store of 3 into an object's CurAction finds none on a virus's path. Verify: the status step (`--input "0:status freeze 90"`, play.py's `status freeze 90`) in a battle: the virus held in an ice block for 90 frames, then on; `tools/xcontrol.py --spec status` lists CurActions 4, 5, 6 and 7. |
| The control family (`src/emu/xvirus_families.c`, `--dev xcontrol`; `tools/xcontrol.py`) | BN6's Mettaur, AI 1: `0x0810AFEC`-`0x0810B5EF` Mettaur's code, `0x0810AF00` Mettaur's struct1, `0x0810AF08` Mettaur's struct2, `0x0810AF2C` Mettaur's held chips, `0x0810B224` Mettaur's state table (13 entries) | The family's own code lies between its held chips and the next family's records (AI 2's struct1, `0x0810B5F0`). A recursive walk of Thumb code from its state table's own entries (and the code pointers its literals hold, which it stores and calls through `mov lr, pc; bx`) reaches 1276 bytes of code; the rest are 17 literal words in 12 pools, 5 jump tables (`0x0810B028` 4, `0x0810B114` 2, `0x0810B1BC` 3, `0x0810B320` 5, `0x0810B3FC` 2 entries), its state table (dead in the copy: zeroed) and three tables of numbers after it (`0x0810B258`, `0x0810B270`, `0x0810B276`), three more (`0x0810B090`, `0x0810B5C0`, `0x0810B5D4`) and four halfwords of alignment. Its 64 BLs call 32 routines outside it (BN6's own in the recipe, each through a veneer: 0x08001532, 0x0800A704, 0x0800AA06, 0x0800AA1A, 0x0800AA40, 0x0800CC86, 0x0800E29C, 0x0800E2CA, 0x0800E5E2, 0x0800E9DC, 0x0800EBD4, 0x0800ED90, 0x0800F322, 0x080103F8, 0x08011680, 0x08011714, 0x080126E4, 0x080165B8, 0x080165C2, 0x08016B02, 0x08016B36, 0x08016B72, 0x08016CE8, 0x0801A04C, 0x0801A152, 0x0801A15C, 0x0801A166, 0x0801BB1C, 0x0801BB46, 0x080C8554 the shockwave's spawner, 0x080E1932, 0x0810AA2A), its 4 within; no word of Gregar outside it names any of it but the tables' and `BN6_HELD_CHIPS`' (whole-ROM word and BL scans). Its states as BN6 has them: BN6's spawn (`0x08016381`) and flinch (`0x080166AF`) in 0 and 3, its own wrappers in 1, 2 and 4-7 and its own states 8-12; hooks all `BN6_FAMILY_NULLSUB`. Its ice block size is AI 1's, 0 (`BN6_ICE_SIZES`). Verify: `python3 tools/xcontrol.py` (CRC32 `0xD9CF832C` of the block checked as it is copied): the moved Mettaur the same as BN6's frame for frame in both specs, the fight and the statuses, every byte of EWRAM and IWRAM accounted for (docs/EMULATION.md, Testing). |
| Virus families moved into the battle engine: what a family draws, its attacks and struct1 (`src/emu/xvirus.c` art_set and traits_of; `src/emu/bn6_families.h`) | RomLayout.sprite_lists `0x031CC4`: list 1 (`BN6_SPRITES_VIRUSES`, `0x08031D24`) and its number 0x1E (`0x08031D9C`), list 4 (`BN6_SPRITES_ATTACKS`, `0x08031FA4`, `BN6_SPRITES_ATTACKS_COUNT` 92 numbers, its word `0x08031CD4`); `BN6_SPRITE_PLACEHOLDER` `0x084DC040`; `BN6_EFFECTS` `0x080E16D4` (`BN6_EFFECT_COUNT` 108) and its literal `BN6_EFFECTS_REF` `0x080E1970`; `BN6_T3_TYPES` `0x08003EC4`, its literal `BN6_T3_TYPES_REF` `0x0800322C`, its empty type `BN6_T3_FREE` 0xD1; struct1's fields `BN6_TRAITS_LIST`, `BN6_TRAITS_SPRITE`, `BN6_TRAITS_PALETTE`, `BN6_TRAITS_MODE`, `BN6_TRAITS_ELEMENT`, `BN6_TRAITS_WEAKNESS`, `BN6_TRAITS_SHADOW` (`BN6_TRAITS_SIZE` 8), struct2's rows (`BN6_STATS_SIZE` 6) | A virus spawns (`0x08016F56`) by its struct1: +0 a list's offset into the sprite lists (4: list 1) and +1 its number go to the sprite loader (`0x080026E4`), +7 says whether it has a shadow (0: `0x08002F90`, none; else `0x08002E3C`, which sets bit 0 of its sprite's byte +3), +5 is ORed into its element (`BN6_T1_ELEMENT`, `0x08016FA0`), +6 goes on as its second-wheel weakness, +4 indexes the 32 bytes at `0x080170A4` (`BN6_TRAITS_MODES`) in battle mode 6 alone (a family whose game's mode is past them, BN5's TestVirs 0x21 and Tuby 0x22, is given 0, as most of BN6's families have), +2 is the palette step (a version's palette is its version times it). BN5's spawn reads its shadow at +5 (`0x08013C08`), so a BN5 record copied as it is makes its virus Heat with no shadow; the installer lays it out BN6's way from the recipe's numbers after checking BN5's names the recipe's sprite, mode and shadow. A sprite list's numbers that point at the placeholder (a white dot) are free where no record names them: list 1's 0x00 and 0x1E (issue #82's research: a dump of every struct1, the viruses', Navis' and player's, and of the obstacles' rows at `0x08021220` names neither). List 4 (the battle's attacks and effects) is named by one word (a whole-ROM word scan finds `0x08031FA4` only at `0x08031CD4`, the sprite lists' fifth), list 5 after it; lists 1, 2, 4 and 5 are grown to 256 numbers (the engine's room, below), a family's sprite listed in the grown copy at a number that holds the placeholder there. The effects are drawn by the fourth kind of object's type 0: its +4 the effect id, times 4, from the table its one literal `0x080E1970` names (read at `0x080E18AA`; no other word of the ROM names it), 4 bytes each: a list's offset, a number, the animation, a palette added to the object's +6; 108 of them, the effects' code from `0x080E1884`, so it is grown to 256 (below), the ids past 108 free. The attacks (the third kind) run the routine of their type from the table `0x0800322C` names, read by their spawner `0x08003358`: types 0xD1 and 0xD2 are 0, and no spawn of an attack in the ROM names either (issue #82's research: a scan of the BLs to the spawner for the type set before them); in romlab Mettaur's shockwave moved to 0xD1 spawned and struck MegaMan (issue #82's research). The table is grown to 256 with the first and fourth kinds' (below): a family's attack of BN6's numbers would take a type whose entry is 0 in the grown copy, 0xD1 or one past BN6's 213, never 0xD2, which with the first kind's 0x5C and the fourth's 0x91 is the second-level dispatch's (below), which runs a family's attacks by their own game's types (issue #82's engine research: each dead in BN6, no spawn names it). Verify: `python3 tools/xfamily.py --spec fight`: CanGard drawn in its own sprite with a shadow, element 0, its reticle (the dispatch's type 0xD2, run as BN5's 0x25; list 4's 0x5C) and muzzle (effect 0x6C, list 4's 0x5D) drawn; `--spec native`: BN6's Mettaur the same frame for frame with the tables grown. |

A BL scan of both blocks finds 4 calls within them and 39 out, to 39 routines, each paired with BN6's by the symbol map and its body compared with calls and literals masked: 29 the same, 10 differing only where BN6's layout differs (collision data 0xA8 bytes apart where BN5's are 0xA0, its flags at +0x70 where BN5's are at +0x68, the battle's pause at +0xA where +9), where BN6 numbers its states two on (an attack's end writes CurAction 8, BN5's 6) or where BN6 adds a step; the hit's placement (BN5 `0x080167B0`, BN6's a thunk to IWRAM at `0x0801A00E`) and the presence update (`0x08016B1A`, `0x0801A018`) were taken on their role and are checked by running them. The immediates it changes: `0x081090C6` and `0x08109110` (`movs r0,#7` and `#8` before its attack's start: CurAction 9 and 10 in BN6), `0x08108FB6` (`adds r4,#0x57`, the muzzle's effect, BN5's list 4 0x3D: copied as BN6's 0x6C), `0x080C955A` (`movs r2,#0x24`, its sprite in list 4: 0x5C), `0x080C9636` (`ldr r0,[r7,#0x68]`: +0x70). The reticle's spawn keeps BN5's type (`0x080C975C`, `movs r0,#0x25`, then BN5's spawner `0x08003178`): its call goes to the second-level dispatch's spawner of the third kind (below), which spawns it as the dispatch's type 0xD2 with 0x25 in the third kind's side table, and the dispatch runs the moved reticle (until the dispatch, the immediate was made BN6's empty 0xD1). Its sound (0xAE) and its strike's effect (0x0E) are the same in both games (their song table entries and effect rows compared). Verify: `python3 tools/xfamily.py` (each block's CRC32 and each immediate checked as it is copied): in BN6's battle beside a Mettaur its states 8, 9 and 10, its reticle walking from its panel to MegaMan's and striking him for 10 (V1), its muzzle's blast; freeze, bubble, paralysis and drag each letting its reticle go; each version deleted, its MaxHP its struct2 row's (60, 120, 150, 180, 230, 280), BN6's delete counts unchanged. |

BN5 `0x080138AE` an object's end (a jump table's), BN6 `0x08016C4E` an object's end (a jump table's); BN5 `0x080165E4` a collision data's tail, BN6 `0x08019F44` a collision data's tail; BN5 `0x08016C3A` an object's element into its collision data, BN6 `0x0801A146` an object's element into its collision data (four more, issue #82's A2b) | A moved family's call goes to its routine's twin in BN6, and the generator compares each pair's bodies (`tools/xrecipe_thumb.py`: walked from the entry, calls and literals masked): the same, the same but for the offsets of loads and stores, or differing. A differing twin serves only once it is audited for what the family's code asks of it, each audit written with its conditions, which the generator checks on each family's code and turns into a decision where one fails. CanGard's nine since run against BN5's own frame by frame (the attack's end writes CurAction 8 where BN5's 6, BN6's two status states first; setAttack0 a routine of its own; BN5 skips a player of AI 0x17 for the counter time; the end lets the panel reservation go first; the collision data 0xA8 bytes where 0xA0, its hit flags at +0x70 where +0x68, the region's high nibble at +0x19; the placement's code in IWRAM). The twelve, read side by side: the panel's reservation (its reserver and count at BN6's +0x1C and +0x14 where BN5's +0x20 and +0x18, and an object bit only BN6's own code reads); the chip an object uses (a virus's path the same; BN6 adds a player's attack bonus on MegaMan's) and its numbers written for the attack (BN6 ORs in its chip record's element hit flag: right for a BN6 chip id, so only where the family's held chips are BN6's ids); on to the decide state (CurAction 8 where 6); an object put in a four-slot table (each game's own table, BN6 marking its own ids 0x49-0x4E and 0xEB); the collision panels highlighted (BN6 turns them by the object's +0x17, the same where the family never flips it); a hit's damage summed (BN5 doubles it in a Liberation Mission's late turns, which no battle of BN6's is); the sprite animated while the object lives (BN6 also holds it on flags 0x10000 and 0x80000000, the same where the family sets neither); object_crackPanel (the panel's fields at BN6's offsets, BN5's sea bit out of the masks); the panel's type set (BN6 times its conveyors 9-12 where BN5 times lava 8 and sea 10: the same for types 0-4, 6 and 7, which the family must keep to). The two of A2b: the arm's position (BN6 answers (0, 7) at once for its obstacles' ids 0xCD-0xFF, `0x08018812`-`0x08018818`, then as BN5's: the families' ids are past 0x200, MegaMan's 0x1A0), which BN5's temp attack object asks for its owner's; an object's end as Cactikil's and WuNote's objects of the first kind name it in their jump tables (BN6's lets the panel's reservation go first, `0x0801BB78`, as its own end does, and leaves out BN5's call `0x08053BF4`, which writes the object's HP into a Liberation Mission's record in that game mode alone, `0x020022F0` +8 = 2); the collision data's tail (its damage word's top bits as statuses: BN6's sets +0x0E 1 where BN5's 0 for bit 14, hands bits 13 and 12 to `0x0801A4D0` where BN5's sets +0x10 0x20 and 0x30, and has no bit 11), as BN6's own setup ends on it, which every family's setup already runs; an object's element into its collision data's +9 (BN6's keeps its low four bits, the same for BN5's elements 0-6). One differing twin is served by BN5's own body instead (`copy`: copied with the family's code, its calls through their twins), where BN6's fix of BN5's bug changes what the family does: the collision data's update (BN6 stores its second word through the collision data, where BN5 stores it through r4, the region number its caller passed, into the BIOS's ROM, where the write does nothing, so BN5's object keeps the target word its setup gave it; BN6 also ORs in the time stop's bit as its setup does): Marina's water changes its region, and `marina_v1_keys` showed BN6's fix. Verify: `python3 tools/xrecipe.py --report` (`.build/xrecipe/report.md`: each family's calls by class, each condition checked); `build.py xdiff` (every family's specs). |

Now each other MegaMan of a NetBattle (by his seat, `BN6_BATTLE_PLAYERS`' words: side 1's player, side 0's partner, side 1's partner) takes a room of BN5's size of his own at the pool's end as the chip AI first takes him (`darkinvs_room.h`: side 1's player's `0x02034740`, on entries 6 and 7; side 0's partner's `0x02034600`; side 1's partner's `0x020344C0`), its entries' bits set in `BN6_AI_POOL_USED` (no spawn takes or lays them out again until the next battle's set-up) and kept in `RAM_CHIP_AI_ROOMS` (`0x0203FFF4`), refused where an actor holds one (he is left out of the chip AI then). His own entry stays his: the copy's +0x7C names it, so BN6's let-go of him frees his own. A NetBattle where no MegaMan but side 0's player meets the chip AI is the battle it was.
