`0x080131A0` generic state 0, `0x080133C4` generic state 1, `0x080133CE` generic state 2, `0x080134A0` generic state 3, `0x08013824` generic state 4, `0x08013944` generic state 5; BN6's `0x08016380` its generic state 0 (of 0-7); `0x08104AC0` the shared virus code's start, `0x08104E88` the families' code's start, `0x08114000` the families' code's end; `0x0801629C` the panel patterns by region (32 words; BN6's `0x08019B78` the panel patterns' twin); `0x08005CD0` a sine table (BN6's `0x080065E0` the sine table's twin); `0x08005D50` a cosine table (BN6's `0x08006660` the cosine table's twin); `BN5_T1_TYPES`, `BN5_T3_TYPES`, `BN5_T4_TYPES`, `BN5_ENEMY_IDS`, `BN5_ENEMY_NAMES`, `BN5_EFFECTS`, the sprite lists and the song table | The generator derives each BN5 family's recipe from the two ROMs, numbers only (`src/emu/xvirus_bn5.c`): its blocks from a walk of its state table's and hooks' entries to the next family's struct1 (the last family's by what the walk covers), the data in them (pools, jump tables, tables of numbers, each by how the code reads it), its calls with their twins (the project's pairs, then the symbol map's by bn6f's names, then a BN6 routine of the same body whose tables' entries are the same too, then the same body where the routine's neighbours put it), the immediates and pool words that are numbers in the other game's terms (CurActions two on, NameIDs, effects, sprites, types, a collision data's offsets), its states, hooks, records, held chips (BN5's chip ids as BN6's of the same name, a chip BN6 has none of held out) and draws (sprites by list and number, effects, sounds by their songs' tracks and voices). BN5's virus dispatcher acts for two ranges of NameIDs before their state each frame (found by its compares on the NameID it loads, `ldrh rX,[r5,#0x28]`: Appley's and Dominerd's), which BN6's does for none of the families' ids: a decision for each. The three data tables are named by literals in the families' code; each's bytes compared with BN6's twin (the sine and cosine tables the same bytes, the patterns BN6's first 32 of 47 the same). Issue #82's A2b, every family installed (`tools/bn5_twins.py`): four tables of numbers a family's code reads ids from, each read by hand (`ID_TABLES`: its entry's size, the entries the family's code reaches, the fields that are ids, the call each id goes to), copied whole as raw data, each live entry's ids given BN6's numbers as byte fix-ups (`XvByte`): Zomon's two effects (`0x08108900`, read at `0x081088B2`-`0x081088B6`, spawned at `0x081088C8`), Whirly's two sprites (`0x0810B588`, list and number, loaded at `0x0810B558`), the fire column's kinds (`0x080D0C0C`, six kinds of 6 bytes, its sprite in list 3 at +0, the list its code's immediate at `0x080D0C6C`: MetFire spawns kind 0 alone, its spawner `0x080D0E2C` with r4 0 at `0x08106B42`-`0x08106B48`) and the records of the third kind's type 0x0B (`0x080C5DF4`, 16 bytes a kind, its sprite at +5 and +6 loaded at `0x080C5EEC`: Marina's water spawns kinds 7-10 by its version, `0x080D879C` read at `0x080D8778`-`0x080D877C`). BN5's image object (the first kind's type 5, `0x080B9A5C`-`0x080B9D10`), the engine's objects' module, is moved once for every track before any family (`tools/bn5_objects.py`): a call of a family's into a module that would be copied goes to the module's copy (WuNote's to the spawner `0x080B9CAE`, r4's low byte the kind, at `0x0810CFC4`-`0x0810CFC6`), its type is the family's as shared, and each kind it spawns must be one that table fixes (WuNote's 0x30, its sprite list 4's 0x21 at the families' list 4 number 0x6C), a family whose call to the spawner names a kind not fixed refused (`kinds_of`, read back from the call: a `movs`, or a `lsls` by 8 or more and the `adds` after it); an object type whose twin in BN6 reads a table by the object's kind that BN6 laid out otherwise is never a twin (`MOVE_TYPES`: the temp attack object, BN5's kinds at `0x080B9A5C`, BN6's at `0x080BA444`, read by `0x080B9CE0` / `0x080BA6D2`, the same code, the two agreeing on kinds 0-4 alone), so a family that spawns one moves BN5's code and table with it. Every type a family's objects come to be is decided once, for every track (`TYPES`: moved through the second-level dispatch unless BN6's type of the number runs the same code and draws the same sprites by their bytes; unreachable where only BN5's panel claims spawn it, behind BattleState+0x32 bit 0x40, which BN5's `0x0802D594` alone sets): moved the first kind's 0x05, 0x11, 0x16, 0x2F, the third's 0x0B, 0x24, 0x25, 0x3E, 0x56 (the fire column: BN6's draws list 3's 0x31 of other bytes), 0x61, 0x6A, 0x6F, 0x84, 0x87, 0x8F, 0xAC, 0xC1, the fourth's 0x78, 0x90; twins the third's 0x03, 0x3B, 0x4B, the fourth's 0x00, 0x11, 0x35; unreachable the fourth's 0x83 (its spawn sent to the dispatch's stray). The generator derives each type's decision anew and stops where it differs from the record; the numbers a family's code keeps as they are, each read by hand in BN6 bit by bit (`KEPT`: the alliance and support bits of a collision data's words and a panel's flags, `0x08000000`, `0x03800000`, `0x06800000`, `0x0A800000`, which BN6's own code tests the same way, e.g. `0x0810A9F2`, `0x0800F546`, `0x080F57CE`, `0x080C7386`, `0x080F1F26`, `0x080CD72C`, `0x080CD7AE`; a collision region's spawn words `0x0405FF00`, `0x0405FF10`, `0x04050101`, `0x04050401`, `0x0705FF01`, `0x06050A01`, BN6's own code passing the same to its twin `0x080C6BDA`, e.g. `0x080BEAC8`, `0x080C68E8`, `0x080D922C`, `0x0810F9A8`, `0x080CC928`, `0x080D1636`, `0x080D757A`, `0x080F0284`, `0x080BD392`, `0x080DC8F8`); and the dispatcher's two cases (`PRESTEP`, the families' shims and pre-steps, above). A CurAction compared (`ldrb rX,[r5,#9]` then each `cmp rX,#n` while rX holds it, past the conditional branches between: TinHawk's `cmp #2; beq; cmp #9`) is given BN6's number as a written one is. A chip id its code gives its object by hand (`strh rX,[r5,#0x2A]`, the chip it uses next, from a `movs`: Zomon's set-up writes its AreaGrab there, BN5 `0x08108932`-`0x08108934`, BN5's 117 as BN6's 163 by the chip registry) is given BN6's id in the immediate; none (`movs #0; subs #1`, Appley's at `0x0810D4D6`-`0x0810D4DC`) is kept, and any other such write is a decision. Tuby and AI 31-33 are not installed: no battle record of BN5's names them (every one of BN5's 386 enemy lists read: no id from 0xB5 on, and AI 31 and 32 have no ids). Verify: `python3 tools/xrecipe.py --check` (the generated file is what the generator writes); `build.py test` holds the generated recipes of the installed families to the installed ones, field for field. |
| Virus families moved into the battle engine: their shims and pre-steps (`src/emu/xvirus_shim.c`, `src/emu/xvirus.c` shims_set, prestep_set and prestep_run; `src/emu/bn6_families.h`, `bn5.h`; issue #82's A2b) | BN6: `BN6_BY_NAME_RANGE` (`0x0800EBD4`, object_getEnemyByNameRange), `BN6_COUNT_BY_NAME` (`0x0810A960`, the shared virus code's count of a NameID range's enemies), `BN6_VIRUS_NAMEID_CASES` (`0x0810A2A6`, the virus dispatcher's read of the virus's NameID), `BN6_VIRUS_STATE_STEP` (`0x0810A348`, the dispatcher past its cases), `BN6_CLEAR_FLAG2` (`0x0801A176`), `BN6_AI_INDEX` (an AI's data's +1). BN5: `BN5_BY_NAME_RANGE` (`0x0800CE54`), `BN5_COUNT_BY_NAME` (`0x08104B1C`), `BN5_VIRUS_DISPATCH` (`0x08104550`-`0x081045CC`, `BN5_VIRUS_DISPATCH_CRC`), its cases `BN5_PRESTEP_APPLEY` (`0x0810455C`, ids 0x9D-0xA2) and `BN5_PRESTEP_DOMINERD` (`0x08104588`, ids 0x19-0x1E), the routines they call `BN5_PRESTEP_TAKE` (`0x0800CD1E`) and `BN5_PRESTEP_TAKE_MIN` (`0x0800CD44`, to `BN5_PRESTEP_TAKE_END` `0x0800CD80`, `BN5_PRESTEP_TAKE_CRC`). Ours: `XV_PRESTEP` (`+0x333900`, 256 words by AI index) and each home's shims (`XV_HOME_SHIMS`, +0x130) | What a family's code asks of the engine that no changed immediate can give, made as a few instructions of the project's own Thumb from the recipe's numbers (`xvirus_shim.h`). A NameID range (`XvRange`): BN5's code looks enemies up by a range of NameIDs whose bounds it makes in instructions (`movs r1,#lo; movs r2,#hi`), and BN6's ids are past an immediate's 255: the call made a call to a shim that loads BN6's bounds (the family's first id and last) into r1 and r2 and branches on to the veneer of BN6's routine; the installer reads the BL's target in BN5's ROM first and takes only `BN5_BY_NAME_RANGE` or `BN5_COUNT_BY_NAME` (each with its BN6 twin: `BN6_BY_NAME_RANGE` takes the same arguments, `BN6_COUNT_BY_NAME` is the same code). A chip compare (`XvCmp`): `cmp rN,#id` and the `beq`/`bne` after it, where the chip registry gives the held chip an id past 255 (Lark's MetaGel and Blinder), made a call to a shim that compares rN with BN6's id and goes on where the branch would, every register but lr and the flags as the two instructions leave them (the generator checks the routine pushes lr). The pre-step: BN5's virus dispatcher acts for Appley's and Dominerd's NameIDs before their state each frame (found by its compares on the NameID it loads at `0x0810455A`, `ldrh r0,[r5,#0x28]`: `cmp r0,#0x9D` / `#0xA2` at `0x0810455C`, then Dominerd's at `0x08104588`), BN6's for its own ids alone (its cases from `0x0810A2A6`, the same load, to `0x0810A346`: `cmp r0,#0x49` first, none of the families' ids): a hook on `BN6_VIRUS_NAMEID_CASES` (a BKPT the hook table runs) reads the virus's AI index (`BN6_T1_AI_DATA`, +1) and, where `XV_PRESTEP` names a pre-step for it, jumps there (`HOOK_JUMP`); the pre-step is BN5's case ported instruction by instruction with the routine it calls (BN6 has no routine that does the same: its role twins `0x0800EA22` and `0x0800EA4E` clear its freeze and bubble bits too and keep the slide's), its collision data's offsets BN6's (the hit's damage at +0x80 where BN5's +0x78, its parts +0x82-+0x8C, its flags +0x70), its calls through veneers to BN6's object_clearFlag2 and PlaySoundEffect, and it goes on to `BN6_VIRUS_STATE_STEP`. The installer checks the dispatcher's and the two routines' CRC32 in the player's BN5 ROM before it ports them. Verify: `python3 tools/xrecipe.py --check`; `build.py test` (the shims made on byte buffers, their instructions read back, test_xvirus_shims); `build.py xdiff dominerd_v1 appley_v1 lark_v1 marina_v1` (a pre-step's family and a range's and a compare's, each the same over 1200 frames). |
| Virus families moved into the battle engine: their sounds (`src/emu/xvirus.c` songs_set and song_there, `src/audio/xsong.c` xsong_pack; issue #82's A2b) | BN6's empty song slots `BN6_SONGS_EMPTY_FIRST` (`0x26`) to `BN6_SONGS_EMPTY_LAST` (`0x62`) of `RomLayout.song_table`; the families' are the last three, `XV_SONG_SLOTS` (3) from `XV_SONG_SLOT_FIRST` (`0x60`), xsong's per-run themes `0x26` to `0x3F` (its `SLOT_LAST`) | A sound of a family's whose song BN6 has under another number or not at all is copied: the generator compares each of the family's sounds with BN6's songs by their tracks and voices (the song's bytes as `xsong_pack` packs them, its player aside) and gives each one BN6 has no song of a slot of BN6's empty ones (each a header of no tracks, shared: found by reading the song table, every entry from `0x26` to `0x62` pointing at the same empty header). The slots are issue #82's shared allocations, one BN5 sound one BN6 slot whatever track copies it (`SOUND_SLOTS` in `tools/xrecipe.py`): Powie's `0xC0` at `0x62`, Skarab's `0xB7` at `0x61` (BN6's `0xB7` the same tracks on another player: copied all the same, so the Navis' and Souls' copies of it land in one place), Flashy's and Eleogre's `0xBA` at `0x60` (one copy, Flashy's: Eleogre's recipe names the same slot). The installer copies a sound only where the core's slot still holds BN6's empty header; where it holds another, it must be the same sound of the same game (its first word the song's header's, another track's install of the shared slot first), else the install fails. It packs the song (its sequence, voice group and samples) into the family's art room and writes the slot's header and its player halves from BN5's own entry for the song (BN6's 32 players are BN5's: it plays on the player BN5 plays it on, as the Navis' copies through `xsong_install_sound` do; the slot must be the one `xsong.c`'s `BN5_SOUNDS` gives the sound); the family's `PlaySoundEffect` calls keep BN5's number, its immediate changed to the slot's. Once the families are in, each copy is noted with `xsong.c` (`xsong_found`), so the Navis' installs find it there and the differential reads the slot by BN5's number (`xsong_copy_of`). Verify: `build.py xdiff powie_v1 skarab_v1 flashy_v1 eleogre_v1 colonel_v1 shadowman_v1` (the songs begun compared each frame, a copy by BN5's number); `--render-song 0x62:2:PATH` plays Powie's. |
| Virus families moved into the battle engine: their arm points (`src/emu/xvirus.c` arms_set, `src/emu/xvirus_grow.c` XG_ARMS; issue #82, the chips track's find) | BN6: `0x08018810` an object's arm point (r0 its NameID, r1 the point, r2 its alliance, r3 its flip; `0x0800F26C` its struct1's list offset and number, `0x08018886` the record: the word at `0x080188A0` + the list offset, + 0x44 x the number), `BN6_ARM_VIRUSES_REF` `0x080188A4` (the viruses' records, list 1's numbers, at `0x0801898C`, the only word naming them), `BN6_ARM_VIRUSES_COUNT` 31 (the Navis' records after them, `0x080191C8`), `BN6_ARM_VIRUS_SIZE` `0x44` (34 points, x and y signed bytes); `0x0800E2CE` a point's x sign (its alliance XOR its flip); the status step (`0x0800E730`, every virus's): `BN6_STRUCK_CONFUSE` `0x80` and `BN6_COLL_CONFUSE` `+0x1E` (`0x0800E894`-`0x0800E8DE`: the other statuses' timers cleared, its mark spawned, its pointer at the collision data's `+0x48`), `BN6_STRUCK_BLIND` `0x20` and `BN6_COLL_BLIND` `+0x20` (`0x0800E908`-`0x0800E93E`, its mark's at `+0x4C`); the mark, the fourth kind's 6 (spawned by `0x080E1D2A`, r4 its kind: 0 confusion's, 1 blindness's), placed each frame at `0x080E1CCA`-`0x080E1CEA`: its owner's place, x plus point 5's x times the sign, z plus its y. BN5: `0x08015140` (its tables at `0x080151B8`), `BN5_ARM_VIRUSES_REF` `0x080151BC` (`0x080154D0`), `BN5_ARM_VIRUSES_COUNT` 31, `BN5_ARM_VIRUS_SIZE` `0x3C` (30 points) | BN6 finds a virus's arm points by the number its struct1 gives its battle sprite in list 1, and a family's number is past BN6's records (from `0x20`) or one of its placeholders (`0x00`, `0x1E`): without a record of its own a status's mark, read at point 5, stood where BN6's bytes there said (CanGard's 10 pixels to one side, Handi's and the others' on its feet). The viruses' records are grown to every number of list 1 (`XV_ARMS`, the families' art room's last `0x4400` bytes: BN6's bytes for each of its list's 32 numbers as it reads them, its 31 records and the Navis' first for the 32nd; record 0's past them), the word pointed at the copy, and each family's install writes its battle sprite's record: its BN5 record's 30 points (by BN5's list 1 number, its struct1's), then points 30-33 as BN6's first virus record has them (BN5 has none: the fourth kind's 0x3C reads 30, the third's 0x95 31, `0x08010820` 32, a freeze's ice block 33; the Navis' track gives its Navis MegaMan's). Verify: `python3 tools/xfamily.py --spec families`: each family confused and blinded (the status step's `confuse` and `blind`), its mark over it at BN5's point 5 every frame (CanGard (0, 48)); with the records left out CanGard's mark stands at (-10, 56), Handi's and TinHawk's at (0, 0). |
| The differential's view of a battle in both games (`src/dev/xdiff.c`, `tools/xdiff.py` and `tools/xdiff_snap.py`; issue #82, docs/DEVTOOLS.md, The differential) | BN6: RAM `0x020013F0` and `0x02001120` (`BN6_RNG_PRIMARY`, `BN6_RNG_SECONDARY`), `0x02036870` (`BN6_T4_OBJECTS`, `BN6_T4_SIZE` `0xC8`, `BN6_T4_COUNT` 32), `0x02009380` and `0x02009AB0` (`BN6_OBJECT_LIST`, `BN6_OBJECT_LIST_END`), an object's node `0x10` before it (`BN6_OBJECT_NODE`), its AI's data at +0x58 (`BN6_T1_AI_DATA`, `BN6_AI_DATA_SIZE` `0x100`), a collision data's `0xA8` bytes (`BN6_COLL_SIZE`), the fight's phase `0x0C` (`BN6_PHASE_FIGHT`) and the Custom window open at `0x78` (`BN6_WINDOW_OPEN`); ROM `0x08159DC8` (`BN6_MPLAYS`, `BN6_MPLAY_COUNT` 32), `0x08019C7C` (`BN6_COLL_MASKS`, `BN6_COLL_MASK_WORDS` 178). BN5: `0x02036F00` (`BN5_T4_OBJECTS`), `0x0200A410` and `0x0200ABA0` (`BN5_OBJECT_LIST`, `BN5_OBJECT_LIST_END`), `0xA0` (`BN5_COLL_SIZE`), ROM `0x08155A74` (`BN5_MPLAYS`), `0x08016364` (`BN5_COLL_MASKS`, `BN5_COLL_MASK_WORDS` 160); its dev battle's record at `BN5_FREE` + `0x200` (guest.c, `guest_dev_battle`) | The battle's objects come in kinds, each described by a 16-byte record the spawn's common routine reads (BN5 `0x08003098`, BN6 `0x08003278`, the same code): a bitmap of slots, the first slot's node, the end, the stride (+0xC), the bytes cleared (+0xD); BN5's records at `0x080030F0`, BN6's at `0x080032D0`, 16 bytes a kind. Kind 1 and 3 give the arrays bn5.h and bn6.h already name; kind 4 (the effects: a muzzle's blast, an ice block, a hit's spark) BN5's first node `0x02036EF0`, BN6's `0x02036860`, `0xC8` apart, 32 of them, the object `0x10` after its node (the spawn's `adds r5,#0x10`, BN5 `0x080030CA`, BN6 `0x080032AA`), its header's +3 its slot. The order the objects run in each frame is a list from a head whose +4 is the first node to an end node, each node's +4 the next: literals pair the two at BN5 `0x08002FC4`/`0x08002FC8` and BN6 `0x080031A4`/`0x080031A8`, and the list's insertion loads the end (BN5 `0x08003230`, BN6 `0x0800342C`). A collision data is `0xA0` bytes in BN5 and `0xA8` in BN6: their pool's start clears each so many (BN5 `0x08016112`, BN6 `0x0801987E`) and writes its +0x44, the stride after it (BN5 `0x0801611A`, BN6 `0x08019886`); their fields' places in each are tests/xdiff/legit.json's layout, each with the paired routines that show it. The words a collision data's +0x30 and +0x34 are set from (what it is and what it strikes) are a table by alliance and region, two words a region: the setup reads it through BN5 `0x08016BBC` / BN6 `0x0801A0BA` (alliance x 4 + region x 8, the table their one literal, at BN5 `0x08016C00` / BN6 `0x0801A0D0`, each the only word naming it in its ROM) and stores at +0x30 (BN5 `0x0801678A`, BN6 `0x08019FE8`; with bit 0x10000 ORed in while time is stopped, the test BN5 `0x08008A78` / BN6 `0x0800A098`, bit 2 of the battle's flags) and +0x34 (`0x080167A6`, `0x0801A004`); each table ends where the same routine's code begins (BN5 `0x080165E4`, BN6 `0x08019F44`: 160 words, 178). Of BN5's 160, BN6's twin is the same word for 30, the word with bit 0x80 for 128, and otherwise for region 61's two (BN5 `0x80000080`, `0x40000080`; BN6 `0x80`, `0x80`); BN6's second pass of a hit tests the bit (`0x081D7BAE`-`0x081D7BD0`), BN5's has no such test. `tools/xdiff_snap.py` reads both and stops where they are not so; it reads each BN5 word as the BN6 entry at its place (over the 160 entries each BN5 word gives one BN6 word). An AI's data is `0x100` bytes in both (AIData; its +0xA0 the attack's variables, `0x50`). The music players: a table of 12 bytes a player, which the routine that starts a song by its number loads beside the song table (its load at BN5 `0x0814D720`, BN6 `0x08150478`), each player's +0 its MusicPlayerInfo, whose +0 is the song's header and +0xC its clock, which the song's start sets to 0 (BN6 `0x08150C9E`-`0x08150CA6`). Verify: `python3 tools/xdiff.py cangard_v1 --story`: both traces hold MegaMan, CanGard and its reticle in their kinds' arrays (each object's +0x30 the same once BN5's word is read as the BN6 entry at its place in the tables: MegaMan's `0x08410000` as `0x08410080`, CanGard's `0x04210000` as `0x04210080`; with `--survey` no difference left at +0x30), the run order names MegaMan first and the reticle after CanGard, the sound 0xAE begins on player 4 in both as the reticle fires (t76), and both RNG words agree each frame. |
| The differential's hooks on both cores (`src/dev/xdiff_events.c`, issue #82; docs/DEVTOOLS.md, The differential) | BN6: `0x0800151C` GetRNG (`BN6_RNG_DRAW`), `0x08001532` GetPositiveSignedRNG (`BN6_RNG_DRAW_POSITIVE`), `0x0800154C` GetRNGSecondary (`BN6_RNG_DRAW_SECOND`), `0x08001562` GetPositiveSignedRNGSecondary (`BN6_RNG_DRAW_SECOND_POSITIVE`); `0x08003278` SpawnBattleObjectCommon (`BN6_SPAWN_COMMON`) and its end `0x080032C8` (`BN6_SPAWN_COMMON_END`); `0x080005CC` PlaySoundEffect (`BN6_PLAY_SOUND`); `0x080E1932` an effect's spawner (`BN6_SPAWN_EFFECT`); `0x08011698` setAttack's common part (`BN6_SET_ATTACK`); `0x0801BB3E` a panel reserved (`BN6_RESERVE_PANEL_DONE`), the object's bit `0x20` (`BN6_OBJ_RESERVED`); `0x08016954` the blink of an object flashing (`BN6_BLINK_FLASHING`), the collision data's flash timer +0x24 (`BN6_COLL_FLASH`); `0x080026E4` sprite_load (`BN6_SPRITE_LOAD`: r1 its list's offset, r2 the number, the sprite's bytes read through the list the core holds, BN6's grown, and logged by their CRC32: `src/dev/xdiff_sprite.c`); `0x08003200` the object runner's return (`BN6_OBJECT_RUNNER_RET`), RAM `0x0200AF70` the object running (`BN6_OBJECT_RUNNING`); the results' phase `0x10` (`BN6_PHASE_RESULTS`). BN5: `0x08001490`, `0x080014A6`, `0x080014C0`, `0x080014D6` (`BN5_RNG_DRAW`, `BN5_RNG_DRAW_POSITIVE`, `BN5_RNG_DRAW_SECOND`, `BN5_RNG_DRAW_SECOND_POSITIVE`), `0x08003098`, `0x080030E8` (`BN5_SPAWN_COMMON`, `BN5_SPAWN_COMMON_END`), `0x08000598` (`BN5_PLAY_SOUND`), `0x080DFFD2` (`BN5_SPAWN_EFFECT`), `0x0800F21C` (`BN5_SET_ATTACK`), `0x0801866E` (`BN5_RESERVE_PANEL_DONE`), `0x080137A8` and +0x26 (`BN5_BLINK_FLASHING`, `BN5_COLL_FLASH`), `0x08002650` (`BN5_SPRITE_LOAD`), `0x08003020` and RAM `0x0200BBF0` (`BN5_OBJECT_RUNNER_RET`, `BN5_OBJECT_RUNNING`) | Located through bn6f's map (`tools/bn6f_match.py`) and each read in both ROMs (capstone). The draws: each routine `push {r7,lr}`, its word loaded, stepped (`x rotl 1 + 1 ^ 0x873CA9E5`), stored, the positive ones clearing bit 31: BN6 has four that code calls (64, 260, 13 and 9 BLs) and a fifth on a third word (`0x0800157C`, `0x020018E8`) none calls; BN5 the same four (54, 380, 6, 10) and its fifth (`0x080014F0`, two). A hook at a routine's first instruction meets lr its caller. The spawns: SpawnBattleObjectCommon is the same code in both, r0 the kind (its 16-byte record, BN6 `0x080032D0`, BN5 `0x080030F0` on), r1 the five words its spawner stacked (the type first), r5 at its end the object or 0; each of its spawners (BN6's eight from `0x08003320`, 28 bytes apart, BN5's seven from `0x08003140`) is `push {r7,lr}; sub sp,#0x14; stm {r0-r4}; movs r0,#kind; mov r1,sp; bl`, so the spawner's caller is the word at r1 + 0x18; the spawn clears the record from +4 for the kind's +0xD bytes (`0x8C` for kinds 1 and 3, `0x7C` for 4), to where its sprite state begins (+0x90, +0x80): an object that sets up no sprite keeps the last occupant's. PlaySoundEffect (`push {r1-r7,lr}`, r0 the song: 1181 and 1104 BLs), the effect spawner (`push {r5,lr}; movs r0,#0; bl` the fourth kind's spawner: r4 the effect, stored at its +4), setAttack (setAttack0-5 BN6 `0x08011680` + 4N, BN5 0-6 `0x0800F200` + 4N, each `movs r2,#N; b` to the common part, which stores r0 at CurAction), sprite_load (r5 the object, its sprite state set up by sprite_initialize, BN6 `0x0800272C`, BN5 `0x08002698`). object_reservePanel (BN6 `0x0801BB1C`, BN5 `0x08018654`) marks the panel in both, and BN6's alone ORs `0x20` into the object's +0 (`0x0801BB36`) before returning 1: a scan of both ROMs for `ldrb; movs #0x20; orrs/bics` on an object's +0 finds that set and no clear (BN6 `0x0801BB78` reads the bit to let a reservation go as an object ends). The blink (BN6 `0x08016934`, bn6f blindVisualHandledHere; BN5 `0x08013788`): the visible bit set unless time stops, then for an object whose flags hold `0x202` its flash timer shifted right twice and the bit cleared past BN5's `bhs` (`0x080137AE`) or BN6's `blo` (`0x0801695A`): opposite phases of one timer. RunBattleObjectLogic (BN6 `0x080031AC`, BN5 `0x08002FCC`) stores each node before calling its object (BN6 `0x080031C2`, BN5 `0x08002FE2`), calls by `mov lr,pc; bx r0` and clears the word after its loop (`0x0800320A`, `0x0800302A`): a call chain read off the stack ends at that return. Verified: `python3 build.py xdiff`, every spec's events in both traces (each draw, spawn, sound, effect and setAttack of CanGard's and Handi's at the same call sites, its draws replayed: handi_v1 4033 of BN6's draws, none unmatched); `python3 tools/xcontrol.py`: the control's runs replay each of the native run's 8800 and 10783 draws and stay the same frame for frame. |
| MegaMan as the differential's subject (`src/dev/xdiff.c`, `src/dev/xdiff_events.c`, `tools/xdiff.py`; the Souls track's S0, issue #82; docs/DEVTOOLS.md, The differential) | BN6: the battle NaviStats `0x0203CE00`, `0x64` a side (`BN6_BATTLE_NAVI_SIZE`); the battle state's +0x0D, MegaMan's side (`BN6_BATTLE_SIDE`; in a link battle the cable's: 0 on the first GBA's, 1 on the second's, both GBAs numbering the sides alike, read on the Arena's two cores at the Custom screen, docs/ONLINE_ARENA.md, Full rules); a collision data's +0x3C, its object's flags (`BN6_COLL_FLAGS`), and their `0x08` (`BN6_OBJ_INVULNERABLE`). BN5: `0x0203B1D0` each side's pending Soul, 16 bytes a side (`BN5_SOUL_PENDING`); its NaviStats' +0x2E, the first folder's Regular chip (`BN5_NAVI_FOLDER1_REG`); `0x080127FA` a side's mood raised as a hit lands (`BN5_MOOD_GAIN`) | Read from each game's code (capstone). The battle NaviStats: BN6's getter `0x08013682` multiplies the side by `0x64` and adds `0x0203CE00` (BN5's `0x08010D18` by `0x60`, `BN5_BATTLE_NAVI_SIZE`), the two laid out alike to +0x5F (each byte the specs' traces show apart is masked in `tests/xdiff/legit.json` with both games' readers: BN6's Beast Out counter +0x21, BN5's +0x3A, BN5's dark meter +0x44, BN6's TagChips +0x56-0x59 and its +0x5A-0x5B). The side: `0x08026874` takes the battle state through the toolkit's +0x18 and hands its +0x0D to the battle NaviStats' byte getter `0x080136CC` as the side (BN5's `BN5_BATTLE_SIDE` at the same place). The flags: `0x0801A152` ORs r0 into an object's collision data's +0x3C (BN5's `0x08016C42`, the same bytes); `0x0800EAFA` gives an object frames that no hit takes, their count at +0x28 and `0x08` ORed in through it, and `0x0800EB08` takes both away. BN5's pending Soul: `0x080123FA` returns side r0's record at `0x0203B1D0` + 16 x side (+0 the Soul, 0xFF none; +1 Chaos Unison; +3 its turns; +6 ColonelSoul's chip), as BN6's `0x0801595E` returns a Cross's at `0x0203A980` (`BN6_CROSS_PENDING`); the Custom screen's record is `0x0203C940`, written at OK by `0x080123E8` (BN6's `0x08015952`, `BN6_CROSS_RECORD`). The Regular chip: BN5's deck reads the folder MegaMan fights with (+0x2D) and the Regular chip at +0x2E plus its number (`0x08008D58`-`0x08008D64`), as BN6 reads its own +0x2E (`BN6_NAVI_FOLDER1_REG`); BN5 has no TagChips. The mood gain: BN5's hit pass calls `0x080127FA` for the side that landed a hit that is no counter (`0x08017018`, the struck object's side XOR 1; a counter gives Full Synchro through `0x080127CE` at `0x0801700C` instead), which adds r1 to that side's battle mood (+0x0E) unless it is 0 (dark) or 0xFF (Full Synchro), 0xFE at most; BN6's twin of the pass (`0x0801A228`-`0x0801A256`) keeps the counter's Full Synchro (`0x0801A246`) and the hit's loss (`0x0801A252`) and calls no gain, so the differential hooks BN5's and reads BN5's mood less what its gains added (a translation, `tools/xdiff_events.py`). BN5's plain charged shot (its family 0x1E, `0x080ECAB8`) fires at once where the battle's bit 0x40 is clear (`0x080ECAE0`, through `0x080090E8` and `0x0802D510`), where BN6's (0x16, `0x080ED140`) always waits 5 frames first (`0x080ED168`): no spec fires one. Verified: with `safe=1` neither game's MegaMan loses HP to CanGard V1 firing along his row, and without it each loses 10 at t85, t279, t473 and t667 (700 frames of each game alone, `game=bn5` and `game=bn6`); `python3 tools/xdiff.py megaman_plain`: PASS, every byte of his player block the same in both games every frame (BN5's mood translated); `python3 tools/xdiff.py soul_knight_ref`: KnightSoul's unison read in BN5 from these places (the pending Soul taken as the turn starts, form 11, the used bit 0x800, its turns 3 down to 0 as the fourth turn starts). |

his NameID `0x1AB` + the form (`BN6_FORM_NAME_BASE`) and its entry of the object ids at `0x080182C4` (`BN6_OBJECT_IDS`, RomLayout's `enemy_ids`; where BN5's ROM grew the table, the engine reads its copy past `EMU_FREE`, `BN6_ENEMY_IDS_REF`'s word, and the proxy's entry is written there); They part at the tables, each indexed through a pointer word beside its reader (`0x0800FC9E`, `0x08011268`, `0x08011384`, `0x0801086C` for both element tables, `0x08014536`, `0x0801469C`, `0x0800FEEC`, `0x0801002C` and four more readers of the palettes, `0x0801265A`, `0x0801E660` and `0x0801E6A8`); at three compares: `0x0800EF62` (HeatCross's +50 to Fire chips, form 1 or its Beast Out 0xD, in the bonuses by form `0x0800EF34`), `0x0800EFE6` (TenguCross's Wind bonus, form 8 or its Beast Out 0x14: Gregar does compare the form with 8, though no CROSSSELECT of Gregar's reaches it) and `0x080EC8DE` (his arm's flash, which the routine `0x080EC8B2` gives the form as its animation); at one call (`0x08014BB6`: the summon `0x080BE0B4` in phase 1 of the crossing, `0x08014B98`, draws the Navi of the form's number); at the NameID's write (`0x08015B22`: `0x1A0` in no form, else the form + `0x1AB`); and at copies of the record (the turn's link packets and both sides' pending forms: `0x0800B3A2`, `0x0801FED2`, `0x0800840C`, `0x080147E4`; a battle against the game's own viruses passes the record to both sides). Entry 8 of each table is Falzar's TenguCross whole (its overlay, element Wind, weakness, Airshoe, loadout and charged shot, palette and face; its sprite MegaMan's own, as every Cross's). CROSSSELECT never lists it: `0x08029EF6` lists Cross i of Gregar's five (flags `0xE2`-`0xE6`, the table at `0x08029F48`) whose flag is set, whose bit in `0x020349A0` is clear and that is not the form he began the battle in (his NaviStats +0x17). Its commit `0x0802937A` puts 1 (0xD from a Beast Out) plus the chosen Cross's index (+0x1A) in r0, `movs r1,#0` at `0x08029394`, writes the record by `0x08015952` (+0 the form, +1, +3), then ORs the index's bit into `0x020349A0`, which the Custom screen's set-up zeroes with the 16 bytes after it (`0x0802684C`) while the battle state's +7 reads 0. The object ids' entry of the proxy's NameID (0x1B3) is read through the triple's reader `0x080182B4` (15 callers, among them his object's set-up `0x080075A4`, version, kind and AI index into his AI data; struct1 and struct2 by AI index, `0x0800F214` and `0x0800F23C`; an overlay by AI index, `0x08010DDA`), and the tested battle makes none of those reads for him in a Cross: leaving the entry TenguCross's changes no byte of it. His object is set up as a battle begins, and every battle of a run begins in no form (one ended in HeatCross, the next began in form 0), with the NameID 0x1A0. Verified: `python3 tools/xproxy.py`: four battles of the host build from one boot with the same keys (HeatCross chosen on CROSSSELECT; a charged shot; FireBrn1; a buster shot from the row above; a step into a StarFish's row, whose bubble breaks the Cross; the buster; the results), every byte of EWRAM and IWRAM after each frame: with `--dev soulproxy` and HeatCross chosen, the same as without it (0 differences); committed as form 8 (`cross 8`), the same frame for frame but for the form's copies (1 against 8) and his NameID (0x1AC against 0x1B3); Gregar's own form 8 (no proxy) differs. `--mutations`: each part of the install left out parts the proxy from HeatCross (the tables, the chip bonus, the arm flash, the summon), but the object id's, which the battle does not reach. |

The power attacks are the Souls' grown power table's entries after the empty ones (BN6's 0x9F-0xA4), their routines Chaos Unison's Thumb (`SOULS_CHAOS`), each the chip writer with the registry's id of its DarkChip and BN6's chip use; the failure is family 0xC7 (`BN6_CHAOS_FAMILY`), BN5's phases in BN6's calls, the Dark Soul (the moved Navi, `xnavi_bn6_id`) spawned on the other side, its chip lists, where the battle gave none, MegaMan's folder's chips the most of a chip first (BN5 keeps the chips he uses most, BN6 no record of them), and BN6's steps of a form's end (`0x08013775`, `0x08011385`, `0x0800FC9F`, `0x080158CD`, `0x08013755`, `0x08015B23`, `0x080144C1`, `0x080143B5`, `0x08011269`, `0x08011715`). The glow is BN5's dark glow, the Souls' copy in list 0 (BN5's 5:0x1C another picture than BN6's). Verify: `python3 tools/xdiff.py chaos_*` (each Soul's success at 45 and failure at 60; NumberSoul's holds: 45, 90, 180, 210 succeed, 60, 75, 105, 120, 150 fail), `--mutations` (the phases a frame longer, the Dark Soul a frame later). ShadowSoul's chaos attack is BN6's own DarkInvs (`chaos_shadow_45`'s recorded verdict: BN5's puts MegaMan under the chip AI from his idle states, `0x080F04C2`, which BN6's twin `0x080F1866` has no path to). |

| Dev tools (`src/dev/devtools.c`, docs/DEVTOOLS.md) | `0x08005A8C` checkThenStartBattle, event flags `0x1700` and `0x173E`, `0x0203A9B0` T1 battle objects, `0x020047CC` Navi stats, `0x02001B80`+0x5C zenny | checkThenStartBattle returns before the random-battle roll while flag `0x1700` is set (a BBS request sets it; the game clears it on entering a map). Flag `0x173E`, tested next, is the SlipRun state: the player's update clears it every frame (bn6f `ow_player.s`), so setting it does nothing. T1 objects are 0xD8 bytes, 0x20 slots, bit 0 of +0 active, +0x16 alliance (0 MegaMan's side), +0x24 HP, +0x26 max HP; HP 0 deletes the object once the turn runs. Navi stats +0x40 HP, +0x42 max HP. Zenny is GameState +0x5C; the text command `EF 0E` (ts_check_give_zenny) with a u32 amount adds to it. Tested headlessly. |

The sprites drawn with `--sheet` (Bass cloaked, facing right through down-left, 24 a pose, 25 the cloak off, 26 throwing it open; the beast crouching 25, 27, 28, rearing 26, 29; the stone standing, 2 broken, 3 shaking); the sounds rendered with `--render-song` (`0xE3` a rumble of 0.8 s, `0xFE` a burst, `0x100` an impact, `0xD7` a fading tone), as bn6f's scenes before the records play them. Measured, 19 October 2026, `--dev arena` with a trace of both sides' fields each frame (kept out of the build): the side that picked MiniBomb on the Custom screen read +0x2A 0x0036 from the battle's start, then +0x09 turned 0x08 to 0x12 and +0x2A read 0xFFFF on the same frame (the chip used, none left in the hand); five charged shots each turned +0x09 0x08 to 0x16 with +0x2A 0xFFFF throughout; the other side's +0x24 fell 100 to 0 as the battle was won. So a chip is counted as used where the action turns to 0x12-0x16 and the chip it uses next gives way that frame, which a charged shot never does. Assumed: the other chip actions (0x13-0x15) and a hand of several chips behave alike (the next chip in the hand taking the field); seen in the three-game runs only with one chip a hand. |

Verify: `--dev arena,arenaboth,arena2v2=1,teamcross=1,teamhp=500,arenadelay=1,quitend` (a fresh data folder): each partner takes HeatCross on his own CROSSSELECT at his first turn, the first turn's crossing (BN6's black field) shows both partners turning into HeatCross while their leads stay as they are, on both GBAs (`teamseat=2` and `3`), and the log's "the partners' Crosses: ... asked to cross ..." with both GBAs the same at every iteration; `teamcross=-1` parts them from the first iteration. Beast Out (`--dev arena2v2,teambeast=1`): `0x08029344` (`BN6_BEAST_COMMIT`, the emblem's pick committed at OK) writes its form into the same record by `0x08015952` (`ldr r4` 0x17 BeastOver, 0xB, or 0xD plus a Cross's, `0x08029356` on; `0x0802936E` the call), so a partner's Beast Out crosses as his Cross does: both partners turn into Gregar's beast at the first turn's crossing on both GBAs (`teamseat=2`, `3`), `teambeast=-1` parts them from the first iteration. What BN6 runs for each side's player alone, read with capstone, and run for his object too by BN6's own code: `0x08015A16` (`BN6_FORM_TURN_DOWN`, r0 an object: his AI data's +0x0F counted down; its body from `0x08015A1C`, `BN6_FORM_TURN_DOWN_BODY`, its `pop {r5,pc}` `0x08015A36`, `BN6_FORM_TURN_DOWN_END`) called at `0x08008472` and `0x0800847C` on the side-player getter `0x080103BC`'s object (`0x0800846E`, `0x08008478`) as each Custom screen opens; `0x08015A38` (`BN6_BEAST_TURN`: his BeastOutCounter less one in a Beast Out form; its body from `0x08015A40`, `BN6_BEAST_TURN_BODY`, its `pop {r5,r7,pc}` `0x08015A7E`, `BN6_BEAST_TURN_END`) called for each side's player as the crossing controller is done (`0x0800840C`); `0x080159C6` (`BN6_FORM_END_CHECK`) and `0x08015994` (`BN6_FORM_LEAVE_ASK`: `BN6_AI_FLAGS` | 0x40, `BN6_AI_LEAVE_ASK`) in the turn's form check for the crossing controller's records' objects; `0x08014A00` (`BN6_CROSS_RESTORE`, mode 8, reached by `mov lr,pc; bx r1`) waiting while a record's object leaves his form (`0x080159A2`: `BN6_AI_MODES` 0x100, `BN6_AI_LEAVING`, or `BN6_AI_LEAVE_ASK`), and taking the records' forms before. `0x0802D6A0` sets up the controller `0x0203C970` (`BN6_TURN_SIDES`, +8 and +0xC each side's player) as the screen opens, whose `0x0802DD1E` answers 0 in Gregar (`movs r7,#0 ... adds r0,r7,#0`), so its `0x0802DD10` never runs: nothing for a partner there. Verify with `teambeast=3` (the leads take Beast Out by the same keys, the partners' control): at each turn's form check each lead and his partner in the same form with the same BeastOutCounter (the log's "the turn's check at iteration ..." lines). |
| A Program Advance on the Custom screen (`--dev arena2v2,teampa=1`, `src/online/arena_team_screen.c`, `arena_team_custom.c`, `src/emu/bn6_link.h`, `src/emu/bn6_chips.h`; epic #167, issue #181) | RAM: the Custom screen's +0x0B (`BN6_CUSTOM_NEXT`, `0x020364CB`): the mode OK goes on to, `BN6_MODE_WAIT` (0x14, written at `0x08029112`) or `BN6_MODE_PA` (0x10, `0x080291DE`) where OK's check (`0x08029520`, called at `0x080291B8`) made a Program Advance; `0x020366C0` (`BN6_CUSTOM_BUILT`, bn6f byte_20366C0): the hand OK builds, a hand's layout, which a link battle's packet carries (`BN6_LINK_SEND`). Chips: `BN6_CHIP_CANNON` (0x01), `BN6_PA_ZCANNON1` (0x140); the Program Advances' ids `BN6_PA_FIRST` 0x140 to `BN6_PA_LAST` 0x15D. | Gregar's OK routine `0x08029110` read with capstone against bn6f's sub_8029110 (`movs r0,#0x14; strb r0,[r5,#0xb]` first; the check, `0x08029328`, `0x080292CC`, `0x0802B6F2` and `movs r0,#0x10; strb r0,[r5,#0xb]` after it; the merge `0x08029224` last). The check's two tables through the words at `0x080295B4` (`0x0802BCB0`) and `0x080295B8` (`0x0802BC60`): 30 records read from Gregar, each +0 its count (3 or 4), +1 its kind (0: one chip in a row of codes; 4: chips of one code), +2 u16 its id (0x140-0x15D), then its chips; `0x0802BAE4` is Z-Canon1's (3, kind 0, 0x140, chip 1). Measured on a partner's screen copy: Cannon A, B and C picked in order, OK leaves the hand at `0x020366C0` as chips 0x140, 0xFFFF, 0x0001, powers 300, 0, 40, entries 0x0001, 0x0201, 0x0401 (the Program Advance in the first place, the end of the hand after it) and +0x0B 0x10, the screen then waiting in mode 0x14 with that hand. Verify: `--dev arena,arenaboth,arena2v2=1,teampa=1,teamhp=500,arenadelay=1,quitend`: the log's "a partner's Program Advance 140 from his picks 0 to 2" and each GBA's "side 0's partner's hand a Program Advance, 140 (power 300)", and his Z-Canon1 taking 300 HP from side 1's partner. |
| A side's MegaMan as an AI asks for him (`src/emu/bn6_ai.h`; epic #167's full-rules audit) | `0x080103BC` (`BN6_SIDE_PLAYER_GET`: r0 a side, its player's object back, eBattleState +0xD0's first word of the side where its name's class, `0x0800F29C`, is 2, else 0; its partner, the side's second word, never); BN5's twin `0x0800E140` (`tools/bn5_twins.py`'s pair, SAME) | Read in Gregar with capstone (the loop re-reads the side's first word four times: `adds r5,#3` where r6 was meant). Its callers by a scan of every `bl`: 149 in Gregar, 163 in BN5 (BN5's moved code reaches BN6's through the recipes' call pairs). In the moved code: BN5's ShadowMan's, NumberMan's and TomahawkMan's AIs (`src/emu/xnavi_bn5.c`'s calls) ask it for the MegaMan they aim at, as enemies in a run's battles; the five chips the audit named (ZSaber, Katana, MoonBld, CustSwrd, DarkCirc) only carry a copy of BN5's `0x080125EE` (a side's player's AIData +0x12, the chaos flag) in the block `0x08012530`-`0x08012704` their recipes copy, which none of their code calls (BN5's one caller, `0x08019704`, lies outside every unit). Seen: 0 asks from moved code over 2v2 NetBattles with thirty Katana, MoonBld, CustSwrd or ShadoMan in side 0's folder (a hook of the routine counting them, kept out of the build), so nothing a NetBattle runs aims through it at a lead alone. |
| A link battle's packet, and a partner's words in it (`src/online/arena_team_link.c`, `src/emu/bn6_link.h`; epic #167, issue #181) | RAM: `0x02036780` this GBA's packet (`BN6_LINK_OUT`, bn6f eStruct2036780, eight u16 a frame): +0 the pad's high byte (the 0xFC mark) and +1 its sequence, written by the link's frame (`0x0803EE6C`, bn6f sub_803EE98: `ldrh` the pad, `lsr #8`, `strb` +0; the count at eStruct200BC30 +7, `strb` +1), +2 the pad held (`BN6_LINK_PAD`, its mark `BN6_KEYS_VALID` set), +4 the word stream's index (0xFF none) and +8 its word, +6 BattleState +0x11 (the battle's ready flags), +0xC eLinkState's (`0x0803F714`), +0xE and +0xF the library's status (`0x0803EE6C` again); `0x020399F0` both sides' packets of the exchange taken this frame (`BN6_LINK_IN`, `BN6_LINK_PACKET` 0x10 apart, side 0's first on every GBA). +5 and +7 (`BN6_LINK_SPARE_LO`, `_HI`) nothing of BN6's writes or reads in a battle as far as read. Others that touch the packets (issue #181's review): `0x0813F8D4` writes OUT +0 and +0xA (`strh`, the pad); the library reaches OUT and IN through its getters `0x0803EF50` (OUT: the u32 at `0x02009A04`) and `0x0803EF48` (IN: `0x02009A08`), writes OUT +0 as a halfword through the first (`0x0803EC18`, `0x0803ECDA`; and +0, +1, +0xE, +0xF at `0x0803EF06`, `0x0803EF0E`, `0x0803ECAE`, `0x0803ECC0`, `0x0803EEF0`, `0x0803EEF6`), and can point OUT elsewhere with its setter `0x0803EF58` (`0x0200B1B0`, `0x0803EB9C`); some 60 calls between 0x0812C000 and 0x08134000 read IN through `0x0803EF48`; none of them reaches +5 or +7 (`0x0813F8D4` read here in Thumb, the rest the review's). Code hooked: `0x0801FF8C` (`BN6_LINK_PAD_STORE`: `strh r1,[r5,#2]`, sub_801FF18's store of the frame's pad into the packet, r5 `0x02036780`, both received packets read before it at `0x0801FF2A`-`0x0801FF82`), `0x0802001C` (`BN6_LINK_READ_B`: `adds r5,#0x10` in sub_801FFD6's read path, side 0's packet read and side 1's next; its argument's bit 3 skips the path) and `0x08020062` (`BN6_LINK_PAD_STORE_B`: the store in sub_801FFD6, the other routine of the battle's link table at `0x0801FF08`, its argument at [sp,#4]; bit 4 skips the store, `0x08020052`, the library setting it at `0x0803EC28` where its send queue cannot take the packet). RAM `0x0203F7D8` (`BN6_LINK_TAKES_ANY`, bn6f eStruct203F7D8 +0): 0 where side 1's packet is taken whatever its pad's mark (`0x0801FF56`, `0x08020020`), set to 1 as the link starts (`0x0801FE5A`) | Read in bn6f: every writer of eStruct2036780 (sub_801FE00, sub_801FE6C, sub_801FF18, sub_801FFD6, sub_803EE98: their stores at +0, +1, +2, +4, +6, +8, +0xC, +0xE, +0xF, the +4 and +6 ones `strb`) and every reader of `0x020399F0`-`0x02039A0F` (sub_801FE6C, sub_801FF18, sub_801FFD6: `ldrsb` +4, `ldrb` +6, `ldrh` +2 and +0xC, `ldr` +8; sub_803E828 and sub_803E850 +0xE and +0xF), matched to Gregar's bytes at the same addresses (`thumbdis.py`: `0x0801FF8C` `strh r1, [r5, #2]` after `ldr r5, =0x02036780`, `0x08020062` the same); the library carries all eight halfwords (the link study, .build/research/battles/linkdelay.md). Verified on the running ROM: a partner's message written at +5/+7 of one GBA's packet read back out of both GBAs' `0x020399F0` four frames later with the same sequence (a trace, `AF` on both), and whole battles of two MegaMen a side with every partner's word taken so, the two GBAs the same at every iteration (docs/ONLINE_ARENA.md, The partners' words in BN6's packet); a 1v1 NetBattle never writes them. |
| Expression: what BN6 has to show a character's mood (`src/emu/bn6_expression.h`; for the Square's peers, docs/ONLINE.md) | `0x0809FE34` the NPC hop's routine (`BN6_NPCSCRIPT_HOP_CODE`); `0x08041CC4` text command F5's (`BN6_TEXT_MUGSHOT_CODE`), its palette banks' pairs `0x08044214` (`BN6_MUGSHOT_BANKS`) and its animations' `0x08044230` (`BN6_MUGSHOT_ANIMS`); `0x080E93BC` the battle's burst object (`BN6_FX_BURST_CODE`), its spawner `0x080E9464` (`BN6_FX_BURST_SPAWN`); `0x02011EE0` the overworld's map objects (`BN6_OW_OBJECTS`), their bits `0x02011E50` (`BN6_OW_OBJECTS_ACTIVE`); `0x080047E0` their spawn (`BN6_OW_OBJECT_SPAWN`); `0x080A4F24` their records (`BN6_OW_OBJECT_RECORDS`) | BN6 has no emotion bubbles of its own: frame 0 of every sprite of lists 4-9 (`--sheet @CAT:FIRST:COUNT:PATH`) and the first frame of every animation whose frame is icon-sized (2 objects or fewer, 256 bytes of tiles or fewer: 452 animations of lists 0-5, 7 and 9) drawn from Gregar and looked over show no "?", note, sweat, anger mark or sleep mark, and one heart, Roll's (list 3's `0x50` (`BN6_BATTLE_ROLL`), LZ77, 7864 bytes, her animation 4); BN6's font has "!" (0xA2), "?" (0x9D), "~" (0xAB) and a small z (0xB3). A mood is shown by the chat box, MegaMan's poses, the hop and sounds. **MegaMan's poses**: list 6's `0x37` (`BN6_OW_MEGAMAN`, plain) has 0x49 animations (`BN6_OW_MEGAMAN_ANIMS`), their frames and delays read from Gregar's sprite: 0-7 standing, 8-0xF walking, 0x10-0x17 running; 0x18 (`BN6_OW_ANIM_GOT`) a hand raised, 3 frames, 35 long: Gregar's text has F8 01 18 41 times, 20 of them followed by FD 00 73 00 (the item-get jingle, `BN6_SOUND_GOT`), and F8 05 18 62 times; 0x19 (`BN6_OW_ANIM_LOG_IN`) and 0x21 (`BN6_OW_ANIM_LOG_OUT`) + facing the log-in and log-out (the Guardians row); 0x2D (`BN6_OW_ANIM_ARRIVE`) blank then turning round, 10 frames, 50 long: Gregar's cutscene bytes 47 00 FF 2D (bn6f cs_write_ow_player_fixed_anim_select) twice, once followed by 4D 76 00 (cs_play_sound 0x76, `BN6_SOUND_LOG_OUT`); 0x33-0x36 the buster held out four ways (`BN6_OW_ANIM_AIM`), 0x38 sparks beside him (`BN6_OW_ANIM_SHOCKED`), 0x39 hunched (`BN6_OW_ANIM_CROUCH`), 0x3F standing to kneeling to down (`BN6_OW_ANIM_COLLAPSE`), 0x40 both arms raised, 4 frames looping (`BN6_OW_ANIM_ARMS_UP`), 0x42 panting on one knee (`BN6_OW_ANIM_PANTING`), 0x43 mid-leap (`BN6_OW_ANIM_LEAP`), read from their frames drawn (`--sheet 6:0x37:ANIM:PATH`); where BN6's own scripts play 0x33-0x48 was not looked up. **The hop**: NPC command 0x11 (`BN6_NPCSCRIPT_HOP`), its rise and fall after it (`BN6_NPCSCRIPT_HOP_RISE`, `BN6_NPCSCRIPT_HOP_FALL`); BN6's bystanders hop as 22 11 60 DC 21 (the shadow left on the floor 0x22, `BN6_NPCSCRIPT_SHADOW_OFF`, the hop, the shadow back 0x21, `BN6_NPCSCRIPT_SHADOW_ON`): 157 times in Gregar, and 11 9E DC 19 times. **The chat box's face**: text command 0xF5 (`BN6_TEXT_MUGSHOT`; bn6f chatbox_F5_mugshot, Gregar `0x08041CC4`, its pool holding 0x08044214, 0x08044230 and 0x18C6): F5 00 face, F5 01 hides it, F5 02 its palette bank, F5 03 its animation, F5 04 the OAM palette. A face's animation is a sub-animation of its one frame (list 8): Gregar's faces have three, 0 still (`BN6_MUGSHOT_STILL`), 1 blinking with the mouth shut (`BN6_MUGSHOT_BLINK`; its object lists 0, 1 and 2: eyes open, half, shut), 2 the mouth moving (`BN6_MUGSHOT_TALK`; lists 3 and 4), and Lan's (0x00) a fourth, 3, eyes shut (`BN6_MUGSHOT_EYES_SHUT`); bn6f chatbox_8040C44 (Gregar 0x08040C18) plays the set one while a letter prints and 1 for 2 between words, leaving 3 alone. Gregar's text has F5 03 n 31 times (1 13, 2 12, 0 4, 3 2). No character has a second face for a mood (MegaMan 0x37 one, Beast MegaMan 0x58 its own); palette banks: MegaMan's 1, most townsfolk's 2, Mr.Prog's (0x3C) 21, the Normal Navi's (0x3E) 4. F8 01 n sets the player's animation, F8 03 waits for its end, F8 04 puts back the one before (`BN6_TEXT_PLAYER`); FD 00 and a u16 plays a sound (`BN6_TEXT_SOUND`). **The "!!" burst**: list 5's 0x07 (`BN6_FX_BURST`, plain), 4 animations of 9 frames (18 long): 0 and 2 "!!", 1 a bat, 3 a skull; Gregar's 0x080E93DC loads it (80 20 14 21 07 22: list 5 x 4, sprite 7) for the battle's type-4 object 0x6B (`BN6_FX_BURST_OBJECT`, bn6f t4_0x6b_80E807C), Param1 its animation, spawned by 0x080E9464 from 0x0801A42E with 0 where CollisionData +0x74 (bn6f ExclamationIndicator, a counter hit) and +0x80 (FinalDamage) are set, and from 0x0801A4A6 with 3 where +0xA4 (InflictedBugs) is 0xF4 or 0xF7. The Square's and Lan's HP's "!" is its animation 0. List 5's 0x05 (`BN6_FX_SPARKLE`: 14 animations, 0, 8 and 9 small star bursts, 7 "TRAP!") and 0x15 (`BN6_FX_GREEN_SPARKLE`: green sparkles) drawn. **Map objects**: 0x38 (`BN6_OW_OBJECT_SLOTS`) of 0x78 bytes (`BN6_OW_OBJECT_BYTES`; bn6f NUM_OVERWORLD_MAP_OBJECTS, OverworldMapObject.inc's Size) at 0x02011EE0, their bits at 0x02011E50, the literals at 0x080049D0 and 0x080049D4 of the spawn's pool; handler 0's records 0xF4 ids (`BN6_OW_OBJECT_IDS`): 0x14 (`BN6_OW_OBJECT_TRANSMIT`) and 0xB2 draw list 5's 0x03 animations 1 and 2 (bn6f's cutscenes spawn 0x14, then 0xB2, at an NPC: cutscene command 0x49 0x2X), and 0x1F (`BN6_OW_OBJECT_UNUSED`), 0x20, 0xB4, 0xB5 and 0xBF are all 0xFF. **Sounds**: FD 00 id in Gregar's text (its plain archives only; most is LZ77): 0x73 (`BN6_SOUND_GOT`) 30 times, 0x74 (`BN6_SOUND_RANK_UP`) 10, 0x8F (`BN6_SOUND_LINK_NAVI`) 2, 0xCD (`BN6_SOUND_BEEP`) 6, 0x14B (`BN6_SOUND_FALL`) 2, 0x14F (`BN6_SOUND_CHIME`) 2, 0x174 (`BN6_SOUND_BARK`) 1, 0x175 (`BN6_SOUND_WOOF`) 2, 0x177 (`BN6_SOUND_DOORBELL`) 3, 0x182 (`BN6_SOUND_INSTALLED`) 5, 0x18F (`BN6_SOUND_PHONE`) 1, 0x19A (`BN6_SOUND_POPS`) 1; the words beside them read in bn6f's text (Falzar's) and, decoded, in Gregar's for 0x73, 0x74, 0x8F, 0xCD, 0x14B, 0x174, 0x175 and 0x19A ; 0x76 and 0x94 (`BN6_SOUND_APPEAR`) as the Guardians row has them. Found by reading bn6f's npc_script.inc, cutscene_script.inc, text_script_commands.inc, chatbox.s and the scripts and tables they name, then each byte pattern, table and routine checked in Gregar (`tools/bn6f_match.py`'s map for the routines). Verify: `--sheet 6:0x37:0x40:PATH` draws MegaMan with both arms raised, 4 frames; `--sheet 5:7:0:PATH` the "!!" burst; Gregar's bytes at 0x080E93DC as above and at 0x08044230 FF FF 00 00. Issue #176 names the poses and sounds the Net Square's emotes play (docs/ONLINE.md, Emotes): `BN6_OW_ANIM_STARTLED` 0x2E (+ 0-3, a step back, four ways, 1 frame), `BN6_OW_ANIM_KNEEL` 0x41 (on one knee, 1 frame) and `BN6_OW_ANIM_PONDER` 0x44 (crouched, looking about, 3 frames), read off the research's contact sheets of list 6's 0x37 and seen played on a peer's puppet and, by text command F8 01, on MegaMan himself in the Square's captures (four-way animations' + 0-3 = his facing / 2: facing 5's 0x35 seen pointing -x, the others assumed in order; `BN6_TALK_PROBES` read from Gregar: facing 1 is +x (8, 0), 3 +y, 5 -x, 7 -y); some frames of 0x39, 0x40 and 0x42 are drawn in the log-in's light palette (seen on the player and a puppet alike). The sounds `BN6_SOUND_TYPE` 0x65, `BN6_SOUND_HIT` 0x6B, `BN6_SOUND_BUSTER_CHARGE` 0x71, `BN6_SOUND_LOG_IN` 0x77 and `BN6_SOUND_CURSOR` 0x7F are bn6f's SoundOffsets names (verify: `--render-song ID:2:PATH` and a listen); `BN6_SOUND_FADE` 0x8E's use was not looked up. F8 01 in a script that prints no text (no box drawn) plays the pose and F8 04 puts back the animation the last F8 01 replaced (each F8 01 keeps its own: after a turn of eight F8 01s, F8 04 left MegaMan facing the seventh step's way), verified in the captures (Beta's own screen). |
| Expression: Battle Network 5 Team Colonel (USA) (`src/emu/bn5_expression.h`) | BN5 `0x03272C` sprite lists | List 6's 0x30 (`BN5_OW_MEGAMAN`, plain) is MegaMan, 0x59 animations (`BN5_OW_MEGAMAN_ANIMS`): 0x00-0x2D timed as BN6's 0x37 (frames and delays read from both ROMs), 0x18 (`BN5_OW_ANIM_GOT`) the item-get pose and 0x2D (`BN5_OW_ANIM_ARRIVE`) the arrival drawn alike; past 0x2D its own (the buster aimed four ways and fired, falls, getting up), no arms-up pose (its 0x40 a frame standing). List 4's 0x26 (`BN5_FX_HEART`) a pink heart, 1 frame, and 0x44 (`BN5_FX_NOTE`) a music note, 4 frames looping (12 long), both plain; where BN5 plays them was not looked up (assumed battle effects). Found by the same icon-sized scan as BN6's (446 animations of BN5's lists 4, 5 and 7), the frames drawn from BN5's ROM as `src/gfx/gfx.c` reads a sprite. Verify: BN5's list 6 entry 0x30 points at `0x083F4CE0`, list 4's 0x26 at `0x08359D30` and 0x44 at `0x0836C4DC`; their animation tables give 0x59, 1 and 1 animations. |
